“I shan’t frighten him,” Flamborough assured him, as they approached Markfield’s room.
===Chinese===
“我会拿这项分析的交接安排做由头。倒不是说明马克菲尔德的结果到手后，我会多看重，先生。他先前给我们的那番证词，在我心里落了个坏印象。人命案里，不该只为袒护朋友就含糊其词。没有这种事，我们的麻烦也够多了。”

“那么，对付他要讲点手法，探长，不然他也许要犯倔。他若拿‘我不记得了’之类做挡箭牌，您就从他嘴里问不出什么了。”克林顿爵士评道。

“我不会吓着他，”两人走近马克菲尔德的房间时，弗拉姆伯勒向他保证道。

===Original===
As they entered, Markfield looked up in surprise at seeing them once more.

“It’s just occurred to me that I forgot to make arrangements about handing that stuff over to you for analysis,” Flamborough said, as he went forward. “It’ll be in sealed jars, of course; and I’d prefer to hand it over to you personally. I suppose I could always get hold of you either here or at your house?”
===Chinese===
两人进门时，马克菲尔德抬起头，惊讶地发现他们竟又来了。

“我恰好想起，忘了安排把那些东西移交给你化验的事，”弗拉姆伯勒斯说着迎上前去。“自然是装在密封的罐子里；我想亲手交到你本人手上。我想，无论在这里还是在你府上，总归找得着你吧？”

===Original===
“You’d better come here. My housekeeper’s away just now nursing some relation who’s down with flu, and my house is empty except when I happen to be at home myself. You’ll find me here between nine in the morning and six at night—except for lunchtime, of course. I generally clear out of here at six and dine down town.”

“I suppose you have a long enough day of it,” the Inspector said in a casual tone. “You don’t come back here and work in the evening?”
===Chinese===
“你还是到这儿来吧。我的管家这会儿外出照护一位染了流感的亲戚去了，除非我碰巧在家，那宅子是空的。早上九点到晚上六点，你在这儿准能找到我——当然，午饭时间除外。我一般六点离开，进城吃晚饭。”

“我看你这一天也够长的了，”探长漫不经心地说。“晚上你不回这儿工作？”

===Original===
“Sometimes, if there’s something interesting that brings me back. But I haven’t done that for weeks past.”

“This place is shut up at night, isn’t it? I mean, you don’t keep a porter or a watchman on the premises?”

“No. But each of the seniors has a private key, of course. I can get in any time I wish. It’s the same at the Research Station.”
===Chinese===
“有时会，若有什么有趣的东西引我回来。不过近几个礼拜都没这么干了。”

“这地方夜里锁门闭户吧？我是说，所里不雇门房或看守值夜？”

“不雇。不过当然，每位资深人员都有一把私用钥匙。我几时想进来都成。农业研究站那边也一样。”

===Original===
The Inspector seemed to be struck by an idea.

“Any valuable stuff on the premises, by any chance?”

“Nothing a thief could make much out of. There’s a thousand or fifteen hundred pounds worth of platinum, dishes, electrodes, and so forth, in the safe. I believe the man on the beat is supposed to give special attention to the place and notify anything suspicious immediately; but I’ve never known anything of the sort to happen.”
===Chinese===
探长似乎被什么念头点醒了。

“所里可巧有什么值钱的物件吗？”

“没有哪样是小偷能发大财的。保险柜里存着价值一千到一千五百英镑的铂——铂皿、电极等等。我想巡警本该对这地方格外留神，一有可疑立时上报；不过这类事我还没听说过。”

===Original===
“Rather a difficult position for our men if the staff can come and go freely at night,” the Inspector pointed out. “If a constable sees a light in the window, what’s he to make of it? Does Dr. Silverdale work late often?”

“I really couldn’t tell you.”
===Chinese===
“倘若职员们夜里能自由出入，我们的人可就难办了，”探长指出。“警察若看见窗口有灯，该怎么作想？西尔弗代尔先生常工作到深夜吗？”

“这我可真说不上。”

===Original===
“You don’t see much of him privately, sir?”

“Very little,” Markfield answered. “Only when I run across him by accident down town, like last night.”
===Chinese===
“私下里您跟他往来不多吧，先生？”

“很少，”马克菲尔德答道。“只是进城时偶然撞见，比如昨晚。”

===Original===
“You met him, did you?”

“Hardly even that. I happened to drop into the Grosvenor for dinner after I left here. I can’t get meals at home just now unless I cook ’em myself. As I was finishing my coffee, Silverdale came into the dining-room with Miss Deepcar and took a table in the window recess. I didn’t disturb them, and I don’t think they noticed me.”
===Chinese===
“您碰见他了？”

“连碰见都算不上。我昨天离开这儿以后，碰巧进格罗夫纳（Grosvenor）吃晚饭。眼下家里开不出饭，除非我自己动手烧。我刚喝完咖啡，西尔弗代尔就和迪普卡小姐进了餐厅，在窗凹里拣了张桌子。我没去搅扰他们，我想他们也没留意我。”

===Original===
“Then they were just beginning dinner when you left the place? What time was that, can you tell me?”

Markfield looked suspiciously at the Inspector.
===Chinese===
“那么您离开那儿时，他们才刚开始用晚饭？那是几点钟，您说得上吗？”

马克菲尔德狐疑地打量了探长一眼。

===Original===
“You’re trying to get me to say something that you want to use against—well, someone else, shall we say? I don’t care about it, frankly. But since you could get the information from the waiter who served them, there’s no harm done. I went to the Grosvenor at 6:35 or thereabouts. I was going down to the Research Station afterwards to pick up some notes, so I dined early that night. Silverdale and Miss Deepcar came in just as I was finishing dinner—that would be about a quarter past seven or thereby. I expect they were going on to some show afterwards.”
===Chinese===
“你想套我的话，好拿去对付——嗯，就算是对付另外哪一位吧？坦白说，我不喜欢这个。不过反正这消息你也能从伺候他们的侍者嘴里问到，说出来倒也无妨。我是 6:35 前后进的格罗夫纳。饭后我还要下农业研究站去取些笔记，所以那晚吃得早。我快吃完的时候，西尔弗代尔和迪普卡小姐才进来——大约七点一刻的样子。我料想他们之后还要赶什么演出去。”

===Original===
“Was she in evening dress?”

“Ask me another. I never can tell whether a girl’s in evening dress or not, nowadays, with these new fashions.”
===Chinese===
“她穿的是晚礼服？”

“这个另请高明。如今这些新式时装，一个姑娘算不算穿晚礼服，我从来分辨不出。”

===Original===
Inspector Flamborough closed his notebook and took his leave, followed by Sir Clinton. When they reached the street again and had got into the waiting car, the Chief Constable turned to his subordinate.

“You collected a lot of interesting information that time.”
===Chinese===
弗拉姆伯勒探长合上笔记本，起身告辞，克林顿爵士跟在后面。两人重又来到街上，上了候着的汽车，郡警察局长这才转向下属。

“这回您收罗到不少有意思的情报啊。”

===Original===
“I noticed you left it all to me, sir; but I think I got one or two things worth having. It’s a bit disconnected; and it’ll take some thinking before it’s straightened out.”

“What’s your main inference, as things stand?” Sir Clinton inquired.
===Chinese===
“我注意到您全推给了我，先生；不过我想，我也到手了一两件有价值的东西。就是有点东鳞西爪；要理出个头绪，还得好好思量一番。”

“照眼下的情形，您的主要推断是什么？”克林顿爵士问道。

===Original===
“Well, sir, it’s a bit early yet. But I’ve been wondering about one thing, certainly.”

“And that is?”
===Chinese===
“呃，先生，眼下说还为时稍早。不过有一件事，我确实一直在犯嘀咕。”

“是什么？”

===Original===
“And that is whether Peeping Tom’s name wasn’t Thomasina,” Flamborough announced gravely.

“There are two sexes, of course,” Sir Clinton admitted with equal gravity. “And inquisitiveness is supposed to be more strongly developed in the female than in the male. The next thing will be to consider whether Mr. Justice shouldn’t be rechristened Justitia. One ought to take all possibilities into account.”
===Chinese===
“就是那位偷窥的汤姆（Peeping Tom），名字会不会其实叫托马西娜，”弗拉姆伯勒斯郑重其事地宣布道。

“人分两种性别，这当然，”克林顿爵士以同样的郑重承认道。“而且据说，好奇心在女性身上，比在男性身上发达得更强。接下来就该考虑，那位‘正义’先生是不是该改名叫尤斯提提亚（Justitia）。一切可能性都该想到才是。”

## VIII The Hassendean Journal / 八 哈森丁的日记

===Original===
When Ronald Hassendean’s journal was found to consist of four bulky volumes of manuscript, Sir Clinton hastily disclaimed any desire to make its acquaintance in extenso and passed over to Inspector Flamborough the task of ploughing through it in detail and selecting those passages which seemed to have direct bearing on the case. The Inspector took the diary home with him and spent a laborious evening, lightened at times by flashes of cynical enjoyment when the writer laid bare certain aspects of his soul. Next day Flamborough presented himself at Sir Clinton’s office with the books under his arm; and the paper slips which he had used as markers made a formidable array as they projected from the edges of the volumes.
===Chinese===
罗纳德·哈森丁的日记一查，竟是整整四大卷手稿。克林顿爵士连忙声明，自己并无从头到尾结识它们的兴致，便把逐字细啃、从中拣选与本案似有直接关联的段落这桩差事，移交给了弗拉姆伯勒探长。探长把日记带回家，苦熬了一个晚上，其间偶尔也闪过几丝刻薄的快意——每当日记作者剖白自己灵魂的某些侧面的时候。第二天，弗拉姆伯勒把那几本书夹在腋下，到克林顿爵士的办公室报到；他当书签用的纸条从书口边缘支棱出来，密密层层，好不壮观。

===Original===
“Good Lord!” exclaimed the Chief Constable in consternation. “Do you mean to say I ought to read through about a hundred and fifty passages in inferior handwriting? Life’s too short for that. Take ’em away, Inspector, and get someone to write me a précis.”
===Chinese===
“老天爷！”郡警察局长愕然叫道。“你是说，我得把大约一百五十段拙劣笔迹的文字统统读一遍？人生苦短，可没这工夫。拿走吧，探长，找个人给我写篇摘要来。”

===Original===
Flamborough’s lips opened into a broad smile under his toothbrush moustache.

“It’s not really so bad as it looks, sir,” he explained. “The white slips were put in to mark anything that seemed to bear remotely on the business; but the passages directly relevant to the affair are indicated by red slips. I think you ought to glance through that last lot. There aren’t really very many of them.”
===Chinese===
弗拉姆伯勒唇上的牙刷胡底下绽开一个宽宽的笑。

“实际没有看上去那么糟，先生，”他解释道。“白纸条标的是一切似乎沾得上边的内容；与本案直接相关的段落，则用红纸条标示。我想后面那一类您总该浏览一遍。其实也没有多少段。”

===Original===
He deposited the volumes on Sir Clinton’s desk so that the marking-tabs projected towards his superior. Sir Clinton eyed them without any enthusiasm.

“Well, I suppose duty calls, Inspector. I’ll go over them with you, just in case you want to give me any special points drawn from your general reading in the Works of Hassendean. If you’ve got a morbid craving for voluminous writers, you’d better start on the *Faerie Queene*. It, also, leads up to the death of a Blatant Beast.”
===Chinese===
他把那几卷摆上克林顿爵士的办公桌，让标笺的纸舌一律朝着上司支棱过去。克林顿爵士瞧着它们，提不起半点兴致。

“唉，想来是职责相召了，探长。我就陪你翻一遍吧，省得你从《哈森丁全集》的通读中得了什么特别的心得，没处奉献。你若是对卷帙浩繁的作家怀着病态的渴慕，不如去啃《仙后》（*Faerie Queene*）。它也是一路写到一头诽谤兽（Blatant Beast）的死。”

===Original===
“I read a bit of it at school, sir. I’m keeping the rest for a rainy day.”

Sir Clinton again eyed the four stout volumes with unconcealed aversion. Quite obviously he was ready to catch at anything in order to postpone the examination of them, even now that he had decided to submit to the Inspector’s ruling.

“Before I start on this stuff, there are one or two points I want to get cleared up. First of all, did you get any reports in reply to our inquiries about young Hassendean’s car being seen on the roads that night?”
===Chinese===
“我在学校里读过一点，先生。剩下的留着下雨天消遣。”

克林顿爵士又把那四大厚卷打量了一遍，嫌恶之情毫不掩饰。明摆着，哪怕他已决定听凭探长的裁断，仍随时准备抓住任何由头，好把检阅这堆东西的日子往后拖。

“动手啃这堆货之前，有一两处我想先弄清楚。首先，关于案发当晚有没有人在路上见过年轻的哈森丁的汽车，我们的查询可有回音？”

===Original===
“No, sir. The only motor information we got was about one car that was stolen under cover of the fog. It’s being looked into. Oh, yes, and there was an inquiry for the name and address of the owner of a car. It seems somebody got hit by a motor and managed to take its number. I don’t think any real damage was done. It’s just one of these try-on cases.”
===Chinese===
“没有，先生。汽车方面唯一的消息，是有辆车趁着大雾被人偷了，正在追查。哦，对了，还有一桩查询，是要某辆车主人的姓名住址。似乎是有谁被汽车撞了一下，还设法记下了车牌号。我看没真出什么伤损，不过是这类想讹一把的案子。”

===Original===
“Something more important now. Did you find out from the man on the beat whether there was a light in Silverdale’s room at the Croft-Thornton on the night of the murders?”

“How did you come to think of that, sir? I didn’t mention it to you.”
===Chinese===
“现在说件更要紧的。你向巡警问明了没有：命案当晚，西尔弗代尔在克罗夫特-索顿研究所的房间里亮没亮灯？”

“您怎么想到这上头的，先生？我可没跟您提过。”

===Original===
“It was just a long shot, Inspector. As soon as Silverdale stated that he had been working all that night at the Croft-Thornton, I was pretty sure he was lying. So were you, I guessed. Then you walked across to the window and looked down. As I was wondering myself whether the window was visible from the street, it didn’t take much mind-reading to see what you were driving at. And from your questions to Markfield later on, I couldn’t help inferring that you had the constable on the beat at the back of your thoughts. Obviously you meant to check Silverdale’s story by asking the constable on duty if he’d noticed a light in Silverdale’s room that night. There was no light, of course?”
===Chinese===
“不过是碰运气的一猜，探长。西尔弗代尔一说他整晚都在克罗夫特-索顿干活，我就十有八九断定他在撒谎。我料想你也是。随后你踱到窗前往下望。当时我自己正琢磨这扇窗从街上望不望得见，要猜你打的什么主意，也用不着多少读心术。后来你又向马克菲尔德问东问西，我不由得推断，你心里一直惦着那个巡警。你分明是想找当值巡警核对，看他那晚留意过西尔弗代尔房里的灯没有。自然是没亮灯，对吧？”

===Original===
“No, sir. There wasn’t a light anywhere in the building, that night. I made the constable look up his notebook.”

“Then you’ve caught Master Silverdale in a very bad lie. By the way, I suppose you noticed that girl who came into his room while we were talking to him: the Miss Deepcar who dined with him down town that night. What did you make of her?”
===Chinese===
“没有，先生。那晚整座楼里哪儿的灯都没亮。我叫那巡警翻了他的值勤簿。”

“那你就逮住西尔弗代尔少爷一个弥天大谎了。对了，我想你留意到我们同他谈话时进他房间去的那个姑娘了吧：就是当晚在城里陪他吃饭的迪普卡小姐。你对她怎么看？”

===Original===
“Pretty girl, sir, very pretty indeed. The quiet sort, I’d judge. One of the kind that a man might do a good deal to get hold of, if he was keen on her.”

Sir Clinton’s expression showed that he did not disagree with the Inspector’s summing up.

“By the way,” he continued, “did you take any note of what she said to Silverdale at that time?”

“Not particularly, sir. It was all Greek to me—too technical.”
===Chinese===
“标致的姑娘，先生，实在标致得很。我看是娴静那一类的。她属于那种姑娘——男人若是迷上了，为了把她弄到手，什么事儿都干得出来。”

克林顿爵士的神色表明，他对探长的品评并无异议。

“对了，”他接着说，“她当时对西尔弗代尔说的话，你留意了没有？”

“没怎么留意，先生。对我简直是天书——太专门了。”

===Original===
“It interested me, though,” Sir Clinton confessed. “I’ve a chemical friend—the London man who’s going to act as a check on Markfield for us in the search for the poison, as a matter of fact—and he talks to me occasionally about chemistry. You don’t know what a ‘mixed melting-point’ is, I suppose?”

“No, sir. It sounds confused,” said the Inspector mischievously.

The Chief Constable treated this as beneath contempt.
===Chinese===
“我倒是感兴趣，”克林顿爵士招认道。“我有位搞化学的朋友——其实就是替我们在毒物搜查中牵制马克菲尔德的那位伦敦人——他偶尔也跟我谈谈化学。‘混合熔点’是什么，你大概不知道吧？”

“不知道，先生。听着就乱糟糟的，”探长促狭地说。

郡警察局长只当这话不屑一顾。

===Original===
“I’ll explain the point,” he pursued, “and then you’ll know as much as I do. A pure substance melts at a higher temperature than it does when it’s contaminated by even a trace of some foreign material. Suppose that you had been given a stuff which you thought was pure quinine and you had no chemicals handy to do the ordinary tests for quinine. What you’d do would be this. You’d take the melting-point of your sample first of all. Then to the sample you’d add a trace of something which you knew definitely was quinine—a specimen from your laboratory stock, say. Then you’d take the melting-point of this mixture. Suppose the second melting-point is lower than the first, then obviously you’ve been adding an impurity to your original sample. And since something, that you know definitely to be quinine, has acted as an impurity, then clearly the original stuff isn’t quinine. On the other hand, if the addition of your trace of quinine to the sample doesn’t lower the melting-point, then your original sample is proved to be quinine also. That mixing of the two stuffs and taking the melting-point is what they call ‘taking a mixed melting-point.’ Does that convey anything to you?”
===Chinese===
“我来把这一点讲明白，”他继续道，“听完你就跟我一样内行了。一种纯物质，熔化时的温度，比它被哪怕一星半点异物沾染后要高。假设有人给了你一样东西，你以为那是纯奎宁，手边又没有试剂做奎宁的常规检验。那你就这么办：先测出你这份样品的熔点。然后往样品里加入微量你确知是奎宁的东西——比如从实验室库存里取出的一份标本。再测这混合物的熔点。倘若第二个熔点比第一个低，那显然，你往原来的样品里掺进的是杂质。而你确知是奎宁的东西竟起了杂质的作用，那么原先那东西分明就不是奎宁。反过来，把你那点奎宁加进样品，熔点并不降低，那你原来的样品便证明也是奎宁。把两样东西混在一起测熔点，他们管这叫‘测混合熔点’。这对你要有些启发么？”

===Original===
“Not a damn, sir,” Flamborough admitted crudely, in a tone of despair. “Could you say it all over again slowly?”

“It’s hardly worth while at this stage,” Sir Clinton answered, dismissing the subject. “I’ll take it up again with you later on, perhaps, after we get the P.M. results. It was an illuminating conversation, though, Inspector, if my guess turns out to be right. Now there’s another matter. Have you any idea when the morning papers get into the hands of the public—I mean the earliest hour that’s likely in the normal course?”
===Chinese===
“一点儿也没懂，先生，半个字也没懂，”弗拉姆伯勒粗声招认，语调里透着绝望。“您能不能再慢慢讲一遍？”

“到这个份上就不值得了，”克林顿爵士答道，把话头一挥。“等尸检结果出来，回头我再跟你讲，也许吧。不过探长，倘若我猜得不错，那可是场大有讲究的谈话。现在还有一桩事。晨报几时到读者手里，你可有数——我指的是正常情况下最早的钟点？”

===Original===
“It happens that I do know that, sir. The local delivery starts at 7 a.m. In the suburbs, it’s a bit later, naturally.”

“Just make sure about it, please. Ring up the publishing departments of the *Courier* and the *Gazette*. You needn’t worry about the imported London papers.”

“Very good, sir. And now about this journal, sir?” the Inspector added with a touch of genial impishness in his voice.

“Evidently you won’t be happy till I look at it,” Sir Clinton grumbled with obvious distaste for the task. “Let’s get it over, then, since you’re set on the matter.”
===Chinese===
“碰巧这个我还真知道，先生。本地投递清晨 7 时开始。郊区自然稍晚些。”

“那就请你再落实一下。给《信使报》（*Courier*）和《公报》（*Gazette*）的发行部打个电话。伦敦贩来的报纸就不必管了。”

“遵命，先生。那么这日记呢，先生？”探长添了一句，声音里带着一点和气的促狭。

“明摆着，我不看它你就不得安生，”克林顿爵士咕哝道，对这差事的嫌恶明明白白。“那就了结了吧，既然你认定了这桩事。”

===Original===
“So far as I can see, sir,” Flamborough explained, “there are only three threads in it that concern us: the affair he had with that girl Hailsham; his association with Mrs. Silverdale; and his financial affairs—which came as a surprise to me, I must admit.”

Sir Clinton glanced up at the Inspector’s words; but without replying, he drew the fat volumes of the journal towards him and began his examination of the passages to which Flamborough’s red markers drew attention.
===Chinese===
“依我看，先生，”弗拉姆伯勒解释道，“里头与我们相干的只有三条线：他同海尔沙姆那姑娘的一段情事；他同西尔弗代尔太太的往来；还有他的财务——这一条我必须承认，出乎我的意料。”

听了探长这话，克林顿爵士抬眼瞥了瞥；但他不答话，只把那几大卷日记拽到面前，开始检阅弗拉姆伯勒的红纸条标出的段落。

===Original===
“He didn’t model his style on Pepys, evidently,” he said as he turned the leaves rapidly, “There seems to be about ten percent of ‘I’s’ on every page. Ah! Here’s your first red marker.”

He read the indicated passage carefully.

“This is the description of his feelings on getting engaged to Norma Hailsham,” he commented aloud. “It sounds rather superior, as if he felt he’d conferred a distinct favour on her in the matter. Apparently, even in the first flush of young love, he thought that he wasn’t getting all that his merits deserved. I don’t think Miss Hailsham would have been flattered if she’d been able to read this at the time.”
===Chinese===
“他显然没师法佩皮斯（Pepys）的文笔，”他一面飞快翻动书页，一面说。“每一页上‘我’字看来占了有一成。啊！你头一张红纸条到了。”

他把标出的那段仔细读了一遍。

“这是他订婚时心境的记述，订的是诺玛·海尔沙姆（Norma Hailsham），”他高声评道。“听着颇为自高自大，仿佛在这件事上，他觉得是赏了那姑娘天大的恩典。显然，即便在青春爱恋最初的热乎劲儿里，他也认为自己得的还不够自己的长处所应得的。我想海尔沙姆小姐当时若是能读到这些，断不会觉得受宠若惊。”

===Original===
He passed rapidly over some other passages without audible comment, and then halted for a few moments at an entry.

“Now we come to his meeting with Mrs. Silverdale, and his first impressions of her. It seems that she attracted him by her physique rather than by her brains. Of course, as he observes: ‘What single woman could fully satisfy all the sides of a complex nature like mine?’ However, he catalogues Mrs. Silverdale’s attractions lavishly enough.”
===Chinese===
他另外几段一掠而过，没出声点评，随后在一条日记前停了片刻。

“这就到他结识西尔弗代尔太太，和她给他的第一印象了。看来吸引他的是她的身段容貌，而非头脑。当然，如他自己所说：‘哪一个女人能独自满足像我这样复杂天性的方方面面？’话虽如此，他把西尔弗代尔太太的种种动人之点开列起来，倒也够慷慨的。”

===Original===
Flamborough, with a recollection of the passage in his mind, smiled cynically.

“That side of his complex nature was highly developed, I should judge,” he affirmed. “It runs through the stuff from start to finish.”

Sir Clinton turned over a few more pages.

“It seems as though Miss Hailsham began to have some inklings of his troubles,” he said, looking up from the book. “This is the bit where he’s complaining about the limitations in women’s outlooks, you remember. Apparently he’d made his fiancée feel that his vision took a wider sweep than she imagined, and she seems to have suggested that he needn’t spend so much time in staring at Mrs. Silverdale. It’s quite characteristic that in this entry he’s suddenly discovered that the Hailsham girl’s hands fail to reach the standard of beauty which he thinks essential in a life-companion. He has visions of sitting in suppressed irritation while these hands pour out his breakfast coffee every day through all the years of marriage. It seems to worry him quite a lot.”
===Chinese===
弗拉姆伯勒脑子里记着那一段，脸上泛起讥诮的笑。

“他复杂天性里的那一方面，我看发育得十分茁壮，”他断言道。“从头到尾贯穿着这堆货。”

克林顿爵士又翻过几页。

“似乎海尔沙姆小姐开始对他的烦恼有点察觉了，”他从书上抬起眼说。“就是他抱怨女人眼界局限的那一段，你记得的。显然他让未婚妻觉出，他的视野比她想象的扫得宽；而她似乎也提议过，他不必花那么多工夫盯着西尔弗代尔太太瞧。很能见其为人的是，在这一条里他忽然发现，海尔沙姆家姑娘的双手够不上他认作终身伴侣必不可少的美之标准。他眼前浮现出这样的图景：婚后漫长的岁月里，每天早晨这双手给他斟早餐咖啡，他坐在那儿强压着火气。这似乎让他烦心得很。”

===Original===
“You’ll find that kind of thing developing as you go on, sir. The plain truth is that he was tiring of the girl and he simply jotted down everything he could see in her that he didn’t find good enough for him.”

Sir Clinton glanced over the next few entries.

“So I see, Inspector. Now it seems her dancing isn’t so good as he used to think it was.”

“Any stick to beat a dog with,” the Inspector surmised.
===Chinese===
“往下读您还会发现这一路货色愈演愈烈，先生。明摆着的实情是，他对那姑娘腻了，便把她身上一切他瞧着配不上自己的地方，一条条都记下来。”

克林顿爵士扫过下面几条。

“我看到了，探长。现在又好像她的舞跳得也不如他原先以为的那么好了。”

“想打狗，还愁找不着棍子嘛，”探长揣度道。

===Original===
“Now they seem to have got the length of a distinct tiff, and he rushes at once to jot down a few bright thoughts on jealousy with a quotation from Mr. Wells in support of his thesis. It appears that this ‘entanglement,’ as he calls it, is cramping his individuality and preventing the full self-expression of his complex nature. I can’t imagine how we got along without that word ‘self-expression’ when we were young. It’s a godsend. I trust the inventor got a medal.”
===Chinese===
“现在他们似乎闹到了明明白白拌一场嘴的地步，他立刻奋笔疾书，记下几条关于嫉妒的高明见地，还引了威尔斯先生（Mr. Wells）一句话来撑腰。据他看来，这场他所谓的‘孽缘’，正束缚着他的个性，妨碍他复杂天性的充分‘自我表现’。我真想不出我们年轻那会儿，没有‘自我表现’这个词是怎么混过来的。真是天赐之宝。我相信发明这词的人准领了勋章。”

===Original===
“The next entry’s rather important, sir,” Flamborough warned him.

“Ah! Here we are. We come to action for a change instead of all this wash of talk. This is the final burst-up, eh? H’m!”

He read over the entry thoughtfully.
===Chinese===
“下一条相当要紧，先生，”弗拉姆伯勒提醒他。

“啊！到了。总算换上实事了，不再这一通滔滔空谈。这是最后彻底崩掉的一场了吧，呃？唔！”

他把那一条寻思着读了一遍。

===Original===
“Well, the Hailsham girl seems to have astonished him when it came to the pinch. Even deducting everything for his way of looking at things, she must have been fairly furious. And Yvonne Silverdale’s name seems to have entered pretty deeply into the discussion. ‘She warned me she knew more than I thought she did; and that she’d make me pay for what I was doing.’ And again: ‘She said she’d stick at nothing to get even with me.’ It seems to have been rather a vulgar scene, altogether. ‘She wasn’t going to be thrown over for that woman without having her turn when it came.’ You know, Inspector, it sounds a bit vindictive, even when it’s filtered through him into his journal. The woman scorned, and hell let loose, eh? I’m not greatly taken with the picture of Miss Hailsham.”
===Chinese===
“呃，到了紧要关头，海尔沙姆家那姑娘似乎让他吃了一惊。即便把他看事的路数全打上折扣，她当时也必是相当狂怒。而且伊冯娜·西尔弗代尔的名字，似乎深深卷进了那场争执。‘她警告我，说她知道得比我想的多；还说我这样做，她定要我付出代价。’又一条：‘她说要不择手段跟我算这笔账。’总的说来，那场面似乎颇为粗鄙难看。‘轮到自己，她可不打算白白让那女人挤掉，非先出这口气不可。’你知道吧，探长，这话就算经过他过滤再录进日记，听着也颇有几分怨毒。女人受了轻慢，地狱便随之开闸，是吧？海尔沙姆小姐这幅画像，我可不大欣赏。”

===Original===
“A bit of a virago,” the Inspector agreed. “What I was wondering when I read that stuff was whether she’d keep up to that standard permanently or whether this was just a flash in the pan. If she’s the kind that treasures grievances. …”

“She might be an important piece in the jigsaw, you mean? In any case, I suppose we’ll have to get her sized up somehow, since she plays a part in the story.”

The Chief Constable turned back to the journal and skimmed over a number of the entries.

“Do you know,” he pointed out after a time, “that young fellow had an unpleasant mind.”

“You surprise me,” the Inspector retorted ironically. “I suppose you’ve come to the place where he gets really smitten with Mrs. Silverdale’s charms?”

“Yes. There’s a curious rising irritation through it all. It’s evident that she led him on, and then let him down, time after time.”
===Chinese===
“有那么点泼妇的意思，”探长附和道。“我读那些东西时琢磨的是：她这股劲头是能一直保持在这水准上呢，还是不过昙花一现。倘若她是那种把仇怨当宝贝攒着的人。 ……”

“你是说，她可能是拼图里要紧的一块？无论如何，既然她在故事里有一角，我想我们总得设法把她掂量清楚。”

郡警察局长重新翻回日记，略读了若干条。

“你可知道，”过了一会儿他指点道，“那小伙子有副叫人不快的头脑。”

“您真让我吃惊，”探长反唇讥道。“我想您是读到他为西尔弗代尔太太的魅力真正神魂颠倒的地方了吧？”

“正是。字里行间有一股渐渐升起的怒火，怪得很。显然她一次次撩拨他，又一次次把他晾在那儿。”

===Original===
“For all his fluff about his complex character and so forth, he really seems to have been very simple,” was Flamborough’s verdict. “She led him a dance for months; and anyone with half an eye could see all along that she was only playing with him. It’s as plain as print, even in his own account of the business.”

“Quite, I admit. But you must remember that he imagined he was out of the common—irresistible. He couldn’t bring himself to believe things were as they were.”

“Turn to the later entries,” the Inspector advised; and Sir Clinton did so.

“This is the one you mean? Where she turned him down quite bluntly, so that even he got an inkling of how matters really stood?”

“Yes. Now go on from there,” Flamborough directed.

Sir Clinton passed from one red marker to the other, reading the entries indicated at each of the points.
===Chinese===
“别看他把自己复杂的天性吹得天花乱坠，这人其实简单得很，”弗拉姆伯勒的判词如此。“她牵着他鼻子转了好几个月；半只眼看事的人从头就看得出来，她不过是拿他耍着玩。这是白纸黑字一样明白的事，即便照他自己的记述也是如此。”

“不错，我承认。但你得记住，他自命不凡——觉得无人能抗拒他。他怎么也不肯相信事情就是那么个样子。”

“翻到后面几条，”探长建议道；克林顿爵士依言翻了过去。

“你说的是这一条吧？她相当不客气地回绝了他，连他都约略觉出事情究竟是怎么回事了？”

“正是。从那儿往下读，”弗拉姆伯勒指点道。

克林顿爵士从一张红纸条看到下一张，把每一处标出的条目都读了。

===Original===
“The tune changes a bit; and his irritation seems to be on the upgrade. One gets the impression that he’s casting round for a fresh method of getting his way and that he hasn’t found one that will do? Is that your reading of it?”

“Yes,” Flamborough confirmed. “He talks about getting his way ‘by hook or by crook,’ and one or two other phrases that come to the same thing.”

“Well, that brings us up to a week ago. There seems to be a change in his tone, now. More expectation and less exasperation, if one can put it that way.”

“I read it that by that time he’d hit on his plan. He was sure of its success, sir. Just go on to the next entry please. There’s something there about his triumph, as he calls it.”
===Chinese===
“调子变了一点；他的恼怒似乎在步步升级。给人的印象是，他在四下里另觅一条遂愿的路子，而还没觅着一条顶用的？你是这么解读的吧？”

“正是，”弗拉姆伯勒证实道。“他谈到要‘千方百计’地遂愿，还有一两句归结到底一个意思的话。”

“好，这就到了一周之前。如今他的口气似乎变了。可以说，期待多了，愤懑少了。”

“我读出的意思是，到那时他已想出了他的计划。他对成功十拿九稳，先生。请再往下看一条。那里有他所谓的凯旋的记载。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton glanced down the page and as he did so his face lit up for a moment as though he had seen one of his inferences confirmed.

“This what you mean?” he asked. “ ‘And only I shall know of my triumph’?”

“That’s it, sir. Highfalutin and all that; but it points to his thinking he had the game in his hands. I’ve puzzled my brains a bit over what he really meant by it, though. One might read it that he meant to murder the girl in the end. That would leave him as the only living person who knew what had happened, you see?”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士的眼睛顺着页面扫下去，扫着扫着，脸上霎时一亮，仿佛看见自己的一项推断得到了证实。

“你说的是这个？”他问。“‘唯有我将知道我的凯旋’？”

“正是，先生。浮夸之词，诸如此类；可这表明他自以为胜券在握。不过他这话究竟何所指，我倒是绞了些脑汁。有一种读法是：到头来他是要杀掉那姑娘。那样一来，知道内情的活人就只剩他一个了，你明白吧？”

===Original===
“I’m not in a position to contradict that assumption,” Sir Clinton confessed. “But so far as that goes, I think you’ll find the point cleared up in a day or two at the rate we’re going.”

“You’re very optimistic, sir,” was all the Inspector found to reply. “Now I’ve left one matter to the end, because it may have no bearing on the case at all. The last year of that journal is full of groans about his finances. He seems to have spent a good deal more than he could afford, in one way and another. I’ve noted all the passages if you want to read them, sir. They’re among the set marked with white slips.”
===Chinese===
“这个假设，我还无从驳倒，”克林顿爵士承认道。“不过就这一点而言，照我们眼下的进度，我想过不了一两天你就见分晓。”

“您真是乐观，先生，”探长只寻出这一句作答。“还有一桩事我留到最后，因为它或许与本案毫不相干。那日记的最后一年，满纸都是对他财务的哀叹。这样那样加起来，他花的似乎比他挣的家底多出好大一截。相关段落我都标出来了，先生，您要不要过目。它们都夹在白纸条标出的那一堆里。”

===Original===
“Just give me the gist of them,” the Chief Constable suggested. “From that, I can see whether I want to wade through the whole thing or not.”

“It’s simple enough, sir. He’s been borrowing money on a scale that would be quite big for his resources. And I gather from some of the entries that he had no security that he could produce. It seems he daren’t go to his uncle and ask him to use his capital as security—I mean young Hassendean’s own capital which was under his uncle’s control as trustee. So he was persuaded to insure his life in favour of his creditor for a good round sum—figure not mentioned.”
===Chinese===
“给我说说大意就行，”郡警察局长提议道。“听了大意，我再看要不要通读全篇。”

“事情够简单的，先生。他借钱的规模，照他的进项来说相当可观。而且我从几条日记里得知，他拿不出可以出示的抵押。他似乎不敢去求他伯父拿他的本金作保——我是说年轻的哈森丁自己的本金，那笔钱在他伯父手里，由他以受托人身份掌管。于是有人劝他为自己保人寿险，指定债权人为受益人，保的是个可观的大数目——数目没提。”

===Original===
“So in the present circumstances the moneylender will rake in the whole sum insured, after paying only a single premium?”

“Unless the insurance company can prove suicide.”

Sir Clinton closed the last volume of the journal.

“I’ve heard of that sort of insurance racket before. And of course you remember that shooting affair in Scotland thirty years ago when the prosecution made a strong point out of just this very type of transaction. Have you had time to make any inquiries along that line yet?”
===Chinese===
“那么照眼前的情形，只消缴过一期保费，那放债的就要把整笔保金统统揽进腰包了？”

“除非保险公司能证明是自杀。”

克林顿爵士合上日记的最后一卷。

“这种保险的猫腻，我从前就领教过。当然，你总记得三十年前苏格兰那桩枪击案吧，控方抓住的强项，恰恰就是这一类交易。这一条线你来得及查过没有？”

===Original===
Flamborough was evidently glad to get the opportunity of showing his efficiency.

“I took it up at once, sir. In one entry, he mentioned the name of the company: the Western Medical and Mercantile Assurance Co. I put a trunk call through to their head office and got the particulars of the policy. It’s for £5,000 and it’s in favour of Dudley Amyas Guisborough & Co.—the moneylender.”
===Chinese===
弗拉姆伯勒显然巴不得有机会显一显自己的干练。

“我立刻就查了，先生。有一条日记里，他提到了保险公司的名字：西部医学商业保险公司（Western Medical and Mercantile Assurance Co.）。我给他们总部挂了个长途电话，问来了保单的详情。保额 £5,000，受益人是达德利·埃米亚斯·吉斯伯勒公司——就是那放债的。”

===Original===
“Sounds very aristocratic,” the Chief Constable commented.

“Oh, that’s only his trade sign. His real name’s Spratton.”

“No claim been made yet?”

“No, sir. I don’t suppose he’s hurrying. The inquest was adjourned, you remember; and until they bring in some verdict excluding suicide, Spratton can’t do much. There’s a suicide clause in the policy, I learned. But if it pans out as a murder, then Spratton’s £5,000 in pocket.”
===Chinese===
“听着真够贵族气的，”郡警察局长评论道。

“哦，那不过是他开店的字号。他的真名叫斯普拉顿。”

“索赔提出了没有？”

“还没有，先生。谅他也不急。死因研讯已经延期了，您记得的；在研讯作出排除自杀的裁定之前，斯普拉顿做不出什么名堂。我打听到，保单里有自杀条款。可这案子若是坐实了谋杀，斯普拉顿那 £5,000 就稳进口袋了。”

===Original===
“In fact, Inspector, Mr. Justice is doing a very good bit of work for Dudley Amyas Guisborough & Co.”

Flamborough seemed struck by an idea.

“I’ll go and pay a call on Mr. Spratton, I think. I’ll do it now.”

“Oh, he’s a local light, is he?”
===Chinese===
“说实在的，探长，那位‘正义’先生正替达德利·埃米亚斯·吉斯伯勒公司卖着大力气呢。”

弗拉姆伯勒似乎被一个念头打中了。

“我想我要去拜会拜会斯普拉顿先生了。这就去。”

“哦，他算本地的一位闻人，是吧？”

===Original===
“Yes, sir. He was mixed up in a case last year. You won’t remember it, though. It never came to much. Just an old man who fell into Spratton’s hands and was driven to suicide by the damnable rapacity of that shark. Inspector Ferryside had to look into the matter, and I remember talking over the case with him. That’s how it sticks in my memory.”
===Chinese===
“是的，先生。去年一桩案子里，他就搅和在里头。不过您不会记得。那案子没闹出多大动静。就是一个老头儿落进了斯普拉顿的手，被那条鲨鱼穷凶极恶的贪得无厌逼上了自尽的路。费里赛德（Ferryside）探长奉命查办此事，我记得还同他谈过这案子。所以它在我记忆里扎了根。”

===Original===
“Well, see what you can make of him, Inspector. But I shan’t be disappointed if you come back empty-handed. Even if he were mixed up in this affair, he’ll have taken good care not to leave a straight string leading back to his front door. If it was a case of murder for profit, you know, there would be plenty of time to draw up a pretty good scheme beforehand. It wouldn’t be done on the spur of the moment.”
===Chinese===
“那好，看看你从他身上能摸出点什么，探长。不过你若是空手回来，我也不会失望。即便他搅和在这案子里，他也必定小心在意，不留一条直通他家前门的线索。你知道，若是谋财害命，事先有的是工夫，尽可以拟一套相当周全的计划。那不会是一时兴起干出来的事。”

## IX The Creditor / 九 债主

===Original===
Inspector Flamborough’s orderly mind found something to respect in the businesslike appearance of the moneylender’s premises. As he waited at the counter of the outer office while his card was submitted to the principal, he was struck by the spick-and-span appearance of the fittings and the industry of the small staff.
===Chinese===
放债人的营业场所一派井然气象，弗拉姆伯勒那讲求条理的头脑也不由生出几分敬意。他在外间的柜台前候着，名片正被呈送进去给老板，眼前那些陈设的光洁齐整、寥寥几名职员的勤勉干练，都给他留下了深刻印象。

===Original===
“Quite impressive as a flytrap,” he ruminated. “Looks like a good solid business with plenty of money to spend. And the clerks have good manners, too. Spratton’s evidently bent on making a nice impression on new clients.”
===Chinese===
“当个捕蝇器倒真够气派，”他暗自沉吟。“瞧着像一桩殷实牢靠的买卖，有的是闲钱可花。职员们也知礼数。斯普拉顿分明是一门心思要给新主顾留个好印象。”

===Original===
He was not kept waiting more than a minute before the clerk returned and ushered him into a room which had very little of the office in its furnishings. As he entered, a clean-shaven man in the late thirties rose from an armchair beside the fire. At the first glance, his appearance seemed to strike some chord in the Inspector’s memory; and Flamborough found himself pursuing an elusive recollection which he failed to run to ground.
===Chinese===
他没等多久，不出一分钟，职员便回来引他进了一个房间——论陈设，这屋里几乎看不出办公室的影子。他进门时，一个年近四十、面庞刮得光洁的男人从炉边的扶手椅里站起身来。乍一照面，此人的相貌仿佛在探长的记忆里拨响了某根弦；弗拉姆伯勒便不由自主地追索起一段捉摸不定的往事，却终究没能追个水落石出。

===Original===
The moneylender seemed to regard the Inspector’s visit as a perfectly normal event. His manner was genial without being effusive.

“Come in, Inspector,” he invited, with a gesture towards one of the comfortable chairs. “Try a cigarette?”
===Chinese===
放债人似乎把探长的来访视作再平常不过的事。他态度亲切，却不失分寸。

“请进，探长，”他招呼道，朝一张舒适的椅子摆了摆手。“来支烟？”

===Original===
He proffered a large silver box, but Flamborough declined to smoke.

“And what can I do for you?” Spratton inquired pleasantly, replacing the box on the mantelpiece. “Money’s very tight these days.”
===Chinese===
他递上一只大大的银烟盒，弗拉姆伯勒却谢绝了。

“那么，我能为您效什么劳呢？”斯普拉顿一面把烟盒放回壁炉架，一面和颜悦色地问。“这些日子，银根可紧得很呐。”

===Original===
“I’m not a client,” Flamborough informed him, with a slightly sardonic smile. “Sorry to disappoint you.”

The moneylender’s eyes narrowed, but otherwise he showed no outward sign of his feelings.
===Chinese===
“我可不是来借钱的客户，”弗拉姆伯勒带着一丝讥诮的笑意说明。“让您失望了，抱歉。”

放债人双眼微眯，除此之外，心绪没有半点形之于色。

===Original===
“Then I’m rather at a loss to know what you want,” he confessed, without any lapse from his initial geniality. “I run my business strictly within the four corners of the Act. You’ve no complaint about that?”

The Inspector had no intention of wasting time.
===Chinese===
“那我就颇有些茫然，不知您所为何来了，”他坦承道，先前那份和气丝毫未减。“我的买卖，向来是严严实实圈在法令范围之内做的。这一点上您没什么可挑剔的吧？”

探长无意虚耗工夫。

===Original===
“It’s this affair of young Hassendean,” he explained. “The young fellow who was murdered the other day. You must have seen the case in the papers. I understand he was a client of yours.”

A flash of intelligence passed over the moneylender’s face, but he suppressed it almost instantly.
===Chinese===
“是为年轻的哈森丁那桩案子，”他说明道。“就是前几天遇害的那个年轻人。案子您想必在报上见过了。据我了解，他曾在您名下借贷。”

一丝了然的神色掠过放债人的脸，但他几乎转瞬便压了下去。

===Original===
“Hassendean?” he repeated, as though cudgelling his memory. “I’ve some recollection of the name. But my business is a large one, and I don’t profess to carry all the details in my head.”

He stepped over to the bell and rang it. When a clerk appeared in answer to the summons, the moneylender turned to give an order:
===Chinese===
“哈森丁？”他重复道，仿佛正苦苦敲打着记忆。“这名字我有些印象。不过敝号买卖做得大，我可不敢自夸把一切细目都装在脑袋里。”

他踱到铃前按了铃。一名职员应召而来，放债人转身吩咐：

===Original===
“I think we had some transactions with a Mr. Hassendean—Mr. Ronald Hassendean, isn’t it?” he glanced at Flamborough for confirmation, and then continued: “Just bring me that file, Plowden.”

It did not take the Inspector long to make up his mind that this byplay was intended merely to give Spratton time to find his bearings; but Flamborough waited patiently until the clerk returned and placed a filing-case on the table. Spratton turned over the leaves for a few moments, as though refreshing his memory.
===Chinese===
“我想我们与一位哈森丁先生有过几笔往来——罗纳德·哈森丁先生，是吧？”他瞥了弗拉姆伯勒一眼以求印证，随后接着说：“把那份档案给我取来，普洛登（Plowden）。”

探长没用多大工夫便认定，这番过场戏无非是给斯普拉顿留出定神稳住的时间；但弗拉姆伯勒耐心等着，直到职员回来把一只档案匣搁在桌上。斯普拉顿翻动纸页片刻，像是在重温记忆。

===Original===
“This fellow would have made a good actor,” Flamborough reflected with a certain admiration. “He does it deuced well. But who the devil does he remind me of?”

Spratton’s nicely-calculated interlude came to an end, and he turned back to the Inspector.
===Chinese===
“这家伙本可以做个好演员，”弗拉姆伯勒暗自思忖，颇带几分钦佩。“演得真叫一个绝。可他究竟让我想起谁来着？”

斯普拉顿那段分寸拿捏得恰到好处的过场收了尾，他转回来面对探长。

===Original===
“You’re quite right. I find that he had some transactions with us!”

“They began about eleven months ago, didn’t they?”
===Chinese===
“您说得一点不错。我查到了，他与本行确有过几笔往来！”

“这些往来，是大约十一个月前起的头吧？”

===Original===
The moneylender nodded in confirmation.

“I find that I lent him £100 first of all. Two months after that—he not having repaid anything—I lent him £200. Then there was a further item of £300 in April, part of which he seems to have paid back to me later on in order to square up for the interest which he hadn’t paid.”
===Chinese===
放债人点头印证。

“我查到，起初我借给他 £100。两个月后——彼时分文未还——我又借给他 £200。再到四月里，另有一笔 £300，其中一部分他后来似乎还给了我，为的是抵结他一直未付的利息。”

===Original===
“What security had you for these loans?”

Again the moneylender’s eyes narrowed for a moment; but his manner betrayed nothing.
===Chinese===
“这几笔放款，您手里握着什么担保？”

放债人的眼睛又眯了片刻；但他的举止神态未露丝毫口风。

===Original===
“Up to that time, I was quite satisfied with his prospects.”

“And after that he borrowed more from you?”
===Chinese===
“在那之前，我对他的前景还算相当满意。”

“在那以后，他又向您添借了？”

===Original===
“Apparently.” Spratton made a pretence of consulting the file. “He came to me in June for another £500, and of course the interest was mounting up gradually.”

“He must have been making the money fly,” Flamborough suggested with a certain indifference. “I wish I could see my way to splash dibs at that rate. It would be a new experience. But when it came to figures of that size, I suppose you expected something better in the way of security?”
===Chinese===
“看情形是。”斯普拉顿装出查阅档案的样子。“六月里他来找我，又借了 £500，利息么，自然也就步步滚高了。”

“他想必是把钱撒得漫天飞了，”弗拉姆伯勒语气淡漠地搭了一句。“我要也能那样阔手阔脚地泼钱，倒算是开一回眼界。不过数目到了这等地步，我想您总指望些更硬扎的担保了吧？”

===Original===
Despite the Inspector’s casual tone, the moneylender seemed to suspect a trap.

“Well, by that time he was in my books for well over a thousand.”
===Chinese===
探长语气虽随意，放债人却似乎疑心话里藏着圈套。

“唔，到那时候，他挂在我账上的已远不止一千镑了。”

===Original===
He appeared to feel that frankness would be best.

“I arranged matters for him,” he continued. “He took out a policy on his life with the Western Medical and Mercantile. I have the policy in my safe if you wish to see it.”
===Chinese===
他似乎觉得坦率才是上策。

“是我替他把这事张罗妥当的，”他继续道。“他在西部医学商业保险公司投了一份人寿险。保单就锁在我的保险柜里，您要过目，随时可取。”

===Original===
“Of course you allowed a reasonable margin for contingencies, I suppose?” Flamborough inquired sympathetically.

“Oh, naturally I expected him to go on borrowing, so I had to allow a fair margin for contingencies. The policy was for £5,000.”
===Chinese===
“您当然为不测留足了相当的余地吧？”弗拉姆伯勒一脸体谅地问道。

“哦，我自然料到他还会接着借下去，所以不得不为不测留出相当的余地。保单的保额是 £5,000。”

===Original===
“So you’re about £4,000 in pocket, now that he’s dead,” Flamborough commented enviously. “Some people are lucky.”

“Against that you’ve got to offset the bad debts I make,” Spratton pointed out.
===Chinese===
“这么说，他这一死，您净落约 £4,000，”弗拉姆伯勒语含艳羡地评了一句。“有人天生走运。”

“可这笔账，您得拿我吃的坏账来相抵，”斯普拉顿指出。

===Original===
Flamborough could not pretend to himself that he had managed to elicit much of importance during his call; but he had no excuse for prolonging the interview. He rose to his feet.

“I don’t suppose we shall need any of these facts if it comes to trying anyone,” he said, as he prepared to leave. “If we do, you’ll have plenty of warning, of course.”
===Chinese===
弗拉姆伯勒没法骗自己说，这一趟访出了多少要紧的东西；但他也寻不出由头把会面再拖下去。他起身离座。

“我想，往后真要开庭审人，这些数目恐怕也用不上，”他一面准备告辞，一面说。“万一用得上，自然会提前充分知会您。”

===Original===
The moneylender opened a door which allowed a direct exit into the corridor, and Flamborough went out. As he walked along the passage, he was still racking his memory to discover who Spratton resembled; and at last, as he reached the pavement outside, it flashed into his mind.

“Of course! It’s the Chief! Put a moustache on to that fellow and dye his hair a bit and he might pass for Driffield in the dusk. He’s not a twin-brother; but there’s a resemblance of sorts, undoubtedly.”
===Chinese===
放债人打开一扇直通走廊的门，弗拉姆伯勒便出去了。一路走过穿堂，他还在苦搜记忆，想弄清斯普拉顿究竟像谁；终于，走到外头的人行道上时，那念头蓦地闪进脑海。

“可不是嘛！是头儿！给那家伙安上两撇胡子，头发再染上一染，暮色里没准就能冒充德里菲尔德了。算不上孪生兄弟；可这份相像，确凿无疑是有的。”

===Original===
He returned to headquarters feeling that he had wasted his time over the moneylender. Except that he had now seen the man in the flesh and had an opportunity of sizing him up, he was really no further forward than he had been before; for the few actual figures of transactions which he had obtained were obviously of little interest in themselves.
===Chinese===
他回到总局，自觉在放债人身上白耗了工夫。除了亲眼见到此人、得着一个掂他斤两的机会之外，他实在不比先前多前进一步；因为问到手的那几笔实打实的交易数目，本身显然无甚意味。

===Original===
As he entered the police station, a constable came forward.

“There’s a gentleman here, Inspector Flamborough. He’s called about the Silverdale case and he wants to see you. He’s a foreigner of the name of Renard.”
===Chinese===
他一进警察局，一名警员迎上前来。

“这儿来了位先生，弗拉姆伯勒探长。为西尔弗代尔那桩案子来的，想见您。是个外国人，姓勒纳尔。”

===Original===
“Very well. Send him along to me,” Flamborough ordered.

In a few moments, the constable ushered in a small man with a black moustache and a shock of stiffly-brushed hair which gave him a foreign appearance. The Inspector was relieved to find that he spoke perfect English, though with a slight accent.
===Chinese===
“好的。领他来见我，”弗拉姆伯勒吩咐道。

不多时，警员领进来一个小个子男人，唇上蓄一撮黑髭，一头硬毛刷似的头发直挺挺支棱着，一副外国人的模样。探长听他英语说得地道流利，只略带一丝口音，不由松了口气。

===Original===
“My name is Octave Renard,” he introduced himself. “I am the brother of Mrs. Yvonne Silverdale.”

Flamborough, with a certain admiration for the fortitude of the little man in the tragic circumstances, made haste to put him at his ease by expressing his sympathy.
===Chinese===
“鄙人奥克塔夫·勒纳尔，”他自我介绍道。“我是伊冯娜·西尔弗代尔太太的兄长。”

想到这个小个子男人身陷惨变犹能撑持，弗拉姆伯勒心生几分敬意，忙不迭致以慰问，好教他宽怀。

===Original===
“Yes, very sad,” said the little Frenchman, with an obvious effort to keep himself under control. “I was very fond of my sister, you understand. She was so gay, so fond of life. She enjoyed herself every moment of the day. And now—”

A gesture filled out the missing phrase.
===Chinese===
“是啊，非常不幸，”小个子法国人说，显然强自按捺着。“我很疼我的妹妹，您是知道的。她那么快活，那么热爱生活。一天里的每一刻，她都过得有滋有味。可如今——”

一个手势，补全了那半截咽下去的话。

===Original===
Flamborough’s face betrayed his commiseration; but he was a busy man, and could ill afford to waste time.

“You wished to see me about something?”
===Chinese===
弗拉姆伯勒脸上现出怜悯之意；但他是个忙人，实在耽误不起工夫。

“您要见我，是有事相商？”

===Original===
“All I know is what was printed in the newspapers,” Renard explained. “I would like to learn the truth of the case—the real facts. And you are in charge of the case, I was told. So I come to you.”

Flamborough, after a moment’s hesitation, gave him an outline of the bungalow tragedy, softening some of the details and omitting anything which he thought it undesirable to make public. Renard listened, with an occasional nervous twitch which showed that his imagination was at work, clothing the bare bones of the Inspector’s narrative with flesh.
===Chinese===
“我所知道的，只是报纸上印的那些，”勒纳尔解释道。“我想了解这案子的真相——真实的情况。人家告诉我，这案子是您主管的。所以我便来找您了。”

弗拉姆伯勒略一迟疑，便把平房惨案的梗概对他讲了一遍，有些细节说得轻描淡写，凡他认为不宜公开的，一概略去。勒纳尔听着，间或神经质地抽动一下，可见他的想象正在运转，正给探长叙述的那副枯骨披上血肉。

===Original===
“It is a bad business,” he said, shaking his head mournfully as Flamborough concluded. “To think that such a thing should have happened just when she had had her great stroke of good-fortune! It is incredible, the irony of Fate.”

The Inspector pricked up his ears.
===Chinese===
“这是件祸事，”弗拉姆伯勒讲完，他悲戚地摇着头说。“想想吧，偏在她撞上了天大的好运之后，竟出了这种事！真是不可思议，命运弄人，一至于此。”

探长竖起了耳朵。

===Original===
“She’d had a piece of good luck, lately, you say, Mr. Renard? What was that?”

“You do not know?” the little man inquired in surprise. “But surely her husband must have told you? No?”
===Chinese===
“您是说，勒纳尔先生，她新近交了一步好运？是怎么回事？”

“您不知道？”小个子男人讶然问道。“可她丈夫总该对您说过吧？没有？”

===Original===
Flamborough shook his head.

“That is strange,” Renard continued. “I do not quite understand that. My sister was the favourite of her aunt. She was down in her will, you understand? And my aunt was a very wealthy woman. Pots of money, as you English say. For some time my aunt has been in feeble health. She has been going downhill for the last year or more. A heart trouble, you understand. And just a fortnight ago, puff!—she went out like that. Like a blown-out candle.”
===Chinese===
弗拉姆伯勒摇了摇头。

“这就怪了，”勒纳尔接着说。“这我有些想不明白。我妹妹是她姑母的心头肉。遗嘱里立着她的名字，您明白吗？我那位姑母，是个非常有钱的女人。照你们英国人的说法，钱多得流油。姑母身子骨衰弱已有些时候了。这一年多来，更是江河日下。心脏的毛病，您明白的。就在两个星期前，噗！——她就这么去了。像一支吹熄的蜡烛。”

===Original===
“Yes?” the Inspector prompted.

“Her will was in the keeping of her lawyer and he communicated the contents to myself and my sister. We were trustees, you see. I had a little bequest to myself; but the principal sum went to my sister. I was surprised; I had not thought that my aunt had so much money—mostly in American stocks and shares. In your English money it came to about £12,000. In francs, of course, it is colossal—a million and a half at least.”
===Chinese===
“后来呢？”探长催问道。

“她的遗嘱存在她的律师手里，律师把内容知会了我和我妹妹。您瞧，我们俩是受托人。给我名下留了小小一笔遗赠；本金则全数归了我妹妹。我很意外；我原想不到姑母竟有这么多钱——大半是美国股票证券。折成你们英国的币制，约计 £12,000。折成法郎，那数目自然就惊人了——至少一百五十万。”

===Original===
“Ah!” interjected Flamborough, now keenly interested. “And your sister knew of this?”

“She learned it from me just two days before her death. And you understand, there was no grief with it. My aunt had suffered terribly in the last few months. Angina pectoris, very painful. We were quite glad to see her suffering at an end.”
===Chinese===
“啊！”弗拉姆伯勒插话道，此刻兴头十足。“这事您妹妹知道么？”

“就在她去世前两天，才从我这里得知的。您也明白，这里面谈不上多少哀伤。姑母最后几个月受了大罪。心绞痛，疼得凶。眼看她的苦楚到了头，我们都还觉得欣慰。”

===Original===
Flamborough felt that this fresh piece of information needed consideration before he ventured on to the ground which had been disclosed.

“Are you staying in Westerhaven, Mr. Renard?” he inquired.
===Chinese===
弗拉姆伯勒觉得，这块新翻出来的地面，还须先掂量掂量，方能举步踏上去。

“您眼下暂居韦斯特黑文么，勒纳尔先生？”他问道。

===Original===
“Yes, for a few days yet, I expect,” the little man answered. “I have some legal matters in my hands which need my presence on the spot. As my sister is now dead, there is the disposal of this money to be considered. I find difficulties which I had not expected.”

“And your address during your stay will be?”
===Chinese===
“是的，我想还要盘桓几天，”小个子男人答道。“我手里有几件法律事务，需要本人到场。如今我妹妹死了，这笔钱的处置也须计议。我遇上了些原先不曾逆料的难处。”

“那么您盘桓期间落脚的地址是？”

===Original===
“I am at the Imperial Hotel. You can always find me there.”

“Well, Mr. Renard, I’d like to have a talk with you later on, if I may. Just at present, I’m very busy. Perhaps you could spare a few minutes when my hands are free.”
===Chinese===
“我住在帝国饭店（Imperial Hotel）。您随时可以在那里找到我。”

“这样吧，勒纳尔先生，若是可以，往后我想再同您谈谈。眼下我实在忙得很。等我手头松快了，您能否拨冗几分钟？”

===Original===
“I shall be delighted,” Renard acquiesced. “Whenever you wish to see me, send a message. I am much worried, you understand?” he concluded, with a quiver in his voice which pierced through the official coating of Flamborough and touched the softer material inside.
===Chinese===
“非常乐意，”勒纳尔欣然应允。“您几时要见我，捎个信来便是。我心里烦乱得很，您明白么？”他收尾时声音一阵发颤，这一颤穿透了弗拉姆伯勒那层官家的外壳，触到了里面较柔软的质料。

## X Information Received / 十 情报到手

===Original===
For the next day or two, Sir Clinton’s interest in the Hassendean case appeared to have faded out; and Inspector Flamborough, after following up one or two clues which eventually proved useless, was beginning to feel perturbed by the lack of direct progress which the investigation showed. Rather to his relief, one morning the Chief Constable summoned him to his office. Flamborough began a somewhat apologetic account of his fruitless investigations; but Sir Clinton cut him short with a word or two of appreciation of his zeal.
“Here’s something more definite for you to go on,” he suggested. “I’ve just had a preliminary report from the London man whom we put on to search for the poison. I asked him to let me have a private opinion at the earliest possible moment. His official report will come in later, of course.”
===Chinese===
此后一两天，克林顿爵士对哈森丁一案的兴趣似乎淡了下去；弗拉姆伯勒探长追查了一两条线索，到头来全证明无用，眼见调查迟迟不见切实的进展，心里渐渐不安起来。倒是让他宽心的是，一天早晨，郡警察局长把他召进了办公室。弗拉姆伯勒开始颇为歉然地叙述自己劳而无功的查访；克林顿爵士却打断他，用一两句话嘉勉了那股干劲。
“给你件比较实在的东西接着往下查，”他提示道。“我刚刚收到那位伦敦人的初步报告——就是咱们派去查毒物的那位。我请他尽早先给我一份私人意见。正式报告当然随后才到。”

===Original===
“Has he spotted it, sir?” the Inspector inquired eagerly.
“He’s reached the same conclusion as I did—and as I suppose you did also,” Sir Clinton assured him.
Flamborough looked puzzled.
“I didn’t spot it myself,” he confessed diffidently. “In fact, I don’t see how there was anything to show definitely what stuff it was, barring dilatation of the eye-pupils, and that might have been due to various drugs.”
===Chinese===
“他认出那东西来了吗，先生？”探长急切地问道。
“他得出的结论跟我的——我想也跟你的——是一路，”克林顿爵士向他保证。
弗拉姆伯勒面露困惑。
“我自己可没认出来，”他怯怯地招认。“说实话，我看不出有什么凭据能断定那究竟是种什么东西，只有瞳孔放大这一条，可这也可能是好些种药物造成的。”

===Original===
“You should never lose an opportunity of exercising your powers of inference, Inspector. I mustn’t rob you of this one. Now put together two things: the episode of the mixed melting-point and the phrase about his ‘triumph’ that young Hassendean wrote in his journal. Add the state of the girl’s pupils as a third point—and there you are!”
===Chinese===
“锻炼推断本事的机会，探长，你一个也不该放过。这一个我总不好替你剥夺了去。现在把两件事合到一处：混合熔点那段过节，还有小哈森丁在日记里写下的关于他‘凯旋’的那句话。再把那姑娘的瞳孔状况添作第三点——答案不就出来了么！”

===Original===
Flamborough pondered for a while over this assortment of information, but finally shook his head.
“I don’t see it yet, sir.”
===Chinese===
弗拉姆伯勒把这堆信息琢磨了好一阵，终于还是摇了摇头。
“我还是没看出来，先生。”

===Original===
“In that case,” Sir Clinton declared, with the air of one bestowing benevolence, “I think we’d better let it dawn on you slowly. You might be angry with yourself if you realised all of a sudden how simple it is.”
He rose to his feet as he spoke.
“I think we’ll pay a visit to the Croft-Thornton Institute now, and see how Markfield has been getting along with his examination. We may as well have a check, before we begin to speculate too freely.”
===Chinese===
“既然如此，”克林顿爵士拿出一副布施恩惠的神气宣布道，“我看还是让你慢慢开窍的好。你要是猛然间醒悟这事有多简单，怕是要跟自己怄气呢。”
说话间他已站起身来。
“我想咱们这就到克罗夫特-索顿研究所走一趟，看看马克菲尔德的检验办得怎么样了。趁咱们还没放开手脚瞎猜，先核对一道为好。”

===Original===
They found Markfield in his laboratory, and Sir Clinton came to business at once.
“We came over to see how you were getting on with that poison business, Dr. Markfield. Can you give us any news?”
Markfield indicated a notebook on his desk.
===Chinese===
他们在实验室里找到了马克菲尔德，克林顿爵士开门见山。
“我们是来瞧瞧那桩毒物的事你办得如何了，马克菲尔德医生。可有什么消息见告？”
马克菲尔德指了指桌上一本笔记簿。

===Original===
“I’ve got it out, I think. It’s all there; but I haven’t had time to write a proper report on it yet. It was—”
“Hyoscine?” Sir Clinton interrupted.
Markfield stared at him with evident appreciation.
“You’re quite right,” he confirmed, with some surprise. “I suppose you’ve got private information.”
===Chinese===
“我想我已经把它查出来了。都记在这儿了；只是还没来得及写成一份像样的报告。那是——”
“东莨菪碱？”克林顿爵士插话道。
马克菲尔德瞪眼望着他，眼中分明透着钦佩。
“您说得一点不错，”他证实道，颇有几分诧异。“我猜您另有一条内线消息。”

===Original===
The Chief Constable evaded the point.
“I’m asking this question only for our own information; you won’t be asked to swear to it in court. What amount of hyoscine do you think was in the body, altogether? I mean, judging from the results you obtained yourself.”
===Chinese===
郡警察局长把这一点岔了开去。
“我问这个只为咱们自己知情；上了法庭，并不会要你对它起誓。照你看，尸身里的东莨菪碱总共有多少？我是说，依你自己得出的结果来判断。”

===Original===
Markfield considered for a moment.
“I’m giving you a guess, but I think it’s fairly near the mark. I wouldn’t, of course, take my oath on it. But the very smallest quantity, judging from my results, would be somewhere in the neighbourhood of seven or eight milligrammes.”
“Have you looked up anything about the stuff—maximum dose, and so forth?” Sir Clinton inquired.
===Chinese===
马克菲尔德思量了片刻。
“我给您的只是个估数，不过我想离谱不远。当然，我不敢为它起誓。但依我的结果判断，最小最小的量，也在七八毫克上下。”
“关于这东西你可查过什么资料——极量之类？”克林顿爵士问。

===Original===
“The maximum dose of hyoscine hydrobromide is down in the books as six-tenths of a milligramme—about a hundredth of a grain in apothecaries’ weights.”
“Then she must have swallowed ten or twelve times the maximum dose,” Sir Clinton calculated, after a moment or two of mental arithmetic.
He paused for a space, then turned again to Markfield.
===Chinese===
“氢溴酸东莨菪碱的极量，书上载明为零点六毫克——按药衡制（apothecaries' weights）约合百分之一格令（grain，药衡单位）。”
“那么她吞下的必定有极量的十倍乃至十二倍，”克林顿爵士心算了片刻，得出这个数。
他顿了片刻，又转向马克菲尔德。

===Original===
“I’d like to see the hyoscine in your store here, if you can lay your hands on it easily.”
Markfield made no objection.
“If you’d come in yesterday, the bottle would have been here, beside me. I’ve taken it back to the shelf now.”
===Chinese===
“我想瞧瞧你们这儿库房里的东莨菪碱，若是不难取到手的话。”
马克菲尔德并无异议。
“你们若是昨天来，那瓶子就在这儿，在我手边。如今我已经把它放回架子上了。”

===Original===
“I suppose you borrowed it to do a mixed melting-point?” Sir Clinton asked.
“Yes. When there’s only a trace of a stuff to identify, it’s the easiest method. But you seem to know something about chemistry?”
“About enough to make mistakes with, I’m afraid. It simply happened that someone described the mixed melting-point business to me once; and it stuck in my mind. Now suppose we look at this store of yours.”
===Chinese===
“我猜你是借去测混合熔点了？”克林顿爵士问。
“正是。要辨认的东西只剩一星半点时，这是最省事的方法。不过，您对化学似乎也略知一二？”
“恐怕也就够用来出岔子的。只是碰巧有人给我讲过混合熔点这套名堂；它就留在我脑子里了。现在咱们瞧瞧你这间库房吧。”

===Original===
Markfield led them along a passage and threw open a door at the end.
“In here,” he said.
“You don’t keep it locked?” Sir Clinton inquired casually, as he passed in, followed by the Inspector.
===Chinese===
马克菲尔德领他们沿一条过道走过去，猛地推开尽头的一扇门。
“就在这儿。”他说。
“不上锁？”克林顿爵士边往里走边随口问道，探长跟在他身后。

===Original===
“No,” Markfield answered in some surprise. “It’s the general chemical store for this department. There’s no point in keeping it locked. All our stuffs are here, and it would be a devilish nuisance if one had to fish out a key every time one wanted some chloroform or benzene. We keep the duty-free alcohol locked up, of course. That’s necessary under the Customs’ regulations.”
Sir Clinton readily agreed.
“You’re all trustworthy people, naturally,” he admitted. “It’s not like a place where you have junior students about who might play thoughtless tricks.”
===Chinese===
“不锁，”马克菲尔德答道，颇有几分诧异。“这是本部门共用的化学药品库。锁着没有意义。所有的药品全在这儿，倘若每回要取一点氯仿或苯，都得先摸出一把钥匙来，那可烦死人了。免税酒精我们自然是锁起来的。那是海关条例的硬性规定。”
克林顿爵士欣然认同。
“诸位自然都是靠得住的人，”他认可道。“这儿不比那种有低年级学生出没、会搞出不知轻重恶作剧的地方。”

===Original===
Markfield went over to one of the cases which lined the room, searched along a shelf, and took down a tiny bottle.
“Here’s the stuff,” he explained, holding it out to the Chief Constable. “That’s the hydrobromide, of course—a salt of the alkaloid itself. This is the compound that’s used in medicine.”
Now that he had got it, Sir Clinton seemed to have little interest in the substance. He handed it across to Flamborough who, after looking at it with would-be sagacity, returned it to Markfield.
===Chinese===
马克菲尔德走到沿壁排开的一只只药柜前，在架子上寻了一气，取下一只小小的瓶子。
“这就是那东西，”他把瓶子递给郡警察局长，说明道。“这自然是氢溴酸盐——生物碱本身成的一种盐。医药上用的就是这种化合物。”
东西一到手，克林顿爵士对这物质似乎就兴味寥寥了。他把它递给弗拉姆伯勒；探长故作高明地端详了一阵，又还给了马克菲尔德。

===Original===
“There’s just one other point that occurs to me,” the Chief Constable explained, as Markfield returned the poison-bottle to its original place. “Have you, by any chance, got an old notebook belonging to young Hassendean on the premises? Anything of the sort would do.”
The Inspector could make nothing of this demand and his face betrayed his perplexity as he considered it. Markfield thought for a few moments before replying, evidently trying to recall the existence of any article which would suit Sir Clinton’s purpose.
“I think I’ve got a rough notebook of his somewhere in my room,” he said at last. “But it’s only a record of weighings and things like that. Would it do?”
“The very thing,” Sir Clinton declared, gratefully. “I’d be much obliged if you could lay your hands on it for me now. I hope it isn’t troubling you too much.”
===Chinese===
“我还想起另一点，”马克菲尔德把毒药瓶放回原处时，郡警察局长说明道。“你们这儿可有哈森丁那年轻人的旧笔记簿？随便哪一路的都行。”
探长摸不透这一要求的用意，思量之际，满脸的困惑都挂在了脸上。马克菲尔德想了几想才作答，显然在追忆有什么物件能派上克林顿爵士的用场。
“我想我房里什么地方有他一本随手记的草簿，”他终于说道。“不过那上头只是称量记录之类。那本行么？”
“正是此物，”克林顿爵士感激地说道。“劳你此刻就替我把它翻出来，感激不尽。但愿不至太搅扰你。”

===Original===
It was evident from Markfield’s expression that he was as much puzzled as the Inspector; and his curiosity seemed to quicken his steps on the way back to his room. After a few minutes’ hunting, he unearthed the notebook of which he was in search and laid it on the table before Sir Clinton. Flamborough, familiar with young Hassendean’s writing, had no difficulty in seeing that the notes were in the dead man’s hand.
===Chinese===
马克菲尔德脸上的神色表明，他跟探长一样摸不着头脑；回自己房里去的一路，好奇心仿佛让他的脚步都快了几分。翻寻了几分钟，他挖出了要找的那本笔记簿，摊在克林顿爵士面前的桌上。弗拉姆伯勒对小哈森丁的笔迹并不生疏，一眼便看出这些记录正出自死者之手。

===Original===
Sir Clinton turned over the leaves idly, examining an entry here and there. The last one seemed to satisfy him, and he put an end to his inspection. Flamborough bent over the table and was mystified to find only the following entry on the exposed leaf:
“By the way,” said Sir Clinton casually, “do you happen to have one of your own notebooks at hand—something with the same sort of thing in them?”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士懒懒地翻动纸页，这里那里察看一两笔。最后一条似乎让他满意了，他便不再检视。弗拉姆伯勒俯身桌上，见摊开的那页上仅有如下一条记录，不由大惑不解：
“对了，”克林顿爵士漫不经心地说，“你手边可有你自己的一本笔记簿——上头记着同类名堂的那种？”

===Original===
Markfield, obviously puzzled, went over to a drawer and pulled out a notebook which he passed to the Chief Constable. Again Sir Clinton skimmed over the pages, apparently at random, and then left the second book open beside the first one. Flamborough, determined to miss nothing, examined the exposed page in Markfield’s notebook, and was rewarded by this:—
“Damned if I see what he’s driving at,” the Inspector said savagely to himself. “It’s Greek to me.”
“A careless young fellow,” the Chief Constable pronounced acidly. “My eye caught three blunders in plain arithmetic as I glanced through these notes. There’s one on this page here,” he indicated the open book. “He seems to have been a very slapdash sort of person.”
===Chinese===
马克菲尔德显然一头雾水，走到一只抽屉跟前，抽出一本笔记簿递给郡警察局长。克林顿爵士又把纸页溜了一遍，看样子是随手乱翻，随后把第二本摊开在第一本旁边。弗拉姆伯勒决意不放过丝毫，细看马克菲尔德那本摊开的一页，得到的犒赏如下：——
“见鬼，我真看不出他这是打的什么主意，”探长狠狠地自语道。“对我简直如读天书。”
“毛手毛脚的年轻人，”郡警察局长刻薄地下了断语。“我扫过这些记录，一眼就撞见三处初等算术的低级错误。这一页上就有一处，”他指了指摊开的那本簿子。“瞧这架势，他是个做事极其马虎草率的人。”

===Original===
“An unreliable young hound!” was Markfield’s slightly intensified description. “It was pure influence that kept him here for more than a week. Old Thornton, who put up most of the money for building this place, was interested in him—knew his father, I think—and so we had to keep the young pup here for fear of rasping old Thornton’s feelings. Otherwise. …”
The gesture accompanying the aposiopesis expressed Markfield’s idea of the fate which would at once have befallen young Hassendean had his protector’s influence been withdrawn.
===Chinese===
“一头靠不住的年轻畜生！”马克菲尔德的形容又加重了几分。“他能在这儿待过一个星期，全凭人情的力量。老索顿（Old Thornton）出的钱盖起了这座所的大半，对他青眼有加——我想是认得他父亲——于是我们只好把这小崽子留在所里，生怕惹老索顿不痛快。要不然。……
伴随这半截话的手势，已把马克菲尔德的心思表露无遗：倘若那层庇护的人情一朝撤去，小哈森丁立时会落得个怎样的下场。

===Original===
The Chief Constable appeared enlightened by this fresh information.
“I couldn’t imagine how you came to let him have the run of the place for so long,” he confessed. “But, of course, as things were, it was evidently cheaper to keep him, even if he did no useful work. One can’t afford to alienate one’s benefactors.”
After a pause, he continued, reverting apparently to an earlier line of thought:
===Chinese===
听了这条新情况，郡警察局长显得恍然开朗。
“我原本还想不通，你们怎么竟容他在所里白逛这么久，”他招认道。“不过，当然，照那情形，纵使他干不出一点有用的活，留着他终归还是合算些。恩主可开罪不起。”
停了片刻，他又接着说，看样子是回到了先前的思路上：

===Original===
“Let’s see. You made out that something like twelve times the normal dose of hyoscine had been administered?”
Markfield nodded his assent, but qualified it in words:
“That’s a rough figure, remember.”
===Chinese===
“咱们且对一对。你方才判定，进入体内的东莨菪碱约有常用量的十二倍？”
马克菲尔德点头称是，嘴上却加了保留：
“记住，那只是个粗略的数。”

===Original===
“Of course,” Sir Clinton agreed. “As a matter of fact, the multiple I had in my mind was 15. I suppose it’s quite possible that some of the stuff escaped you and that your figure is an underestimate?”
“Quite likely,” Markfield admitted frankly. “I gave you the lowest figure, naturally—a figure I could swear to if it came to the point. As it’s a legal case, it’s safer to be under than over the mark. But quite probably, as you say, I didn’t manage to isolate all the stuff that was really present; and I wouldn’t deny that the quantity in the body may have run up to ten milligrammes or even slightly over it.”
“Well, it’s perhaps hardly worth bothering about,” the Chief Constable concluded. “The main thing is that even at the lowest estimate she must have swallowed enough of the poison to kill her in a reasonably short time.”
===Chinese===
“自然，”克林顿爵士附和道。“说实话，我心里的倍数是 15。依你看，会不会有些剂量从你手里漏了过去，你那个数估低了？”
“很有可能，”马克菲尔德爽快地承认。“我给您的自然是个最低数——真到那个地步，我敢起誓的数。这既是桩官司，报低总比报高稳妥。不过很可能如您所说，实有的东西我没能全部分离出来；体内含量上到十毫克、甚至略微出头，我也不来否认。”
“也罢，这或许不值得多计较，”郡警察局长收束道。“要紧的是，哪怕按最低的估计，她吞下的毒物也足以在不算长的时辰里要她的命。”

===Original===
With this he seemed satisfied, and after a few questions about the preparation and submission of Markfield’s official report, he took his leave. As he turned away, however, a fresh thought seemed to strike him.
“By the way, Dr. Markfield, do you know if Miss Hailsham’s here this morning?”
“I believe so,” Markfield answered. “I saw her as I came in.”
===Chinese===
这一点上他似乎满意了，又问了几个关于筹备、呈递马克菲尔德正式报告的问题，便起身告辞。然而转身之际，一个新念头似乎又击中了他。
“对了，马克菲尔德医生，你知道海尔沙姆小姐今天上午在所里么？”
“我想在，”马克菲尔德答道。“我进来的时候看见她了。”

===Original===
“I’d like to have a few words with her,” Sir Clinton suggested.
“Officially?” Markfield demanded. “You’re not going to worry the girl, are you? If it’s anything I can tell you about, I’d be only too glad, you know. It’s not very nice for a girl to have the tale going round that she’s been hauled in by the police in a murder case.”
The Chief Constable conceded the point without ado.
===Chinese===
“我想同她谈几句，”克林顿爵士提议。
“正式传问？”马克菲尔德质问道。“你们不是要为难那姑娘吧？但凡有我能代答的，您知道，我万分乐意。一个姑娘家，闹得风言风语，说她在人命案里叫警察传了去，那名声可不大好听。”
郡警察局长二话不说便认了这一点。

===Original===
“Then perhaps you could send for her and we could speak to her in here. It would be more private, and there need be no talk about it outside.”
“Very well,” Markfield acquiesced at once. “I think that would be better. I’ll send for her now.”
He rang a bell and despatched a boy with a message. In a few minutes a tap on the door sounded, and Markfield ushered Norma Hailsham into the room. Inspector Flamborough glanced at her with interest, to see how far his conception of her personality agreed with the reality. She was a girl apparently between twenty and twenty-five, dressed with scrupulous neatness. Quite obviously, she spent money freely on her clothes and knew how to get value for what she spent. But as his eyes travelled up to her face, the Inspector received a more vivid impression. Her features were striking rather than handsome, and Flamborough noted especially the squarish chin and the long thin-lipped flexible mouth.
===Chinese===
“那就劳你派人请她来，咱们就在这屋里同她谈。这样更私密些，外头也不至于起闲话。”
“很好，”马克菲尔德当即应承。“我想这样更好。我这就打发人去叫她。”
他按了铃，打发一名听差捎话去。几分钟之后，门上响起轻轻的叩击声，马克菲尔德把诺玛·海尔沙姆让进屋来。弗拉姆伯勒探长饶有兴味地打量她，想看看自己先前对这人品的揣想与真人对得上几分。这是个二十到二十五岁之间的姑娘，衣着一丝不苟地整洁。显而易见，她在衣装上花钱毫不吝惜，也懂得怎样把花的钱穿出效果来。可当他的目光上移到她脸上时，探长得到的是更加鲜活的一击。她的容貌说不上标致，却自有一种逼人的特色，弗拉姆伯勒尤其留意到那方方正正的下巴，和那张薄薄的、柔软善变的长嘴。

===Original===
“H’m!” he commented to himself. “She might flash up in a moment, but with that jaw and those lips she wouldn’t cool down again in a hurry. I was right when I put her down as a vindictive type. Shouldn’t much care to have trouble with her myself.”
He glanced at Sir Clinton for tacit instructions, but apparently the Chief Constable proposed to take charge of the interview.
“Would you sit down, Miss Hailsham,” Sir Clinton suggested, drawing forward a chair for the girl.
===Chinese===
“哼！”他心里评点。“她兴许一霎时就烧起来，可有那样的下巴、那样的嘴，要再冷却下去可没那么快。我把她归到睚眦必报一类，是对的了。换了我，可不想同她结怨。”
他朝克林顿爵士瞟去一眼，以求心照不宣的指示，但郡警察局长显然打算亲自主持这场问话。
“请坐，海尔沙姆小姐，”克林顿爵士提议道，为姑娘拉过一把椅子。

===Original===
Flamborough noticed with professional interest that by his apparently casual courtesy, the Chief Constable had unobtrusively manoeuvred the girl into a position in which her face was clearly illuminated by the light from the window.
“This is Inspector Flamborough,” Sir Clinton went on, with a gesture of introduction. “We should like to ask you one or two questions about an awkward case we have in our hands—the Hassendean business. I’m afraid it will be painful for you; but I’m sure you’ll give us what help you can.”
Norma Hailsham’s thin lips set in a hard line at his first words, but the movement was apparently involuntary, for she relaxed them again as Sir Clinton finished his remarks.
===Chinese===
弗拉姆伯勒以行家的兴味注意到，郡警察局长借着这份看似漫不经意的礼数，不动声色地把姑娘调度到一个位置上——窗光正清清楚楚照亮她的脸。
“这位是弗拉姆伯勒探长，”克林顿爵士抬手引见，接着往下说。“我们手头有桩棘手的案子——哈森丁那桩事——想问你一两个问题。只怕这会让你难受；但我相信，你能帮的忙你总肯帮。”
头一句话出口，诺玛·海尔沙姆的薄唇便抿成一条硬线，不过这动静看来是无心的，因为克林顿爵士话音落定，她又把嘴唇松开了。

===Original===
“I shall be quite glad to give any help I can,” she said in a level voice.
Flamborough, studying her expression, noticed a swift shift of her glance from one to the other of the three men before her.
“She’s a bit over-selfconscious,” he judged privately. “But she’s the regular look-monger type, anyhow; and quite likely she makes play with her eyes when she’s talking to any man.”
===Chinese===
“凡是我能帮的忙，我都乐意，”她平声说道。
弗拉姆伯勒端详着她的神情，注意到她的目光在面前三个男人身上飞快地挨个扫过。
“她这人有点自我意识过剩，”他私下断定。“不过反正她是惯拿眼色做买卖的那路人；多半她同任何男人说话，都要拿眼睛施展手段。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton seemed to be making a merit of frankness:
“I really haven’t any definite questions I want to ask you, Miss Hailsham,” he confessed. “What we hoped was that you might have something to tell us which indirectly might throw some light on this affair. You see, we come into it without knowing anything about the people involved, and naturally any trifle may help us. Now if I’m not mistaken, you knew Mr. Hassendean fairly well?”
“I was engaged to him at one time. He broke off the engagement for various reasons. That’s common knowledge, I believe.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士似乎有意把坦白标榜成一种美德：
“说实话，我并没有什么明确的问题要问你，海尔沙姆小姐，”他招认道。“我们指望的是，你或许有些话可以告诉我们，间接地照亮这桩案子。你也明白，我们接手时对涉案诸人一无所知，自然，任何鸡毛蒜皮都可能帮上忙。要是我没弄错，你同哈森丁先生相当熟识？”
“我一度同他订过婚。他因为种种缘故解除了婚约。我想这是尽人皆知的。”

===Original===
“Could you give us any of the reasons? I don’t wish to pry, you understand; but I think it’s an important point.”
Miss Hailsham’s face showed that he had touched a sore place.
“He threw me over for another woman—brutally.”
“Mrs. Silverdale?” Sir Clinton inquired.
===Chinese===
“那些缘故，你能告诉我们一些么？你明白，我无意刺探隐私；不过我想那是个要紧的关节。”
海尔沙姆小姐的脸色表明，他戳着了痛处。
“他为了另一个女人甩掉了我——甩得残忍。”
“西尔弗代尔太太？”克林顿爵士问。

===Original===
“Yes, that creature.”
“Ah! Now I’d like to put a blunt question. Was your engagement, while it lasted, a happy one? I mean, of course, before he was attracted to Mrs. Silverdale.”
Norma Hailsham sat with knitted brows for a few moments before answering.
“That’s difficult to answer,” she pointed out at last. “I must confess that I always felt he was thinking more of himself than of me, and it was a disappointment. But, you see, I was very keen on him; and that made a difference, of course.”
===Chinese===
“不错，就是那么个货色。”
“啊！现在我要问一句直话。你们那段婚约，存续期间还算美满么？当然，我是说，在他被西尔弗代尔太太迷上之前。”
诺玛·海尔沙姆蹙着眉坐了片刻，方才作答。
“这不好回答，”她终于指出。“我必须承认，我总觉着他的心思多在自己身上、少在我身上，这让我失望。可是你要知道，我那时候迷他迷得很；这自然就不同了。”

===Original===
“What led to the breaking of your engagement?”
“You mean what led up to it? Well, we were having continual friction over Yvonne Silverdale. He was neglecting me and spending his time with her. Naturally, I spoke to him about it more than once. I wasn’t going to be slighted on account of that woman.”
There was no mistaking the undercurrent of animosity in the girl’s voice in the last sentence. Sir Clinton ignored it.
===Chinese===
“你们的婚约是怎么破裂的？”
“你是问破裂之前的来龙去脉？唉，为了伊冯娜·西尔弗代尔，我们摩擦不断。他冷落我，把工夫都泡在她身上。自然，这事我不止一次同他讲过。为了那么个女人受作践，我可不干。”
姑娘末一句话里那股怨毒的潜流，错认不了。克林顿爵士只作不闻。

===Original===
“What were your ideas about the relations between Mr. Hassendean and Mrs. Silverdale?”
Miss Hailsham’s thin lips curled in undisguised contempt as she heard the question. She made a gesture as though averting herself from something distasteful.
“It’s hardly necessary to enter into *that*, is it?” she demanded. “You can judge for yourself.”
===Chinese===
“依你看，哈森丁先生同西尔弗代尔太太之间，是种什么关系？”
听到这一问题，海尔沙姆小姐的薄唇撇出毫不掩饰的轻蔑。她做了个手势，仿佛要从什么秽物跟前侧过身去。
“*那*一层还用得着细说么？”她质问道。“您自己掂量去吧。”

===Original===
But though she verbally evaded the point, the tone in which she spoke was sufficient to betray her private views on the subject. Then with intense bitterness mingled with a certain malicious joy, she added:
“She got what she deserved in the end. I don’t pretend I’m sorry. I think they were both well served.”
===Chinese===
不过她嘴上虽然避开了这一点，说话的腔调却足以把她在这上头的私见兜了出来。随即，她夹着刻骨的怨毒与几分恶意的快意，又添上一句：
“到头来她得了她应得的。我不装模作样说难过。我看他们两个都报应临头，各得其所。”

===Original===
Then her temper, which hitherto she had kept under control, broke from restraint:
“I don’t care who knows it! They deserved all they got, both of them. What business had she—with a husband of her own—to come and lure him away? She made him break off his engagement to me simply to gratify her own vanity. You don’t expect me to shed tears over them after that? One can forgive a good deal, but there’s no use making a pretence in things like that. She hit me as hard as she could, and I’m glad she’s got her deserts. I warned him at the time that he wouldn’t come off so well as he thought; and he laughed in my face when I said it. Well, it’s my turn to laugh. The account’s even.”
And she actually did laugh, with a catch of hysteria in the laughter. It needed no great skill in psychology to see that wounded pride shared with disappointed passion in causing this outbreak.
===Chinese===
话到此处，她那一直克制着的火性终于挣脱了缰绳：
“谁知道了都随他去！他们落的报应，一分一毫都是应得的，两个都是。她自己有丈夫——凭什么跑来把他勾走？她害得他毁弃了同我的婚约，纯粹是为填她自己的虚荣心。事已至此，你们还指望我为他们抹眼泪？人能容让的也尽够了，这种事上再装模样就没了意思。她往死里整我，如今她遭了报，我高兴。当年我警告过他，说他落不到他自以为的那般好处；我话才出口，他当面就笑。好，这回轮到我笑了。这笔账两清。”
她竟真笑出了声，笑声里噎着一阵歇斯底里的抽搐。不必多大的心理学功夫也看得出：这场发作，一半是受创的傲气，一半是失意的情焰。

===Original===
Sir Clinton checked the hysteria before it gained complete hold over her.
“I’m afraid you haven’t told us anything that was new to us, Miss Hailsham,” he said, frigidly. “This melodramatic business gets us no further forward.”
The girl looked at him with hard eyes.
“What help do you expect from me?” she demanded. “I’m not anxious to see him avenged—far from it.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士趁歇斯底里还没有把她整个攫住，先拦止住了。
“只怕你没告诉我们一件咱们不知道的事，海尔沙姆小姐，”他冷冷地说。“这套戏文唱不出什么进展。”
姑娘拿一双冷硬的眼睛望着他。
“您还指望我帮什么忙？”她质问。“替他报仇，我可不热心——远远说不上。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton evidently realised that nothing was to be gained by pursuing that line of inquiry. Whether the girl had any suspicions or not, she certainly did not intend to supply information which might lead to the capture of the murderer. The Chief Constable waited until she had become calmer before putting his next question:
“Do you happen to know anything about an alkaloid called hyoscine, Miss Hailsham?”
“Hyoscine?” she repeated. “Yes, Avice Deepcar’s working on it just now. She’s been at it for some time under Dr. Silverdale’s direction.”
Flamborough, glancing surreptitiously at Markfield, noted an angry start which the chemist apparently could not suppress. Put on the alert by this, the Inspector reflected that Markfield himself must have had this piece of information, and had refrained from volunteering it.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士显然明白，顺着这条线再问下去捞不到什么。不论这姑娘心里有无怀疑，她断然不打算供出什么可助缉拿凶手的消息。郡警察局长等她平静了些，才提出下一问：
“你可凑巧知道一种叫东莨菪碱的生物碱，海尔沙姆小姐？”
“东莨菪碱？”她重复道。“知道，艾维斯·迪普卡这阵子正摆弄的就是它。她在西尔弗代尔博士手下做这东西有些时候了。”
弗拉姆伯勒斜眼觑着马克菲尔德，只见这位化学师怒得猛一激灵，显然没能压住。探长由此生了警觉，心想这条消息马克菲尔德本人必定早就握着，却一直忍着不肯主动献出来。

===Original===
“I meant as regards its properties,” Sir Clinton interposed. “I’m not an expert in these things like you chemical people.”
“I’m not an alkaloid expert,” Miss Hailsham objected. “All I can remember about it is that it’s used in Twilight Sleep.”
“I believe it is, now that you mention it,” Sir Clinton agreed, politely. “By the way, have you a car, Miss Hailsham?”
===Chinese===
“我指的是它的性质，”克林顿爵士插话道。“这些名堂我不内行，不像你们搞化学的。”
“我也不是生物碱专家，”海尔沙姆小姐抗辩道。“关于它，我记得起来的只有一条：它用在‘朦胧睡眠’（Twilight Sleep）里。”
“经你这一提，我想确是如此，”克林顿爵士客气地附和。“对了，你有汽车么，海尔沙姆小姐？”

===Original===
“Yes. A Morris-Oxford four-seater.”
“A saloon?”
“No, a touring model. Why do you ask?”
===Chinese===
“有。一辆莫里斯-牛津（Morris-Oxford）四座车。”
“轿车型的？”
“不，是敞篷游览型。您问这个做什么？”

===Original===
“Someone’s been asking for information about a car which seems to have knocked a man over on the night of the last fog. You weren’t out that night, I suppose, Miss Hailsham?”
“I was, as it happened. I went out to a dance. But I’d a sore throat; and the fog made it worse; so I came away very early and got home as best I could. But it wasn’t my car that knocked anyone down. I never had an accident in my life.”
===Chinese===
“有人来打听一辆汽车，像是上回起雾那晚撞倒过一个人。那晚你没出门吧，我想，海尔沙姆小姐？”
“偏偏出去了。我去参加一场舞会。可我嗓子疼；雾又一熏，疼得更凶；所以我很早就告辞了，好歹摸回了家。但撞人的绝不是我的车。我这辈子没出过一回事故。”

===Original===
“You might have been excused in that fog, I think, even if you had a collision. But evidently it’s not your car we’re after. What was the number of the car we heard about, Inspector?”
Flamborough consulted his notebook.
===Chinese===
“那样的雾里，我想，即便真撞了车也情有可原。不过咱们要找的显然不是你这辆。咱们听说的那辆车的牌号是多少来着，探长？”
弗拉姆伯勒翻了翻他的笔记簿。

===Original===
“GX.9074, sir.”
“Say that again,” Markfield demanded, pricking up his ears.
“GX.9074 was the number.”
===Chinese===
“GX.9074，先生。”
“再说一遍，”马克菲尔德竖起耳朵要求道。
“GX.9074，就是那个号。”

===Original===
“That’s the number of my car,” Markfield volunteered.
He thought for some time, apparently trying to retrace his experiences in the fog. At last his face lighted up.
“Oh, I guess I know what it is. When I was piloting Dr. Ringwood that night, a fellow nearly walked straight into my front mudguard. I may have hurt his feelings by what I said about his brains, but I swear I didn’t touch him with the car.”
===Chinese===
“那是我的车号，”马克菲尔德主动说明。
他想了半晌，看样子是在雾夜里追溯自己的经历。末了，他的脸豁然一亮。
“哦，我猜我知道是怎么回事了。那晚我给林伍德医生领路，有个家伙差点一头撞进我的前挡泥板。我数落他那副脑子不长用，兴许伤了他的体面，可我发誓，车是万万没沾着他。”

===Original===
“Not our affair,” Sir Clinton hastened to assure him. “It’s a matter for your insurance company if anything comes of it. And I gathered from Dr. Ringwood that you didn’t exactly break records in your trip across town, so I doubt if you need worry.”
“I shan’t,” said Markfield, crossly. “You can refer him to me if he comes to you again.”
“We’d nothing to do with the matter,” Sir Clinton pointed out. “He was told he’d get the owner’s address from the County Council. I expect he got into a calmer frame of mind when he’d had time to think.”
===Chinese===
“不关咱们的事，”克林顿爵士忙不迭向他担保。“真出了什么后果，那是你保险公司的事。况且据我从林伍德医生那里听来，你那趟穿城之行也实在没快到哪里去，所以我看你犯不着担心。”
“我才不担心，”马克菲尔德愠声道。“他若再来找您，您把他推给我就是。”
“这事咱们插不上手，”克林顿爵士指出。“人家告诉他，车主的地址可向郡议会查询。我料他有过工夫细想之后，心绪总归平静下来了。”

===Original===
He turned to Miss Hailsham, who seemed to have recovered complete control over herself during this interlude.
“I think that’s all we need worry you with, Miss Hailsham. I’m sorry that we put you to so much trouble.”
As a sign that the interview was at an end, he moved over to open the door for her.
===Chinese===
他转向海尔沙姆小姐；这一段插话的工夫，她似乎已完全恢复了对自己的克制。
“要烦劳你的就这么多，海尔沙姆小姐。让你耽搁这么半天，抱歉之至。”
为示意问话已毕，他走过去替她开了门。

===Original===
“I certainly don’t wish you success,” she said icily, as she left the room.
“Well, I think that’s all we have to do here, Inspector,” Sir Clinton said as he turned back from the open door. “We mustn’t take up any more of Dr. Markfield’s time. I don’t want to hurry you too much,” he added to Markfield, “but you’ll let us have your official report as soon as you can, won’t you?”
Markfield promised with a nod, and the two officials left the building. When they reached headquarters again, Sir Clinton led the way to his own office.
===Chinese===
“我是断然不祝你们成功的，”她冷冰冰地丢下一句，出了房间。
“好，咱们在这边的事我想就办齐了，探长，”克林顿爵士从敞开的门边转回身来说。“马克菲尔德医生的时间，咱们不能再多占了。我不想催你太紧，”他向马克菲尔德补了一句，“不过正式报告你总要尽早给咱们送来，是吧？”
马克菲尔德点头应承，两位官员离开了那座楼。回到总部之后，克林顿爵士领路进了自己的办公室。

===Original===
“Sit down for a moment or two, Inspector,” he invited. “You may as well glance over the London man’s report when you’re about it. Here it is—not for actual use, of course, until we get the official version from him.”
He passed over a paper which Flamborough unfolded.
“By the way, sir,” the Inspector inquired before beginning to read, “is there any reason for keeping back this information? These infernal reporters are all over me for details; and if this poison affair could be published without doing any harm, I might as well dole it out to them to keep them quiet. They haven’t had much from me in the last twenty-four hours, and it’s better to give them what we can.”
===Chinese===
“坐一两分钟吧，探长，”他招呼道。“趁着手，你不妨把那位伦敦人的报告过一过目。喏，在这儿——当然，在他送来正式文本之前，还不能实际派用场。”
他递过一页纸，弗拉姆伯勒把它展开。
“对了，先生，”探长开读之前问道，“这条消息可有什么压着不发的道理？这帮可恶的记者缠着我不放，净要细节；这桩毒物的事若是公布出去并无妨害，我不如零星打发给他们，图个清静。过去这二十四小时，他们从我这儿没掏到多少东西，能给他们的还是给他们为好。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton seemed to attach some importance to this matter, for he considered it for a few seconds before replying.
“Let them have the name of the stuff,” he directed at last. “I don’t think I’d supply them with any details, though. I’m quite satisfied about the name of the drug, but the dose is still more or less a matter of opinion, and we’d better not say anything about that.”
Flamborough glanced up from the report in his hand.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士似乎颇看重此事，思量了几秒钟才作答。
“毒物的名字可以给他们，”他终于指示道。“不过细节我想就不必供了。药名我完全放心，剂量却多少还在两可之间，那上头咱们最好一字不提。”
弗拉姆伯勒从手里的报告上抬起眼来。

===Original===
“Markfield and the London man both seem to put the dose round about the same figure—eight milligrammes,” he said.
“Both of them must be super-sharp workers,” Sir Clinton pointed out. “I don’t profess to be a chemist, Inspector, but I know enough about things to realise that they’ve done a bit of a feat there. However, let’s get on to something more immediately interesting. What did you make of the Hailsham girl?”
“What did I make of her?” Flamborough repeated, in order to gain a little time. “I thought she was more or less what I’d expected her to be, sir. A hard vixen with a good opinion of herself—and simply mad with rage at being jilted: that’s what I made of her. Revengeful, too. And a bit vulgar, sir. No decent girl would talk like that about a dead man to a set of strangers.”
“She hadn’t much to tell us that was useful,” Sir Clinton said, keeping to the main point. “And I quite agree with you as to the general tone.”
===Chinese===
“马克菲尔德和那位伦敦人，两边的剂量似乎都定在同一个数上下——八毫克，”他说。
“两位想必都是超一流的快手，”克林顿爵士指出。“探长，我不敢自命化学家，但这些事我懂的足以明白：他们这是立了一手绝活。闲话少叙，说点眼下更有趣的吧。那位海尔沙姆姑娘，你作何品评？”
“我作何品评？”弗拉姆伯勒重复了一遍，好赢得一点时间。“我觉得她大体不出所料，先生。一个心肠硬、自我感觉又好的泼妇——叫人甩了，气得简直发疯：这就是我的品评。还记仇。而且有点下作，先生。哪个正派姑娘也不会对着一群生人那样谈论一个死者。”
“她没有什么有用的话好讲，”克林顿爵士紧扣正题说。“至于那股总的腔调，我跟你所见略同。”

===Original===
Flamborough turned to a matter which had puzzled him during their visit to the Institute:
“What did you want young Hassendean’s notebook for, sir? I didn’t quite make that out.”
“Why, you saw what I got out of it: arithmetical errors which proved conclusively that he was a careless worker who didn’t take any trouble at all to verify his results.”
===Chinese===
弗拉姆伯勒转到一个在研究所时就让他纳闷的问题上：
“您要小哈森丁的笔记簿做什么用，先生？这个我没大弄明白。”
“嗐，你看见我从里头得了什么了：算术上的错误，板上钉钉地证明他是个马虎的干活人，验证自己的结果一概不肯费半点事。”

===Original===
“I had a kind of notion that you got more out of it than that, sir, or you wouldn’t have asked to see Markfield’s notebook as well. It doesn’t take someone else’s notebook to spot slips in a man’s arithmetic, surely.”
Sir Clinton gazed blandly at his subordinate:
“Now that you’ve got that length, it would be a pity to spoil your pleasure in the rest of the inference. Just think it out and tell me the result, to see if we both reach the same conclusion independently. You’ll find a weights-and-measures conversion table useful.”
===Chinese===
“我隐约觉着您从中所得不止这些，先生，要不然您也不会再要看马克菲尔德的笔记簿了。要看出一个人算术上的差错，总不至于非得再借旁人的一本簿子吧。”
克林顿爵士温然地望着他的下属：
“你既已走到这一步，余下的推断再替你点破，就忍心夺了你的乐趣了。只管想下去，把结果告诉我，看咱们俩各自独立推演，是否殊途同归。你会发觉一张度量衡换算表颇有用处。”

===Original===
“Conversion table, sir?” asked the Inspector, evidently quite at sea.
“Yes. ‘One metre equals 39.37 inches,’ and all that sort of thing. The sort of stuff one used at school, you know.”
“Too deep for me, sir,” the Inspector acknowledged ruefully. “You’ll need to tell me the answer. And that reminds me, what made you ask whether the dose could have been fifteen times the maximum?”
===Chinese===
“换算表，先生？”探长问道，显然一头雾水。
“对。‘一米等于 39.37 英寸’之类。就是当年上学时用的那一套，你晓得。”
“太深奥了，我啃不动，先生，”探长懊恼地认了。“答案还得您来揭晓。这倒提醒我了，您方才问剂量会不会达到极量的十五倍，那是何缘故？”

===Original===
