“Yes, sir. It was huddled up anyhow in the ditch, just as it might have fallen if it had been dropped in with a thud.”

“Single-handed business, then, you believe?”

“Well, sir, I think if two people had been handling him—one taking his shoulders and another taking his feet—he’d have fallen more tidily. He certainly looked as if he’s been bundled in anyhow. I’d put it down as a single-handed job from the look of it.”

===Chinese===
“从尸体的样子看，您认为它是被扔进去的？”

“是的，先生。尸体在沟里胡乱蜷作一团，正像咣当一声丢进去时可能摔成的那副样子。”

“那么，您认为这是单人独手的勾当？”

“唔，先生，我想若是两个人搬弄他——一个架肩膀、一个抬脚——他躺下去该齐整得多。他那样子分明是被人不管不顾硬塞进去的。单凭这副样子，我便断定是单人独力的活计。”

===Original===
“I suppose you examined the pockets, and so forth?” Sir Clinton asked.

“Of course, sir. But there was nothing in *them* of any use to us.”

The Inspector’s voice betrayed that he had something still in reserve. Now he brought it forward.

“I examined his hands, sir; and in the right one, I found something important. The hand was clenched, and when I got it open at last, this fell out.”

===Chinese===
“口袋等等，您总查验过了吧？”克林顿爵士问。

“当然，先生。可*它们*里头没有一件东西对咱们有用。”

探长的话音里透出，他还留着一手没亮。这时他亮了出来。

“我查验了他的双手，先生；在右手里，我发现了要紧的东西。那只手攥得死紧，等我好歹掰开了，这东西掉了出来。”

===Original===
He produced a button with a shred of cloth attached to it, which he laid on the desk before Sir Clinton. The Chief Constable picked it up, examined it closely, and then, pulling out a pocket magnifying glass, made a still more minute inspection.

“Very interesting, Inspector. What do you make of it?”

“Obviously it was torn off the murderer’s clothes during the struggle, sir. And I’ve seen something like it before. You see that canary-coloured stain on the bit of cloth and also on the threads that hold the button to the fabric?”

“Dyed with picric acid, by the look of it, I should say. Is that what you mean?”

===Chinese===
他取出一枚纽扣，上头连着一小条布，把它摆在克林顿爵士面前的桌上。郡警察局长拾起来凑近细看，随后掏出一柄随身放大镜，愈发精细地查验起来。

“很有意思，探长。您从中看出什么名堂？”

“这分明是搏斗时从凶手衣裳上撕下来的，先生。这样的东西我从前见过。您瞧见那布片上、还有纽扣连着布料的那几根线上，那金丝雀黄的渍子没有？”

“照这光景，我该说是苦味酸染的。您指的是这个么？”

===Original===
“Yes, sir. And the pattern of the cloth’s another point.”

“You mean it looks like a button torn off the old jacket that Silverdale was wearing, that day we saw him at the Croft-Thornton Institute—his laboratory coat?”

“That’s undoubtedly what it is, sir. I remember that stain perfectly. And as soon as I saw it, I remembered the pattern of the cloth.”

“And your view is?”

===Chinese===
“正是，先生。还有布料的花样，又是一层。”

“您是说，它像是从西尔弗代尔那件旧褂子上扯落的纽扣——咱们在克罗夫特-索顿研究所见他那天他穿着的那件——他的实验室工作服？”

“毫无疑问正是它，先生。那块渍子我记得真真切切。一看见它，我就想起了布料的花样。”

“那么您的见解是？”

===Original===
“I think that when Silverdale set out to murder Whalley he was afraid that some blood from the face might get on to his coat. So he put on his old laboratory jacket. If it got spotted, he could destroy it and rouse no suspicions. It was only an old coat that he might think was worn out. Quite a different thing from destroying some of his ordinary clothes. That would have been suspicious. But an old coat—no one would wonder if he got rid of it and brought another one down to the laboratory to replace it.”

“It sounds deuced plausible, Inspector, I must admit. But—”

“But what, sir?”

“Well,” Sir Clinton answered thoughtfully, “it leaves us again with the choice between the single and the double bluff, you see, even if one goes no further with one’s inquiries.”

===Chinese===
“我想，西尔弗代尔动身去杀沃利的时候，生怕脸上溅出的血会沾上衣服。于是他穿上了那件旧的实验室工作服。若是沾了血点，销毁它也不致引人起疑。那不过是件旧褂子，他尽可以当作穿残了的。同销毁几件日常衣裳完全是两码事。那样就要招疑了。可一件旧褂子——他处理掉它、另带一件到实验室补上，谁也不会多想。”

“听起来着实可信得很，探长，我不得不承认。不过——”

“不过什么，先生？”

“这个嘛，”克林顿爵士若有所思地答道，“您瞧，这就又把咱们撂回到那道岔路口上了：单层诈术，还是双层诈术——哪怕侦查到此为止，不再往前。”

===Original===
The Inspector pondered over the point for a few seconds, but at the end of his cogitation he seemed unimpressed. Apparently, however, he thought it wise to change the subject.

“In any case, sir, I think Whalley’s part in the bungalow affair is pretty plain now. I told you he was the sort of fellow who was out for easy money, no matter how dirty it might be. By the way, he was the man who inquired about the number of that motor which he said knocked him spinning—an obvious try-on to get damages, although he wasn’t hurt at all. You can see he’d do anything to make money and save himself from honest work. If you remember that, it’s easy enough to see the part he played at the bungalow. He was the person you christened Peeping Tom.”

“Anything further about him that you can think of, Inspector? I don’t say you’re wrong, of course.”

===Chinese===
探长就这一点琢磨了几秒钟，可这一番苦思到头，他似乎不为所动。不过看样子，他也觉得换个话题才是上策。

“无论如何，先生，我想沃利在平房一案里扮演的角色，如今算是相当明白了。我说过，他是那种一心捞轻省钱的人，多脏也不在乎。顺便提一句，打听那辆据他说把他撞了个跟头的汽车牌号的，正是此君——明摆着是讹赔偿金的把戏，其实他压根儿没受一点伤。您看得出，为了弄钱、也为了躲开正经营生，他什么都干得出来。记着这一层，他在平房里充当的角色就容易看明白了。他就是您赐过诨号的那位‘偷窥的汤姆’。”

“关于他，您还能想到些别的吗，探长？当然，我并不是说您错了。”

===Original===
“Well, sir, if Silverdale expected to take his wife in flagrante delicto, he’d need an independent witness, wouldn’t he? Possibly Whalley was the man he picked out for the work.”

“Do you think he was the sort of witness that was wanted? I’m not so sure of his suitability myself.”

“It wasn’t exactly a nice job, sir,” the Inspector pointed out. “Silverdale would hardly care to take one of his close friends to inspect an affair of that sort. And of course a woman—”

He broke off suddenly, as though struck by a fresh idea. Sir Clinton ignored the last phrase of the Inspector.

“Assume that Whalley was the witness, then, what next?”

===Chinese===
“唔，先生，倘若西尔弗代尔指望当场拿获妻子的奸情，他就需要一名独立的证人，对不对？沃利或许就是他挑来干这差事的人。”

“您觉得他是所需的那号证人吗？依我看，他合不合适，我可说不准。”

“这差事到底不怎么体面，先生，”探长指出。“西尔弗代尔总不至于带上哪位至交好友，去旁观那样一桩事。再说，女人当然——”

他猛地收住话头，仿佛被个新念头打中了。克林顿爵士对探长的末半句只当没听见。

“姑且假定沃利就是那名证人，那么往下呢？”

===Original===
“Assume that Silverdale posted Whalley at the second window and went round to the first one—at the front. Then, to make the thing complete, he breaks in through the window and jumps into the room. Young Hassendean has his pistol and mistakes the state of affairs—thinks that Silverdale means to thrash him or worse. He pulls out his pistol and there’s a struggle for the possession of it. The pistol goes off accidentally, and the bullet hits Mrs. Silverdale in the head by pure chance. Then the struggle goes on, and in the course of it, young Hassendean gets shot twice over in the lung.”

===Chinese===
“再假定西尔弗代尔把沃利安在第二扇窗下，自己绕到第一扇——正面那扇。随后，为了把戏做全，他破窗而入，跳进房里。小哈森丁身上带着手枪，又错认了眼前的局势——以为西尔弗代尔要揍他，或者更下狠手。他拔出枪来，两人便争夺起来。枪走了火，那颗子弹纯属碰巧，正中西尔弗代尔太太的头部。随后争夺继续，在这过程中，小哈森丁肺部接连中了两枪。”

===Original===
The Chief Constable looked at his subordinate with quite unaffected respect.

“It looks as if you’d come very near the truth there,” he admitted. “Go on.”

“The rest’s fairly obvious, if you grant what’s gone before. Whalley’s seen the whole affair from his post at the window. He sneaks off into the dark and gets out of Silverdale’s reach. If he hadn’t, then Silverdale would probably have shot him at sight to destroy the chance of evidence against him. But when Whalley has time to think things over, he sees he’s got a goldmine in the business. If he can blackmail Silverdale, he’s got a steady income for life. But I expect he weakened and tried to play for safety. He blackmailed Silverdale; then he came to us, so that he could say he’d been to the police, meaning to give information. Then he went back to Silverdale, and in some way he let out that he’d given us a call. That would be enough for Silverdale. Whalley would have to go the way the maid went. And so he did.”

===Chinese===
郡警察局长望着他的下属，眼中是全然不加掩饰的敬意。

“看来您那儿已经非常贴近真相了，”他承认道。“接着讲。”

“往下的事相当明显，只要您认可了前面的假设。沃利从窗口的岗位上目击了全部经过。他溜进黑暗里，脱出了西尔弗代尔的手掌心。若非如此，西尔弗代尔多半会一见他就开枪，好铲除对他不利的证源。可待沃利得空把前前后后掂量掂量，他看出这桩买卖里有座金矿好挖。倘若能讹诈西尔弗代尔，他便一辈子有了一份安稳进项。但我料他后来心虚了，想两头上保险。他讹了西尔弗代尔；随后到咱们这儿来了一趟，好教他日后说得出口：自己进过警察局，本是有心检举的。然后他回到西尔弗代尔跟前，不知怎么一来，漏出风声说他来找过咱们。这在西尔弗代尔看来就尽够了。沃利便只好走上女仆走过的那条路。他也果真走了。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton had listened intently to the Inspector’s reconstruction of the episode.

“That’s very neat indeed, Inspector,” he adjudged at the close. “It’s quite sound, so far as it goes, and so far as one can see. But, of course, it leaves one or two points untouched. Where does the murder of the maid come into the business?”

Flamborough reflected for a moment or two before answering.

“I’m not prepared to fill that gap just at this moment, sir. But I’ll suggest something. Renard told us that Mrs. Silverdale was going to draw up a note of the terms of her new will. It’s on the cards that Silverdale knew about that—she may have mentioned it to him. He’d want to get that note and destroy it at any cost, before there was any search of his house or any hunting through Mrs. Silverdale’s possessions.”

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士全神贯注地听完了探长对这段情节的重构。

“确实干净利落之至，探长，”他在收尾时评断道。“就事论事、就目力所及，它站得住脚。不过，它当然也留下一两点没碰到的空白。女仆被杀一事，在这桩买卖里占个什么位置呢？”

弗拉姆伯勒想了片刻，才开口作答。

“眼下方才这个缺口，我还不想就填补，先生。不过我可以提个由头。勒纳尔告诉我们，西尔弗代尔太太正打算把新遗嘱的条款拟成一纸字据。西尔弗代尔很可能知道这事——她或许向他提起过。他会不惜代价抢先把那纸东西弄到手毁掉，赶在任何搜查他宅子、翻检西尔弗代尔太太遗物的举动之前。”

===Original===
“He might have thought it worth while, I admit. But I’d hardly think it important enough to lead to an unnecessary murder. Besides, it wasn’t necessary for Silverdale to murder the maid at all. It was his own house. He could search where he chose in it and nobody could object. The maid wouldn’t see anything strange in that.”

“It was pretty clear that the maid knew her murderer, anyhow,” the Inspector pointed out. “Everything points to that. I admit I’m only making a guess, sir. I can’t bring any evidence against Silverdale on that count yet. For all one can tell, she may have seen something—blood on his coat from the shots, or something of that sort. Then he’d have to silence her.”

Sir Clinton made no comment on the Inspector’s suggestion. Instead, he turned to a fresh aspect of the case.

===Chinese===
“他或许觉得值得干一场，我承认。可要说这事当紧到非添一桩没必要的凶杀不可，我不这么看。况且，西尔弗代尔根本犯不着去杀那女仆。那是他自家的宅子。他爱在哪儿翻就在哪儿翻，谁也拦不得。女仆瞧见了也断不会觉得有什么蹊跷。”

“无论如何，那女仆认得杀她的人，这一点是相当清楚的，”探长指出。“种种迹象都指向这一层。我承认我不过是在瞎猜，先生。在这一条上，我眼下还提不出对西尔弗代尔不利的证据。据咱们所知，她或许撞见了什么——枪响后溅在他衣服上的血，或这一类。那他就不得不封她的口了。”

克林顿爵士对探长的猜测不置一词，转而面向案情的另一个方面。

===Original===
“And where does Mr. Justice come into your theory of the affair? He wasn’t your friend Whalley. That’s evident.”

The Inspector rubbed his nose thoughtfully, as though trying to gain inspiration from the friction.

“It’s a fact, sir, that I can’t fit Mr. Justice into my theory at present. He wasn’t Whalley, and that’s a fact. But hold on a moment! Suppose that Whalley wasn’t Silverdale’s witness at all. Come to think of it, Whalley was hardly the sort that one would pick out for the job, if one had been in Silverdale’s shoes.”

===Chinese===
“还有，那位‘正义’在您这套理论里占个什么位置？他总不是您那位朋友沃利。这是明摆着的。”

探长若有所思地揉着鼻子，仿佛想从这点摩擦上蹭出些灵感来。

“实情是，先生，眼下的确没法把‘正义’安插进我的理论。他不是沃利，这是实情。可是且慢！假定沃利压根儿就不是西尔弗代尔的证人。细想起来，倘若换了旁人处在西尔弗代尔的境地，也难挑中沃利这号人来当这份差。”

===Original===
“I’m quite convinced of that, at any rate, Inspector. You needn’t waste breath in persuading me.”

“Yes, but there’s another possibility that’s been overlooked, sir,” Flamborough interrupted eagerly. “I’ve been assuming all along that Silverdale was the only person at the opened window. But suppose he’d brought someone along with him. Both of them might have been looking through the front window, whilst Whalley was at the side window, quite unknown to them at the time.”

“Now you’re getting positively brilliant, Inspector,” Sir Clinton commended. “I think you’ve got at least half the truth there, beyond a doubt.”

===Chinese===
“至少这一点我是完全信服的，探长。您不必白费口舌来说服我。”

“是，不过还有一层被大家忽略的可能，先生，”弗拉姆伯勒急煎煎地插话道。“我一直想当然，以为敞开的那扇窗前只有西尔弗代尔一个人。可假定他还带了个人同去呢。两个人说不定都在前窗朝里张望，而沃利守在侧窗，他们当时竟全然不知。”

“这下您可真是灵光四射了，探长，”克林顿爵士称赞道。“我想您至少捞着了半截真相，这毫无疑义。”

===Original===
“Who could Silverdale’s witness have been?” the Inspector pursued, as if impatient of the interruption. “What about the Deepcar girl?”

“Think again,” Sir Clinton advised him drily. “Do you really suppose that Silverdale—who seems in love with the girl—would have picked her out for business of that sort? It’s incredible, Inspector.”

The first flush of enthusiasm at his discovery passed from Flamborough’s thoughts at the tone of the Chief Constable’s voice.

“I suppose you’re right, sir,” he had to admit. “But there’s another girl who’d have enjoyed the job—and that’s the Hailsham girl. She’d have given a good deal just for the pleasure of seeing those two humiliated. She’d have gloated over the chance of giving that particular evidence in court and squaring accounts with young Hassendean and Mrs. Silverdale. It would have been all jam to her, sir. You can’t deny that.”

===Chinese===
“那么西尔弗代尔的证人可能是谁呢？”探长追问，仿佛嫌方才那阵打岔搅了他的思路。“迪普卡那姑娘如何？”

“再想想吧，”克林顿爵士干巴巴地进言道。“您当真以为，西尔弗代尔——瞧那样子正爱着这姑娘——会挑她去办那路差事？难以置信，探长。”

听了郡警察局长这话音，弗拉姆伯勒心头因这一发现而腾起的那阵热乎劲儿，便退了下去。

“想必您是对的，先生，”他不得不承认。“可还有一个姑娘，干这差事她准会乐在其中——那就是海尔沙姆那姑娘。单为瞧那两个人受辱的快活，让她出多大的代价她都肯。若能有机会上法庭作那一番证词，同小哈森丁和西尔弗代尔太太算清旧账，她准会幸灾乐祸地咂摸个够。这对她简直是满盘皆甜，先生。这一点您没法否认。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton conceded the point without ado.

“I won’t deny it,” he said curtly. “But you needn’t let your mind run exclusively on the female population of Westerhaven in a matter of this sort. A man would be a much more convenient witness for Silverdale to take with him. Why leave Silverdale’s male friends out of account?”

“If you’re thinking of Markfield, sir, we’ll not get much out of him, I’m afraid,” Flamborough pronounced. “So far, except when he couldn’t help it, he’s done his level best to refuse any information about Silverdale and his doings—if he hasn’t actually served out misleading statements to us. I don’t much care for Dr. Markfield’s way of going about things.”

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士二话不说便认下了这一点。

“我不否认，”他简短地说。“不过在这类事情上，您犯不着把心思净绕着韦斯特黑文的女性居民打转。对西尔弗代尔来说，带一个男人同去做证人，要便利得多。为什么把西尔弗代尔的男性朋友们撇在账外呢？”

“您若是想着马克菲尔德，先生，恐怕从他嘴里问不出多少东西，”弗拉姆伯勒断言道。“直到如今，除了万不得已，凡是涉及西尔弗代尔及其行止的问题，他一概竭力拒答——若不是干脆递给咱们些指东打西的说法的话。马克菲尔德医生这路行事的做派，我不怎么欣赏。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton crossed the room and took down his hat from its peg.

“Well, let’s sample his methods once more, Inspector. We’ll go round now to the Croft-Thornton and look into the question of the jacket. You can bear the burden of the interview, if you like; but I should prefer to hear what goes on. And you might press Silverdale a little more sharply about his doings on the night of the bungalow affair. We may as well give him a chance of second thoughts, though really I don’t expect anything from him at this stage.”

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士穿过房间，从衣帽钩上取下帽子。

“好吧，咱们再去领教一回他这套路数，探长。这就绕到克罗夫特-索顿去，把那件工作服的问题查一查。您乐意的话，会见的担子由您来挑；不过我宁可亲耳听听是怎么个情形。您也不妨就平房一案当晚他的行踪，把西尔弗代尔挤兑得再紧一些。给他一个三思改口的机会也好——虽说到了这一步，我实在不指望从他身上得出什么。”

## XIV The Jacket / 十四 工作服

===Original===
Sir Clinton and the Inspector found Markfield at work in his laboratory when they reached the Croft-Thornton Institute. Flamborough wasted no time in preliminaries, but plunged at once into the business which had brought him there.

“What do you make of that, Dr. Markfield?” he demanded, producing the shred of cloth with the button attached and showing them to the chemist.

Markfield examined the object carefully, but his face showed only a certain bewilderment when he looked up at the Inspector again.

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士和探长抵达克罗夫特-索顿研究所时，发现马克菲尔德正在实验室里工作。弗拉姆伯勒在开场客套上一秒也不肯耽搁，开门见山便切入了他此行的正题。

“这个您怎么看，马克菲尔德医生？”他问道，取出那块连着纽扣的碎布，拿给化学家过目。

马克菲尔德把那东西仔细察看了一番，可再抬眼望向探长时，脸上只有几分困惑。

===Original===
“It seems to be a button and a bit of cloth with a picric acid stain on it,” he pointed out with a tinge of irony. “Do you want me to make an expert examination of it? If so, you’d better tell me some more about it, so that I’ll know what you want with it.”

Flamborough stared at him for a moment or two, as though trying to read something in his expression, but Markfield seemed in no way put out.

“I’m not a mind-reader, Inspector,” he pointed out. “You’ll need to explain clearly what you expect me to do; and I’ll have to be told whether I can cut bits out of your specimen for chemical analysis.”

===Chinese===
“这看来是一枚纽扣加一小块布，上头染着苦味酸的污渍，”他略带讥诮地指出。“您是要我给它做个专家鉴定？若是这样，您最好再多给我讲些情况，我也好明白您要拿它做什么文章。”

弗拉姆伯勒盯了他一两眼，仿佛想从他神情里读出些什么，马克菲尔德却似乎毫不为意。

“我可不会读心术，探长，”他指出。“您得把要我做的事交代明白；还有，能不能从您的证物上剪几小块下来做化学分析，也得先给我个话。”

===Original===
Flamborough saw that his attempt to draw Markfield was not going to be so easy as he had hoped.

“Have a good look at the thing first of all,” he suggested. “Can you remember anything like it?”

Markfield stolidly examined the object once more.

===Chinese===
弗拉姆伯勒看出，想从马克菲尔德身上套出话来，不会像他指望的那样容易。

“先把这东西好好端详端详，”他提议道。“您能记起什么同类的东西么？”

马克菲尔德又木然把那东西检视了一遍。

===Original===
“It’s a button and a piece of cloth,” he said at last. “Of course I’ve seen buttons before, and bits of cloth are not uncommon. I should think that this stain is a picric acid one, but that’s a matter for further examination before I could say anything definite. Is that what you wanted?”

Flamborough kept his temper with difficulty.

“What I want to know, Dr. Markfield, is whether you have recently seen anything that you could associate with that thing—any garment from which it might have been torn, or anything of that sort.”

===Chinese===
“这是一枚纽扣和一块布，”他终于开口。“纽扣我从前自然见过，布头也不算稀罕。依我看，这污渍是苦味酸的；不过那得进一步检验，我才好说出定论。您要问的就是这个么？”

弗拉姆伯勒费了好大的劲才按住性子。

“我想知道的是，马克菲尔德医生，您近来可曾见过什么能同这东西对上号的物件——比如它可能是从哪件衣裳上撕落的，或诸如此类的情况。”

===Original===
Markfield’s eyes narrowed and he glanced with obvious unfriendliness at the Inspector.

“It’s a coat-button, by the look of it. I’m no specialist in buttons, I admit. It might have come off any lounge suit, so far as I can see.”

“I’d advise you not to fence with us too long, Dr. Markfield,” Flamborough suggested. “Look at the cloth. Does that remind you of anything that’s familiar to you?”

===Chinese===
马克菲尔德眯起眼睛，朝探长投去一瞥，那不友善再明显不过。

“看样子，这是枚外衣纽扣。我承认，纽扣这一门我不是专家。依我看，随便哪一套便装西服上都可能掉下这么一枚来。”

“我劝你别跟咱们支吾周旋太久，马克菲尔德医生，”弗拉姆伯勒提点道。“看看这块布。它能让你想起什么眼熟的东西么？”

===Original===
Markfield’s face betrayed his obvious annoyance.

“I suppose you’ve identified it already for yourselves. Why come to me? Presumably you mean that it’s a bit torn off Dr. Silverdale’s laboratory coat. Well, I can’t swear to that. It may be, for all I know. Why not compare it with the coat, and if the coat’s torn, you’ve got your evidence, whatever it may be. I don’t see why you drag me into the thing at all.”

Flamborough’s voice grew hard as he answered:

===Chinese===
马克菲尔德的脸上泄露了再明白不过的愠怒。

“想必你们自己早已认出它的来历了。何必来找我？你们的意思，八成是说这是从西尔弗代尔医生实验室工作服上撕下来的一角。哼，这个我可不敢具结。据我所知，也许是，也许不是。何不拿它去同那件工作服比对——衣服上要是撕了口子，你们的证据便到手了，管它是什么性质的证据。我不明白，你们凭什么非要把我拖进这桩事里来。”

弗拉姆伯勒答话时，嗓音变得硬了起来：

===Original===
“There’s one thing I want you to bear in mind, Dr. Markfield. A man may very easily become an accessory after the fact in a murder case; and the penalty runs as high as penal servitude for life. I’m not at all satisfied with the way in which you seem to have determined to evade some of the questions I’ve had to put to you; and I’d like to remind you that you may be running risks. It would be far better if you’d deal frankly with us instead of shuffling.”

The covert threat seemed to have its effect on Markfield. He looked sulky, but he appeared to make up his mind to alter his tactics.

“Well, ask your questions, then,” he snapped. “But put them on matters of fact. I’m not going to say what I *think* about this and what I *suppose* about that. I’ll tell you anything that I *know* definitely, if you ask about it.”

===Chinese===
“有一件事我要你放在心上，马克菲尔德医生。在谋杀案里，一个人是极容易变成事后从犯的；而刑罚最重可至终身苦役。你像是铁了心要回避我不得不向你提出的一些问题，对这副做派我殊为不满；我也要提醒你，你这是在冒险。与其这样闪烁其词，你若肯同我们坦率打交道，情形会好得多。”

这番暗藏的威吓似乎对马克菲尔德起了作用。他面带愠色，但看样子已拿定主意变换策略。

“好吧，那您就发问，”他没好气地道。“但只许问事实。我不打算说我对这桩*想*些什么、对那桩又*猜*些什么。凡我确切*知道*的事，只消您问到，我都可以奉告。”

===Original===
Flamborough wasted no time before taking up the challenge.

“Very good, Dr. Markfield. We’ll stick to facts, if you like. Now once upon a time you saw Dr. Silverdale acting in some private theatricals, I believe. I learned that from Dr. Ringwood. That’s correct, isn’t it?”

“Yes. We were members of a small amateur show at one time.”

===Chinese===
弗拉姆伯勒立即应下这番挑战，毫不拖延。

“很好，马克菲尔德医生。你若愿意，咱们就只谈事实。话说当年，你曾见过西尔弗代尔医生参加一场业余戏剧演出，我想没错。这是我从林伍德医生那里听来的。是这么回事吧？”

“是。有一阵子，我们同是一个小型业余剧社的成员。”

===Original===
“In any of his parts, did Dr. Silverdale play the banjo?”

Markfield reflected for a moment.

“I think he did.”

===Chinese===
“在他担任的那些角色里，西尔弗代尔医生弹过班卓琴么？”

马克菲尔德思忖了片刻。

“我想他弹过。”

===Original===
“He’s an expert banjo-player?”

“He plays the banjo,” Markfield corrected. “I’m not going to give you my opinion about his playing. That’s not a question of fact; it’s a mere matter of taste.”

Flamborough let this pass without comment.

===Chinese===
“他是个班卓琴高手？”

“他弹班卓琴，”马克菲尔德纠正道。“他的琴艺如何，我不打算发表意见。那不是事实问题，不过是口味问题罢了。”

弗拉姆伯勒对这句话未加评论，径直放了过去。

===Original===
“He plays the banjo, anyhow. That’s what I want to get at.”

He stepped across the laboratory to where a little glass apparatus was attached to a tap at a sink and examined the rubber tubing attached.

“What’s this thing here?” he demanded.

===Chinese===
“反正他弹班卓琴。我要查的就是这一条。”

他穿过实验室，走到水池边一件接在龙头上的小玻璃器具跟前，察看连在上面的橡皮管。

“这儿的这件东西是什么？”他问道。

===Original===
“A water-pump,” Markfield answered, as though not quite following the Inspector’s train of thought.

“And this rubber tubing, what sort of stuff is it?”

“Pressure-tubing. What about it?”

===Chinese===
“水泵，”马克菲尔德答道，像是没能完全跟上探长的思路。

“那这橡皮管呢，是种什么材料？”

“压力橡皮管。怎么了？”

===Original===
“Does Dr. Silverdale use anything of that sort?”

“Everybody in the place uses it. Whenever one wants quick filtering one uses a water-pump with pressure-tubing connections.”

“Miss Deepcar and Miss Hailsham use it, then?”

===Chinese===
“西尔弗代尔医生用这类东西么？”

“这地方人人都在用。谁要想过滤快些，就用泵接上压力橡皮管。”

“那么迪普卡小姐和海尔沙姆小姐也用喽？”

===Original===
“I should think there are a dozen or two of these pumps in this department alone. They’re ordinary fittings in every chemical laboratory. If I may ask, Inspector, what are you getting at?”

Flamborough switched off to a fresh line without making any direct reply.

“Is Miss Deepcar here today?”

===Chinese===
“光我们这一个部里，我估摸这种泵就有一二十台。凡属化学实验室，这都是寻常的配置。恕我冒昧，探长，您这到底想问向何处？”

弗拉姆伯勒不作正面回答，把话锋一转，又换了条新线。

“迪普卡小姐今天在这儿么？”

===Original===
“I don’t think so. I believe she’s out of town—been away for a couple of days. I’ll send a message to find out definitely if you want to know.”

Flamborough shook his head.

“Don’t trouble. I can find out for myself.”

===Chinese===
“我看不在。我记得她出城去了——已经走了两天了。您若想问个准信，我可以派人去打听明白。”

弗拉姆伯勒摇了摇头。

“不必费心。我自己能打听。”

===Original===
“I heard that she would be back the day after tomorrow,” Markfield volunteered. “But you’d better find out for yourself of course.”

Again the Inspector turned to a fresh line.

“Do you know anything about a man Whalley—Peter Whalley?” he demanded.

===Chinese===
“我听说她后天回来，”马克菲尔德主动补了一句。“不过，您当然还是自己去问的好。”

探长又转向一条新的问话路线。

“你可知道一个姓沃利的人——彼得·沃利？”他问道。

===Original===
“Whalley?” Markfield repeated as though trying to recall the name. “Whalley? Oh, yes. He came to me with some story about having been hit by my car on a foggy night. I didn’t believe him. I knew I’d hurt no one with the car, though once I came near it that night. Mr. Whalley got no change out of me.”

“He didn’t go any further in the matter, then?”

“I heard no more about it. The thing was so obviously a try-on that I didn’t even advise my insurance company about it.”

===Chinese===
“沃利？”马克菲尔德重复了一遍，像是努力回想这个名字。“沃利？哦，有的。他来找过我，编排了一套话，说某个雾夜里叫我的汽车撞了。我没信他。我心里有数，那晚我的车没伤着任何人，虽说有一回也险些出事。沃利先生在我这里没讨着半点便宜。”

“那么，他后来没有在这件事上继续纠缠？”

“我再没听人提起过。那事明摆着是讹钱的把戏，我连自己的保险公司都没知会。”

===Original===
Flamborough reflected for a few moments, obviously trying to think of fresh questions which he could put; but apparently he had come to the end of his stock.

“We’ll go along to Dr. Silverdale’s room,” he said, leading the way to the door. “You had better come with us, Dr. Markfield. You’ll do as a witness, perhaps.”

“I’m not very keen,” Markfield retorted grumblingly.

===Chinese===
弗拉姆伯勒沉吟了片刻，显然在搜肠刮肚地想些新问题；但看样子，他的存货已经告罄。

“咱们这就上西尔弗代尔医生的房间去，”他说着，领头朝门口走去。“你最好跟我们一道来，马克菲尔德医生。兴许能充个见证人。”

“我可不大乐意，”马克菲尔德嘟嘟囔囔地回了一句。

===Original===
However, he followed Sir Clinton and the Inspector along the corridors to Silverdale’s laboratory. The room was empty, but the door was unlocked and the Inspector opened it and stepped inside. A glance round the place revealed Silverdale’s laboratory jacket hanging on a peg; and Flamborough went over and took it down.

“Now we’ll see,” he said, laying it on the table and spreading it out for examination. “Ah, I thought there was no mistake.”

He pointed to the right-hand side, where it was obvious that one of the buttons had been wrenched away, taking a piece of the cloth with it.

===Chinese===
不过，他还是跟着克林顿爵士和探长沿走廊来到西尔弗代尔的实验室。屋里空无一人，门却没有上锁，探长推门走了进去。环视一周，便见西尔弗代尔的工作服挂在衣钩上；弗拉姆伯勒走过去，把它取了下来。

“这下见分晓，”他说着把它放到桌上，铺展开来察看。“啊，我说什么来着，错不了。”

他指向右手边一侧——那里明摆着有一枚纽扣被硬生生拧落，还带走了一块布。

===Original===
“Now we’ll see if it fits,” Flamborough continued, producing the fragment of fabric found in Whalley’s hand and adjusting it to the tear in the coat. “That’s clear enough. You see now the stains on the two bits correspond exactly.”

Markfield leaned over and satisfied himself that the Inspector’s statement was accurate.

“What is this bit of cloth?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“现在来试试合不合得上，”弗拉姆伯勒接着说，取出在沃利手中发现的那块织物碎片，往衣服的破口上比对。“这就再清楚不过了。你们瞧，两块布上的污渍恰好一一对应。”

马克菲尔德俯身验看，确认探长所言不虚。

“这块布是怎么回事？”他问。

===Original===
Flamborough, however, had found something further, and Markfield got no answer to his question.

“Look there,” the Inspector ejaculated, indicating a small brownish stain on the breast of the jacket. “That’s blood, clear enough.”

Markfield seemed about to repeat his demand for information when steps sounded in the corridor outside. Flamborough picked up the coat, moved swiftly across the room, and hung the garment on its original peg. As he turned away unconcernedly from the spot, the door opened and Silverdale entered the laboratory. He seemed taken aback by the presence of the police and looked from one to another in the group without speaking. Then he came forward.

===Chinese===
然而弗拉姆伯勒又有了新的发现，马克菲尔德的问题没有得到回答。

“瞧那儿，”探长脱口喊道，指着工作服胸襟上一小块发褐的污渍。“那是血，再清楚不过。”

马克菲尔德正要再追问一遍，外面走廊里响起了脚步声。弗拉姆伯勒抄起衣服，疾步穿过房间，把那件衣裳挂回原来的衣钩。他若无其事地从那儿转过身来，门恰好开了，西尔弗代尔走进实验室。见警察在场，他似乎吃了一惊，一言不发地把这一群人挨个儿打量了一遍。随后他走上前来。

===Original===
“Do you want me?” he asked, in a colourless voice.

Markfield seemed rather ashamed at being caught there in the company of the two officials. He was about to say something when Flamborough robbed him of the opportunity.

“I’ve come to put one or two questions, Dr. Silverdale,” the Inspector began. “First of all, have you had any dealings lately with a man named Peter Whalley?”

===Chinese===
“你们是找我？”他问，声调平板，不带一丝色彩。

叫人撞见自己同这两位官方人物待在一处，马克菲尔德显得颇有几分讪讪然。他正要开口，弗拉姆伯勒抢了先，不给他这个机会。

“我是来提一两个问题的，西尔弗代尔医生，”探长开腔道。“首先，您近来同一个名叫彼得·沃利的人可有过什么来往？”

===Original===
Silverdale was obviously taken aback.

“Whalley?” he repeated. “I know nothing about anyone of that name. Who is he?”

Flamborough seemed to discount this statement, but he did not persist along that direct line.

===Chinese===
西尔弗代尔显然吃了一惊。

“沃利？”他重复道。“这个名字的人，我一无所知。他是谁？”

弗拉姆伯勒对这话似乎大打折扣，但他没有沿着这条直线穷追下去。

===Original===
“Can you tell us what you were doing last night?” he demanded.

Silverdale reflected for a time before answering.

“I left here about six o’clock—between six and six-thirty. Then I walked down to the Central Hotel and had dinner. I suppose I left the hotel again about a quarter to eight. I walked home, as it was a clear night; and I did some work until about half-past eleven. After that I went to bed and read for a while before going to sleep.”

===Chinese===
“能告诉我们您昨晚的行止么？”他问道。

西尔弗代尔想了一阵才作答。

“我六点钟左右离开这儿——六点到六点半之间。随后我步行到中央旅馆（Central Hotel）用了晚餐。大概七点三刻，我离开了旅馆。那晚天色晴朗，我便走路回家；到家后做了些工作，直到十一点半前后。此后我上了床，看了一阵书才入睡。”

===Original===
Flamborough jotted something in his notebook before going further.

“I suppose you could produce some witnesses in support of that?” he asked.

Silverdale appeared to consult his memory.

===Chinese===
弗拉姆伯勒在本子上草草记了几笔，才继续问下去。

“这些，我想您总拿得出几位证人来佐证吧？”他问。

西尔弗代尔像是先在记忆里检索了一番。

===Original===
“I met Miss Hailsham as I was leaving here,” he explained. “That would give you the approximate time, if she remembers it. The waiter at the Central could probably satisfy you that I was there—it’s the tall one with the wart on his cheek who looks after the tables at the north window. After that, you’ll have to take my word for it.”

“What about your maids at Heatherfield?”

“I haven’t anyone on the premises. No maid would take the place owing to the murder. I merely sleep there and take my meals at an hotel. A charwoman comes in during the day and cleans the place.”

===Chinese===
“我离开这儿的时候，遇见了海尔沙姆小姐，”他解释道。“她若记得，便能给你们一个大致的时刻。中央旅馆的侍应生，大概也能让你们确信我在那里——就是那个高个儿、腮帮上生着疣的，他照管北窗那几张桌子。再往后，你们就只得信我一句话了。”

“希瑟菲尔德那边您的女仆们呢？”

“宅子里我如今一个人也没有。出了那桩命案，哪个女仆也不肯来屈就。我不过在那里过夜，三餐都在旅馆里解决。白天有个打杂的女佣进来打扫。”

===Original===
“Ah,” said the Inspector, thoughtfully. “Then you can’t prove that you were actually at home after, say, half-past eight? By the way, you hadn’t a visitor by any chance?”

Silverdale shook his head.

“No, I was quite alone.”

===Chinese===
“啊，”探长若有所思地说。“这么说，比方说八点半以后，您就无法证明自己确实在家了？顺便问一句，您该不会正巧有过访客吧？”

西尔弗代尔摇了摇头。

“没有，我一直独自一人。”

===Original===
Flamborough made another note; and then continued his interrogation.

“I want you to cast your mind back to the night when Mrs. Silverdale came by her death. I asked you once before what you were doing that night, but you put me off. I think you’d find it more advisable to be frank, now that I’m putting the question again.”

Silverdale’s face showed some conflict of emotions, and he evidently considered the matter for almost a minute before answering.

===Chinese===
弗拉姆伯勒又记了一笔；随后继续他的讯问。

“我要您把记忆拉回到西尔弗代尔太太罹难的那一夜。那一晚您在做什么，我先前问过一回，您把我搪塞了过去。如今我旧事重提，我想您会发觉，还是坦白为妙。”

西尔弗代尔的脸上显出几分情绪的交战，他显然把这桩事斟酌了将近一分钟，才开口作答。

===Original===
“I’ve nothing further to add,” he said at last.

“I’ll put it plainly, so that there can be no mistake,” Flamborough emphasised. “Can you give us any account of your movements on the night that your maid was murdered at Heatherfield?”

Silverdale tightened his lips and shook his head.

===Chinese===
“我没有什么可补充的，”他终于说道。

“我把话挑明了说，免得滋生误会，”弗拉姆伯勒加重了语气。“在您家女仆于希瑟菲尔德被害的那一夜，您能否向我们交代一下自己的行止？”

西尔弗代尔抿紧了嘴唇，摇了摇头。

===Original===
“I’ve no information to give you,” he said at length.

“I may as well tell you, Dr. Silverdale,” said Flamborough warningly, “that we have a certain amount of information drawn from other sources. We may know more than you think. Wouldn’t it be best to be frank with us?”

Silverdale shook his head definitely without making any vocal reply. Flamborough concealed his disappointment, though his face grew darker. He put his hand into his waistcoat pocket and drew out something.

===Chinese===
“我无可奉告，”他末了说道。

“我不妨告诉您，西尔弗代尔医生，”弗拉姆伯勒带着告诫的口吻说，“我们从其他来源已经得到了一定分量的情报。我们知道的可能比您以为的要多。同我们坦白些，岂不是上策？”

西尔弗代尔断然摇头，没有出声作答。弗拉姆伯勒掩饰住了失望，脸色却沉了下来。他把手伸进马甲口袋，掏出一样东西。

===Original===
“Do you recognise that, Dr. Silverdale?”

Silverdale examined it.

“Yes, that’s a cigarette-holder of mine. I recognise it by the fly in it.”

===Chinese===
“您认得这个么，西尔弗代尔医生？”

西尔弗代尔把它端详了一番。

“认得，那是我的一个烟嘴。凭里头那只苍蝇，我认得出它。”

===Original===
“When did you discover that you had lost it?”

Silverdale was obviously at a loss.

“I can’t tell you. Ten days ago or so, I should think.”

===Chinese===
“您是什么时候发觉它丢了的？”

西尔弗代尔显然一时语塞。

“说不上来。十天前光景吧，我估摸着。”

===Original===
“Was it before or after the murder of your maid that you missed it? Think carefully.”

“I can’t remember,” Silverdale explained. “I didn’t note it down in a diary or anything of that sort, of course. I use two or three holders. I leave them in the pockets of different suits. Naturally if one of them goes a-missing, I simply use one of the others; and perhaps the missing one may turn up later. I can’t give you any exact date when this one went astray.”

Flamborough returned the holder to his pocket.

===Chinese===
“您发觉它不见，是在女仆被害之前，还是之后？仔细想想。”

“记不得了，”西尔弗代尔解释道。“我自然没有往日记本里记这一笔什么的。我的烟嘴有两三只，分别搁在不同衣裳的口袋里。丢了一只，我照常用别的便是；过些时候，丢了的那只兴许又自己冒出来。这一只是哪一天没的影儿，我给不出您一个确切的日子。”

弗拉姆伯勒把烟嘴收回了口袋。

===Original===
“You play the banjo, don’t you, Dr. Silverdale?”

Silverdale seemed completely astounded by this question.

“I used to do so,” he admitted, “but I haven’t played for quite a long time. The banjo isn’t much in request nowadays.”

===Chinese===
“您弹班卓琴，是吧，西尔弗代尔医生？”

这个问题似乎令西尔弗代尔惊愕万分。

“从前弹过，”他承认道，“可我已经很久不弹了。如今班卓琴不大时兴了。”

===Original===
“Have you bought strings for your instrument recently?”

“No. I haven’t. Last time I used it, two of the strings snapped, and I never troubled to replace them.”

“Just so,” Flamborough said, as though attaching no great importance to the point. “Now there’s another thing I’d like to ask about. I think that’s your laboratory coat hanging on the peg over there?”

===Chinese===
“近来您给自己的琴配过弦么？”

“没有。没配过。上一回用它的时候，断了两根弦，我一直懒得去换。”

“原来如此，”弗拉姆伯勒说道，好像并不怎么把这个话头当回事。“现在还有一件事我想问问。挂在那边衣钩上的，我想是您那件实验室工作服吧？”

===Original===
Silverdale glanced across the room and nodded.

“When did you wear that coat last?” Flamborough demanded.

“Last night,” Silverdale answered, after a slight hesitation.

===Chinese===
西尔弗代尔朝屋子那头瞥了一眼，点了点头。

“那件工作服您上一回穿，是什么时候？”弗拉姆伯勒追问。

“昨晚，”西尔弗代尔略一迟疑，答道。

===Original===
“You mean you took it off when you left the Institute to go out to dinner?”

“Yes. This morning I’ve been up at the Research Station, so I’ve had no occasion to change my jacket.”

Flamborough crossed the room, took down the coat, and spread it out on the table once more.

===Chinese===
“您是说，您离开研究所出去用晚餐的时候，把它脱了下来？”

“是的。今天早上我一直待在农业研究站，所以还没得着机会换工作服。”

弗拉姆伯勒穿过房间，取下那件工作服，又一次把它铺展在桌上。

===Original===
“Can you explain this?” he questioned, putting his hand on the tear.

Silverdale stared at the rent in the cloth with dismay gathering on his face. He looked like a man who finds himself surrounded by enemies in unknown strength.

“I can’t account for it,” he said curtly, with whitened lips.

===Chinese===
“这您能解释么？”他问，一只手按在那道破口上。

西尔弗代尔紧盯着布上的裂口，脸上渐渐聚起惊惶。他那模样，像一个人发觉自己被一群不知众寡的敌人团团围住。

“我解释不了，”他简短地说，嘴唇都发了白。

===Original===
“Or for this bloodstain on it, I suppose?” Flamborough demanded, putting his finger on the spot.

Silverdale’s discomposure became even more obvious. It was clear that he felt himself in a most dangerous position; and his denials betrayed his nervousness.

“I’ve no idea how it came there. I noticed nothing of the sort when I took the coat off last night. Neither the tear nor that stain. I can’t account for it at all.”

===Chinese===
“那上头这块血迹，想必您也解释不了？”弗拉姆伯勒追问，手指按在那污渍上。

西尔弗代尔的慌乱愈发显露。他显然感到自己的处境凶险至极；那一连串否认，都透出了他的心虚。

“它怎么弄上去的，我毫无所知。昨晚脱下这件工作服的时候，这类情形我一样也没瞧见。破口也好，那块污渍也好，都没有。这事我全然解释不了。”

===Original===
“You’re sure you can’t?” the Inspector persisted.

“I can’t,” Silverdale repeated.

Much to the Inspector’s annoyance, Markfield broke into the interrogation.

===Chinese===
“您肯定解释不了？”探长紧追不舍。

“解释不了，”西尔弗代尔重复道。

令探长大为恼火的是，马克菲尔德插进来打断了讯问。

===Original===
“Why are you so sure that Dr. Silverdale has anything to do with the matter?” he interjected in a sardonic tone. “It’s not impossible that someone borrowed his jacket last night after he’d gone. Several of us were on the premises after he left, I know.”

Flamborough, glancing up, surprised an expression on Sir Clinton’s face which indicated that his opinion of Markfield had risen on account of this interposition; and the Inspector felt his irritation against Markfield increasing once more.

“I’m not asking for your assistance now, Dr. Markfield,” he pointed out, chillingly. “I want to know what Dr. Silverdale knows about the matter. You can hardly speak as an authority on that point, can you?”

===Chinese===
“您凭什么这样笃定，这事同西尔弗代尔医生有干系？”他讥诮地插言道。“昨晚他走后，有人借用了他的工作服，这也并非不可能。他离开之后，我们当中有几个人还留在所里，这我知道。”

弗拉姆伯勒抬眼之间，冷不防捕捉到克林顿爵士脸上的一种神情——那表明经此一举，他对马克菲尔德的评价看涨了；而探长只觉得自己对马克菲尔德的火气又蹿高了一截。

“眼下还轮不到你帮忙，马克菲尔德医生，”他冷冷地指出。“我想知道的是西尔弗代尔医生对此事知道些什么。在这一点上，你恐怕算不得权威，对吧？”

===Original===
Markfield made no reply; but his smile was a comment in itself and did nothing to soothe the Inspector’s ruffled feelings.

“I’ll have to take this coat, Dr. Silverdale,” Flamborough explained in an official tone. “It’s a piece of evidence which we must have in our charge.”

Then, as an afterthought, he added:

===Chinese===
马克菲尔德没有作答；可他那一笑本身就是一句评语，丝毫无助于抚平探长被搅起的心绪。

“这件工作服我必须带走，西尔弗代尔医生，”弗拉姆伯勒用公事公办的口吻说明道。“这是一件物证，必须归我们保管。”

接着，像是临时又想起，他补了一句：

===Original===
“A man Whalley has been murdered. The case didn’t get into the morning newspapers. You’ll see it in the evening news.”

His voice took on a sub-tinge of warning:

“If you think the better of your attitude, you’d be well advised to come to us at once and tell us what you can. It’s hardly necessary to tell you that your silence on these points is bound to raise suspicions; and if you can clear things up, you may save yourself a good deal of trouble.”

===Chinese===
“一个姓沃利的人被谋杀了。这案子没上早晨的报纸。晚报上您会看到。”

他的话音里透出一丝暗藏的警告：

“您若把这套态度重新掂量过、打算改弦更张，那就最好立刻来找我们，把您能说的都说出来。不必我多讲，您在这些问题上保持缄默，势必要招人疑心；您若能把事情说清，或许能替自己省去好些麻烦。”

===Original===
Markfield seemed to take a cynical pleasure in destroying the Inspector’s effects. Instead of leaving him the last word, he closed the interview himself.

“They used to say a man was innocent until he was proved guilty, Inspector,” he remarked ironically, “but I see you’ve interchanged the adjectives nowadays. It must save a lot of trouble to the police.”

===Chinese===
马克菲尔德似乎以拆探长的台为乐，颇带几分愤世的快意。他不把压轴的话留给探长，自己把这场问话收了场。

“从前人们常说，一个人在被证明有罪之前是无辜的，探长，”他讥讽地说，“可我看如今你们把这两个形容词对调了个个儿。这准替警察省下不少麻烦。”

## XV Sir Clinton’s Double / 十五 克林顿爵士的替身

===Original===

Two days after the interview with Markfield and his colleague at the Croft-Thornton Institute, Inspector Flamborough came into Sir Clinton’s office, obviously in a state of faint trepidation.

“I’ve arrested Silverdale this morning, sir,” he announced in a voice which betrayed that he was not quite sure whether this step would meet his superior’s approval.

The Chief Constable exhibited neither surprise nor disapproval at the news.

===Chinese===

在克罗夫特-索顿研究所与马克菲尔德及其同事那次会面过去两天后，弗拉姆伯勒探长走进克林顿爵士的办公室，神色间显然带着一丝隐隐的不安。

“今天上午我把西尔弗代尔逮捕了，先生，”他通报说，语气里透着他拿不准这一步能否博得上司的嘉许。

听了这消息，郡警察局长既不惊讶，也没有不以为然。

===Original===

“I shouldn’t care to say that you’ve got a complete case against him, Inspector. Not yet, at any rate. But he’s got himself to thank for his troubles; and now I expect things will begin to move a bit quicker in the case. Mr. Justice will be calling up his last reserves.”

Flamborough seemed to feel that his action needed some justification, though Sir Clinton had asked for none.

“Well, sir, it seems to me we had to forestall a possible bolt on Silverdale’s part. There’s quite enough evidence to justify his detention on suspicion in the meantime.”

===Chinese===

“我还不敢说您已经握有对付他的完整案证，探长。至少眼下还不敢说。不过他这些麻烦是咎由自取；现在我料想，案子的进展要开始加快了。那位‘正义’先生就要征调他最后的预备队了。”

虽然克林顿爵士并未要求，弗拉姆伯勒似乎仍觉得自己的举动需要几分辩白。

“是这样，先生，依我看，我们必须先发制人，堵死西尔弗代尔可能的潜逃。眼下的证据，暂且足以支持以嫌疑为由将他羁押。”

===Original===

“There’s just one point I’d like to know about,” Sir Clinton said, disregarding the Inspector’s statement. “You’ve got four deaths to choose from. Which of them are you going to select as your main case? You can hardly put him on trial for all four simultaneously. There’s nothing against it legally, but you’d confuse the jury, I’m afraid.”

“I thought the bungalow business would be best, sir. There’s a fair chance of establishing a motive in it; whereas in the Heatherfield affair there’s only conjecture as to what he was after; and in the Whalley case we simply haven’t got enough evidence apart from the jacket, unless we can prove that Silverdale was the bungalow murderer. And if we can prove that, then there’s no need to enter into the Whalley case at all.”

Sir Clinton acquiesced with a nod.

===Chinese===

“我只想弄清楚一点，”克林顿爵士说，全不理会探长的这番表白。“您手头有四条人命可供挑选。您打算选哪一桩作主案？总不能让他同时为四案受审。法律上固然没有障碍，可我怕您会把陪审团搅糊涂。”

“我以为平房那桩最合适，先生。那一桩里，确立动机还颇有几分把握；希瑟菲尔德那案，他图的究竟是什么，只有猜测可言；沃利那案，除开那件工作服，我们实在拿不出足够的证据——除非我们能证明西尔弗代尔就是平房案的凶手。而倘若证明了这一点，那就全然不必再立案追究沃利案了。”

克林顿爵士点头认可。

===Original===

“The bungalow affair is the key to the whole series,” he admitted without qualification.

There was a knock at the door and a constable entered.

===Chinese===

“平房案是整个连环案子的钥匙，”他毫无保留地承认。

这时有人敲门，一名警士走了进来。

===Original===

“A young lady wants to see you, sir,” he announced as he crossed the room and handed a card to the Chief Constable. “She insisted that she must see you personally. There’s a woman with her.”

“Send her up,” Sir Clinton ordered, after a glance at the card.

When the constable had left the room, Sir Clinton flicked the tiny oblong of pasteboard across his desk to the Inspector who picked it up.

===Chinese===

“有位年轻小姐要见您，先生，”他一边穿过房间、把一张名片递给郡警察局长，一边通报。“她坚持非亲自见您不可。同她一起来的还有位妇人。”

“请她上来，”克林顿爵士瞥了一眼名片，吩咐道。

警士退出房间后，克林顿爵士手指一弹，把那一小片长方形纸板弹过桌面送与探长，探长将它拾了起来。

===Original===

“Miss Avice Deepcar,” he read. “What the deuce can she be wanting here?”

“Calm yourself, Inspector. The next instalment will be published in a moment or two. You’d better wait here while she interviews me.”

When Avice Deepcar entered the office, Flamborough was puzzled by her manner. She seemed to be agitated, but it was not the sort of agitation he had expected. When she spoke, it sounded as if she were both indignant and perturbed.

===Chinese===

“艾维斯·迪普卡小姐，”他念道。“她到这儿来能有什么鬼名堂？”

“稍安勿躁，探长。这部连载的下一期，转眼就要刊出了。她会见我的时候，您最好就在这儿等着。”

艾维斯·迪普卡走进办公室时，她的神态令弗拉姆伯勒摸不着头脑。她似乎心绪激荡，但那激荡又不是他原先料想的那一种。她一开口，听来既像愤慨，又像惶惶不安。

===Original===

“You’re Sir Clinton Driffield, aren’t you?” she demanded, scanning the Chief Constable closely.

The Chief Constable confessed to his identity.

“Then I’ll come straight to the point,” Avice said. “What is the meaning of your visiting my house last night, terrorising my maid, and making a search through my private papers? I’m going to see my solicitor about it—I don’t believe it’s legal. But in the meantime, I want to know why you did it.”

===Chinese===

“您就是克林顿·德里菲尔德爵士，对么？”她一面紧盯着郡警察局长打量，一面质问。

郡警察局长承认了自己的身份。

“那我就开门见山了，”艾维斯说。“您昨夜光临舍下，恫吓我的女仆，把我的私人信件搜了个遍，这究竟是什么意思？这事我要去见我的律师——我不信这是合法的。但在那之前，我要先知道您为什么这么干。”

===Original===

Flamborough was completely taken aback by this charge. He stared open-mouthed at his superior. Sir Clinton occasionally did things which mystified him; but this seemed something completely out of the common.

“He must have got a search-warrant without saying anything about it to me,” the Inspector reflected. “But why on earth didn’t he take me with him, even if he wanted to go through her papers personally?”

Sir Clinton’s face had become an inscrutable mask.

===Chinese===

这番指控让弗拉姆伯勒全然措手不及。他张着嘴瞪住自己的上司。克林顿爵士偶尔也会做些令他如坠云雾的事；可这一桩，似乎格外不同寻常。

“他准是背着我弄到了搜查证，”探长暗自琢磨。“可即便他想亲自翻查她的文件，究竟为什么不把我带上？”

克林顿爵士的脸已化作一副高深莫测的面具。

===Original===

“Perhaps you wouldn’t mind being a little more explicit, Miss Deepcar,” he suggested. “I’m not quite sure that I understand your grievance.”

Avice Deepcar’s face showed that she had nothing but contempt for this apparent quibbling.

“If you think it worth while, I’ll give you details,” she said, disdainfully. “But you’re not thinking of denying it, are you? I’ve got a witness to prove the facts, you know.”

===Chinese===

“迪普卡小姐，您若不介意，不妨把话说得再明白些，”他提议道。“我不大敢说，我已经听明白了您的委屈何在。”

艾维斯·迪普卡的神色表明，对这种显然的支吾搪塞，她心里只有轻蔑。

“您若觉得值得，我就把细节讲给您听，”她鄙夷地说。“不过您总不至于是想抵赖吧？我有证人能证实这些事实，这您是知道的。”

===Original===

With a gesture, Sir Clinton invited her to tell her story.

“I’ve been away from home for a day or two,” Avice began. “This morning, I came back to Westerhaven by the eleven o’clock train and drove to my house in a taxi. I left my maid alone in the house while I was away; and when I got home again, I found her in a great state of excitement. It seems that last night you came to the door, showed her your card, and told her that you had come to search the house on some matter connected with these awful murders. Naturally she was greatly shocked; but there was nothing for it but to let you in. You went over the whole place, searched in every corner of the house, opened every drawer, poked your nose into all my private possessions—in fact, you behaved as if I were a criminal under suspicion.”

===Chinese===

克林顿爵士做了个手势，请她陈述原委。

“我离家在外一两天，”艾维斯开始说。“今天上午，我乘十一点钟的火车回到韦斯特黑文，坐出租汽车到了家。我不在时，家里只留女仆一人看家；等我回到家，只见她激动得非同小可。据她说，昨夜您上门出示了名片，对她讲，您是为着与这几桩可怕凶案有关的某项事由，来搜查这所房子的。她自然大为震惊；可也别无办法，只好放您进来。您把整所宅子巡视了一遍，每个角落都搜遍，每只抽屉都打开，我样样私人物件您都要把鼻子探一探——总而言之，您那副做派，简直就当我是个受嫌疑的罪犯。”

===Original===

She paused, as though to rein in her temper after this sudden outpouring. Indignation had brought a slight flush to her cheeks, and her quickened breathing betrayed the agitation she was trying to keep under control. Mechanically she changed the position of her feet and smoothed down her skirt; and Flamborough’s sharp eye noted a trembling in her hand as she did so. Sir Clinton maintained his silence and gazed at her as though he expected further information.

===Chinese===

她停了下来，仿佛这阵突然的倾泻之后要把性子勒住。愤慨使她双颊微微泛红，急促的呼吸泄露了她竭力按捺的激动。她机械地挪了挪站姿，抚平裙子；弗拉姆伯勒眼尖，看见她做这些动作时手在发颤。克林顿爵士保持沉默，凝视着她，仿佛还在等待更多的东西。

===Original===

“My maid was very much put about, naturally,” she went on. “She asked you again and again what was at the back of it all; but you gave her no explanation whatever. When you’d completed your search of my house, you sat down with a pile of correspondence you’d collected—you see I know all about it—and you began to read through my private letters. Some of them you put aside; others you laid down in a pile on my desk. When you’d finished reading them all, you took away the ones you’d selected and left the rest on the desk. Then you left the house, without offering the slightest explanation of this raid of yours. I shan’t stand that, you know. You’ve no right to do things of that sort—throwing suspicion on me in this way, without the faintest ground for it. Naturally, my maid has been babbling about it and everyone knows the police have been on the premises. It’s put me in a dreadful position; and you’ll have to give me an explanation and an apology. It’s no use trying to deny the facts, you know. I can prove what I’ve said. And I want my letters back at once—the ones you stole. … You’ve no right to them, and I simply won’t put up with this kind of thing.”

===Chinese===

“我的女仆自然给搅得心神不宁，”她继续道。“她一遍又一遍地问您，这一切背后到底是什么；可您连一句解释也不给她。您搜查完毕之后，便抱着一摞收拢来的信件坐了下来——您瞧，这一切我全知道——开始通读我的私人信件。有几封您搁置一旁；另外几封您摞成一叠，放在我的书桌上。全部读罢，您把选中的带走，其余的留在桌上。随后您便离开了这所房子，对您这场突袭没有半句解释。这种事我不会善罢甘休，您知道。您没有权利干这种事——这样毫无根由地往我身上泼嫌疑。我的女仆自然已把这事念叨得人人皆知，人人都晓得警察光临过这所宅子。这把我推到了一个糟透了的境地；您必须给我一个解释，外加一份道歉。想否认这些事实是没用的，您知道。我说的每一句都能证实。还有，我要立刻收回我的信——您偷走的那些。……您无权占有它们，这种事情我断断不能容忍。”

===Original===

She broke off once more, evidently afraid that she was letting her feelings get the better of her. For a moment or two Sir Clinton made no reply. He seemed to be considering something carefully before he spoke.

===Chinese===

她再度收住话头，显然是怕情绪占了理智的上风。有一两秒钟，克林顿爵士未作回答。他似乎在把某件事仔细掂量过了，才肯开口。

===Original===

“I suppose you know, Miss Deepcar,” he said at last, “that Dr. Silverdale is under arrest.”

The girl’s expression changed in an instant. Something like fear replaced her earlier anger.

===Chinese===

“我想您已经知道了吧，迪普卡小姐，”他终于说，“西尔弗代尔医生被捕了。”

姑娘的神情霎时大变。几分近似恐惧的东西取代了先前的怒火。

===Original===

“Dr. Silverdale? Arrested?” she demanded, with a tremor in her voice. “What do you mean?”

“He was arrested yesterday in connection with the affair at the bungalow.”

Avice Deepcar’s eyes showed her amazement at the news.

===Chinese===

“西尔弗代尔医生？被捕了？”她嗓音发颤地质问。“您这是什么意思？”

“他昨天被捕了，与平房那桩案子有牵连。”

这个消息令艾维斯·迪普卡目露惊愕。

===Original===

“The affair at the bungalow?” she repeated. “But he had nothing to do with that! He couldn’t have had.”

All her indignation seemed to have been swept away by this fresh information. She had the appearance of someone upon whom a wholly unexpected peril has descended. Sir Clinton seemed satisfied by the effect of his words; but without giving her time for thought, he pursued his narrative.

“Several things have turned up which seem to implicate him in that affair, and when we tried to extract some information from him about his movements on the night of the bungalow murder, he refused to say anything. He wouldn’t tell us where he had been at that time.”

===Chinese===

“平房那桩案子？”她重复道。“可他跟那案子毫不相干呀！不可能相干。”

这个新消息似乎把她满腔的愤慨一扫而空。她那模样，恰似全然料不到的祸事正从天而降、落到头上的人。克林顿爵士似乎对自己这番话的效果颇为满意；但他不给她思索的空隙，径自讲了下去。

“查出了好几件事，似乎都把他同那桩案子牵连在一起；我们想从他口中探知平房凶案当晚的行踪时，他却拒绝吐露一个字。他不肯告诉我们，那时候他身在何处。”

===Original===

Avice Deepcar clasped and unclasped her hands mechanically for a second or two. It was obvious that she was thinking swiftly and coming to some decision upon which much might turn.

“He won’t say where he was?” she demanded in a trembling voice. “Why not?”

===Chinese===

艾维斯·迪普卡机械地把双手握紧又松开，持续了一两秒钟。显而易见，她在飞速地转着念头，正走向某个决定，而许多事情都可能系于此。

“他不肯说他当时在哪儿？”她声音发抖地质问。“为什么不肯？”

===Original===

Sir Clinton made a vague gesture with his hand.

“I can hardly tell you his motive. Perhaps he hasn’t an alibi. I’ve told you what we know.”

He looked keenly at the girl before him, evidently expecting something; and he was not disappointed.

===Chinese===

克林顿爵士含糊地摆了摆手。

“他的动机我很难替他解说。也许他拿不出不在场的证明。我知道的都已告诉您了。”

他锐利地注视着面前的姑娘，分明在期待着什么；而他并未失望。

===Original===

“I can tell you where he was at that time,” Avice said at last. “Probably you won’t believe me, but this is true, at any rate. He and I dined together in town that evening and after dinner we went home to my house. We had a lot to talk over. We reached my house about half-past eight. And then we began to talk things over. We had such a lot to discuss that the time passed without our noticing it; and when at last he got up to go, it was between one and two in the morning. So you see he couldn’t possibly have been at the bungalow.”

Sir Clinton interjected a question:

===Chinese===

“他当时在哪里，我能告诉您，”艾维斯终于说。“您多半不会相信我，可至少这是真话。那天晚上，他和我一起在城里用了晚餐，饭后我们一同回我家去。我们有许多事要商谈。八点半前后我们到了我家，随后便开始把事情逐件谈开。要商量的实在太多，时间不知不觉便溜了过去；待他终于起身告辞，已是凌晨一点到两点之间。所以您瞧，他绝不可能到过平房。”

克林顿爵士插问一句：

===Original===

“Why didn’t Dr. Silverdale tell us all this frankly when he was questioned about his movements during that night?”

Avice Deepcar flushed at the direct attack, but she evidently had made up her mind to make a clean breast of the whole business.

“I told you that Dr. Silverdale was with me that night from dinnertime until the early hours of the morning. As it happened, my maid was away that day and did not return until the next afternoon. You must have a pretty good idea of what people would have said about me if they got to know I’d been alone with Dr. Silverdale in my house. I shouldn’t have cared, really; because there was nothing in it. We were simply talking. But I expect that when you questioned him he thought of my position. He’s a married man—at least he was a married man then—and some people would have twisted the whole business into something very unpleasant for me, I’m sure. So I think, knowing him well, that he very likely didn’t want to give me away. He knew he’d had nothing to do with the murders, and I expect he imagined that the real murderer would be detected without his having to give any precise account of his doings on that night. If I’d known that he was running the risk of arrest, of course, I’d have insisted on his telling what really happened; but I’ve been out of town and I’d no idea things had got to this pitch.”

===Chinese===

“西尔弗代尔医生受讯问他那一夜的行踪时，为什么不把这些向我们和盘托出？”

这一记直拳令艾维斯·迪普卡涨红了脸，但她显然已横下心，要把整桩事情坦白干净。

“我已告诉过您，那天晚上从晚饭时分直到凌晨，西尔弗代尔医生都同我在一起。碰巧得很，我的女仆那天外出，到第二天下午才回来。人们若是得知我单独同西尔弗代尔医生待在自己家中，会说些什么难听的话，您心里必定相当有数。说实在的，我原本不会在乎，因为这里头本就什么也没有。我们不过是在谈话。可我想，您讯问他的时候，他顾虑到了我的处境。他是有妇之夫——至少当时还是有妇之夫——而有些人定会把整桩事情歪曲编排，叫我十分难堪，这一点我确信。所以，深知他的为人，我以为他多半不愿把我供出去。他知道自己同凶案毫不相干，我料想他原以为，不必精确交代他那晚的行止，真凶也终会被查出来。我若早知道他正冒着被捕的风险，自然早就坚持要他把实情说出来了；可我一直不在城里，全然不知事情已闹到这般地步。”

===Original===

Flamborough intervened as she paused for a moment.

===Chinese===

她略停了片刻，弗拉姆伯勒趁机插话。

===Original===

“Your maid was away that night? Then you’ve got no one else who could give evidence that Dr. Silverdale was with you during that crucial period?”

Avice seemed to see a fresh gulf opening before her.

“No,” she admitted, with a faint tremor in her voice. “We were quite alone. No one saw us go into the house and no one saw him leave it.”

===Chinese===

“那晚您的女仆外出了？那么，除了您本人，再没有旁人能证明西尔弗代尔医生在那个关键时辰与您在一起了？”

艾维斯似乎看见一道新的深渊正在面前裂开。

“没有，”她承认，嗓音里带着一丝轻颤。“就我们两个人。没人看见我们进屋，也没人看见他离开。”

===Original===

“H’m!” said Flamborough. “Then it rests on your own evidence entirely? There’s no confirmation of it?”

“What confirmation do you need?” Avice demanded. “Dr. Silverdale will tell you the same story. Surely that’s sufficient?”

Before Flamborough could make any comment on this, Sir Clinton turned the interview back to its original subject.

===Chinese===

“哼！”弗拉姆伯勒说。“那么这事全然系于您一人的证词了？没有佐证？”

“您还要什么佐证？”艾维斯反问。“西尔弗代尔医生会对您讲出同样的经过。这难道还不够？”

弗拉姆伯勒还来不及就此发表意见，克林顿爵士已把这场问话引回了原先的题目。

===Original===

“I should like to be clear about the other matter first, if you please, Miss Deepcar. With regard to this police raid on your house, as you called it, can you tell me something more about it? For instance, you say that I produced my card. Was that card preserved?”

“No,” Avice admitted. “My maid tells me that you only showed it to her; you didn’t actually hand it over to her.”

===Chinese===

“对不起，迪普卡小姐，我想先把另一件事弄个清楚。关于您所谓的这场警察突查您的家宅，您能不能再多告诉我一些详情？譬如，您说我曾出示过名片。那张名片还保存着么？”

“没有，”艾维斯承认。“我的女仆告诉我，您只是拿给她看了看，并没有当真交到她手里。”

===Original===

“Then anybody might have presented it?”

“No,” Avice contradicted him. “My maid recognised you. She’d seen your photograph in a newspaper once, some months ago, and she knew you from that.”

“Ah! Indeed! Can you produce this maid? She’s not out of town at present or anything like that?”

===Chinese===

“那么，随便谁都可能出示那张名片了？”

“不，”艾维斯反驳他。“我的女娘认出了您本人。几个月前，她有一次在报上见过您的照片，从那以后便认得您了。”

“啊！原来如此！这位女仆您能带来么？她眼下没出城、或诸如此类的情形吧？”

===Original===

“I can produce her in a few moments,” Avice retorted with obvious assurance. “She’s waiting for me somewhere in this building at the present time.”

Sir Clinton glanced at Flamborough and the Inspector retired from the room. In a very short time he returned, bringing with him a middle-aged woman, who glanced inquisitively at Sir Clinton as she entered.

“Now, Marple,” Avice Deepcar demanded, “do you recognise anyone here?”

===Chinese===

“几分钟之内我就能把她带到，”艾维斯带着显而易见的把握回敬道。“此时此刻，她正在这栋楼里的什么地方等着我。”

克林顿爵士朝弗拉姆伯勒斯了一眼，探长便退出房去。不多时他回来了，领进一位中年妇人；那妇人进门时，好奇地朝克林顿爵士打量了一眼。

“好了，马普尔，”艾维斯·迪普卡问道，“这儿的人你可认得谁？”

===Original===

Mrs. Marple had no hesitation in the matter.

“That’s Sir Clinton Driffield, Miss. I know his face quite well.”

Flamborough’s suspicion that his superior had been moving in the background of the case were completely confirmed by this evidence; but he was still further surprised to catch a gleam of sardonic amusement passing across the face of the Chief Constable.

===Chinese===

马普尔太太在这事上毫不含糊。

“那就是克林顿·德里菲尔德爵士，小姐。他这张脸我认得很熟。”

这句证词把弗拉姆伯勒的那层疑心——他的上司一直在案子幕后活动——彻底坐实了；可紧接着他又惊诧地捕捉到，一丝讥诮的笑意掠过郡警察局长的脸。

===Original===

“You recognise me, it seems?” he said, as though half in doubt as to what line to take. “You won’t mind my testing your memory a little? Well, then, what kind of suit was I wearing when I came to your house?”

Mrs. Marple considered carefully for a moment or two before replying:

===Chinese===

“这么说，你认得我？”他说，口吻仿佛一时拿不定该取什么路数。“你不介意我考一考你的记性吧？那么好，我上你家去的时候，身上穿的是哪一类衣服？”

马普尔太太细细想了片刻，才回答道：

===Original===

“An ordinary suit, sir; a dark one rather like the one you’ve got on just now.”

“You can’t recall the colour?”

“It was a dark suit, that’s all I can remember. You came in the evening, sir, and the light isn’t good for colours.”

===Chinese===

“一套寻常衣服，先生；深色的，同您此刻穿的这套挺相像。”

“颜色你回想不起来了？”

“是套深色衣服，我记得的就只这么多。您是傍晚来的，先生，那种光线看不清颜色。”

===Original===

“You didn’t notice my tie, or anything like that?”

“No, sir. You’ll remember that I was put about at the time. You gave me a shock, coming down on me like that. It’s the first time I ever had to do with the police, sir; and I was all on my nerves’ edge with the idea that you’d come after Miss Avice, sir. I couldn’t hardly get to believe it, and I was all in a twitter.”

Sir Clinton nodded sympathetically.

===Chinese===

“我的领带，或是那一类的东西，你没留意到？”

“没有，先生。您总还记得，我那时心里正乱糟糟的。您那样劈头压到我跟前，可把我吓了一大跳。我这辈子头一回同警察打交道，先生；一想到您是冲着艾维斯小姐来的，先生，我简直怕得神经都绷到了断的边缘。这事我怎么也难相信，整个人慌得没了主张。”

克林顿爵士体谅地点了点头。

===Original===

“I’m sorry you were so much disturbed. Now have a good look at me where the light’s bright enough. Do you see anything that strikes you as different from the appearance I had that night?”

He moved across to the window and stood patiently while Mrs. Marple scanned him up and down deliberately.

“You haven’t got your eyeglass on today, sir.”

===Chinese===

“叫你受了这么大的惊扰，我很抱歉。现在你到光线够亮的地方，好好看看我。你可看出有什么地方，跟我那晚的模样不一样？”

他移步走到窗前，耐心地站定，任由马普尔太太上上下下从容端详。

“您今天没戴单片眼镜，先生。”

===Original===

“Ah! Did you say eyeglass or eyeglasses?”

“Eyeglass, sir. I remember you dropped it out of your eye when you began to read Miss Avice’s letters.”

“Apart from the eyeglass, then, I’m much the same?”

===Chinese===

“啊！你说的是单片眼镜，还是一副眼镜？”

“单片眼镜，先生。我记得您开始读艾维斯小姐那些信的时候，把它从眼睛上摘落了下来。”

“那么，除开单片眼镜，我这模样大致照旧？”

===Original===

“You’ve got quit of your cold now, sir. You were quite hoarse that night you came to the house—as if you’d got a touch of sore throat or something like that.”

“That’s true. I’ve no cold now. Anything more?”

Mrs. Marple subjected him to another prolonged scrutiny.

===Chinese===

“您的伤风如今好了，先生。您来我家的那晚，嗓子哑得很——就像喉咙有点发炎什么的。”

“这倒是真话。我现在没有伤风。还有别的么？”

马普尔太太又对他细细端详了半晌。

===Original===

“No, sir. You’re just like you were that night.”

“And you recognised me from some newspaper portrait, it seems?”

“Yes, sir. I saw your picture in the evening paper once. It was just a head-and-shoulders one; but I’d have recognised you from it even if you hadn’t shown me your card.”

===Chinese===

“没有了，先生。您就跟那晚一个样。”

“这么说，你是凭报上的一幅肖像认出我的？”

“是的，先生。我有一次在晚报上见过您的照片。不过是张半身像；可即便您不出示名片，我凭那张像也认得出您。”

===Original===

Sir Clinton reflected for a moment.

“Can you remember what was on that card?” he asked.

Mrs. Marple consulted her memory.

===Chinese===

克林顿爵士沉吟了片刻。

“那张名片上印着什么，你还记得么？”他问。

马普尔太太翻检着自己的记忆。

===Original===

“It said: ‘Sir Clinton Driffield (and some letters after the name), Chief Constable.’ Then in the left-hand corner was the address: ‘Police Headquarters, Westerhaven.’ ”

Sir Clinton caught Flamborough’s eye and they exchanged glances. The Inspector had little difficulty in seeing that his first impression had been wrong. It was not the Chief Constable who had ransacked Avice Deepcar’s house.

Sir Clinton took out his card-case and handed a card to Mrs. Marple.

===Chinese===

“上面写的是：‘克林顿·德里菲尔德爵士（姓名后头还有几个字母），郡警察局长。’随后在左下角是地址：‘韦斯特黑文，警察总部。’”

克林顿爵士与弗拉姆伯勒目光相接，两人交换了个眼色。探长毫不费力地看出，自己最初的印象错了。把艾维斯·迪普卡的家翻了个底朝天的，并非郡警察局长。

克林顿爵士取出名片匣，抽出一张名片递给马普尔太太。

===Original===

“It wasn’t that card I showed you, was it?”

Mrs. Marple scanned the card for a moment.

“Oh, no, sir. This one reads quite different.”

===Chinese===

“我给您看的那张，不是这一张吧？”

马普尔太太把名片端详了片刻。

“哦，不是，先生。这一张的字样全然不同。”

===Original===

Sir Clinton nodded and took back the card.

“I think that’s really all I want to know, Mrs. Marple. Perhaps Inspector Flamborough may want to ask you a question or two later on.”

Avice Deepcar seemed by no means satisfied at this close to the interview.

===Chinese===

克林顿爵士点点头，把名片收了回去。

“我想，我要知道的实在就都齐了，马普尔太太。过一会儿，弗拉姆伯勒探长或许还要问你一两个问题。”

问话到此收场，艾维斯·迪普卡似乎远不满足。

===Original===

“That’s all very fine, Sir Clinton,” she said, “but you seem to think you’ve satisfied me. You haven’t. You can’t invade my house in this way and then pass the whole thing off as if it were part of your routine. And you can’t carry away a pile of my private letters and keep them without my consent. I insist on having them back. If you don’t, I’ll see my solicitor at once about the matter. And may I remind you again that you owe me some apology for your proceedings?”

Sir Clinton seemed in no way ruffled.

===Chinese===

“这一切说得倒轻巧，克林顿爵士，”她说，“可您似乎以为已经把我打发满意了。并没有。您不能这样闯进我的家，过后又把这桩事轻轻巧巧说成您例行公事的一部分。您也不能抱走一摞我的私人信件，不经我同意就扣下不还。我坚持把它们收回。您若不还，我立刻为此去见我的律师。还有，可否容我再提醒您一回——您这套行径，还欠我一份道歉呢？”

克林顿爵士似乎毫不动气。

===Original===

“Of course I apologise for anything I’ve done which may have inconvenienced you, Miss Deepcar. I’m quite sincere in saying that I very much regret that you should have been worried in this way. Nothing that I have done has been meant to throw any suspicion on you, I can assure you. As to the letters, I think your best plan will be to consult your solicitor as you suggest. Ask him to ring me up at once, and I’ll try to settle the matter as soon as we can. I’ve no wish to cause you any trouble—none whatever.”

Avice glanced suspiciously from him to the Inspector. It was evident that this solution did not satisfy her; but obviously she realised that nothing would be gained by attempting to argue the point.

===Chinese===

“迪普卡小姐，凡是我做过、可能给您添了不便的事，我自然道歉。我说非常遗憾您竟受到这般搅扰，这话出于万分诚意。我可以向您保证，我所做的一切，没有一样意在把嫌疑引到您身上。至于那些信件，我想您最好的办法，就是照您自己的意思去请教您的律师。请他即刻给我来电话，我一定设法尽早了结此事。我无意给您添任何麻烦——一丝一毫也没有。”

艾维斯狐疑地看看他，又看看探长。显然，这个了结并不能叫她满意；但同样显然，她明白再抓住这一点争辩下去，不会有任何好处。

===Original===

“Very well,” she said at last, “I’ll go straight to my solicitor now. You’ll hear from him very shortly.”

Sir Clinton held the door open for her and she passed out of the room, followed by Mrs. Marple. After a few seconds, the Chief Constable turned to Flamborough.

“What do you make of it all, Inspector?”

===Chinese===

“很好，”她终于说，“我这就径直去见我的律师。很快您就会得到他的信儿。”

克林顿爵士替她拉开门，她走出房间，马普尔太太跟在后面。过了几秒钟，郡警察局长转向弗拉姆伯勒。

“这一切您作何看法，探长？”

===Original===

“Well, sir, that Mrs. Marple seems to me honest enough, but not very bright.”

Sir Clinton nodded in assent.

“She recognised her visitor from his resemblance to some blurred newspaper portrait; and she recognised me from my resemblance to her visitor. That’s your idea?”

===Chinese===

“唔，先生，依我看，那位马普尔太太人够诚实，就是不大灵光。”

克林顿爵士点头赞同。

“她是凭来客同某幅模糊的报纸肖像的相似，认出了那位来客；又凭我同她那位来客的相似，认出了我。您是这个意思吧？”

===Original===

“It looks like it. I never saw you wearing a single eyeglass, sir. And it occurs to me that a single eyeglass helps to change the normal expression of a face owing to the wrinkling that you make in holding it in your eye. Also if it’s an unfamiliar thing, one would drop it when one began to read documents, so as not to be hampered by it.”

“True. I suppose that satisfies you—along with the faked visiting-card which was meant to impress her with the fact that a high official had descended on her—that I personally wasn’t mixed up in the business. I’ve the best of reasons for knowing that myself, of course, since I know I was elsewhere at the time. But what do you make of the raid?”

===Chinese===

“看来是这样。我从来没见您戴过单片眼镜，先生。而且我想到，单片眼镜会改变一张脸惯常的表情，因为要把它扣在眼窝里，脸上就得挤出皱纹来。再说，那若是个不惯用的物件，人一开始读文件就会把它放落，免得受它牵累。”

“不错。我想，这总算叫您信服了——连同那张伪造的拜会名片，那本是要让她震慑于一位大员驾临她宅上——我本人并未沾手这桩事。这一点，我自己自然有最充分的理由知道，因为我晓得那时候我在别处。不过，对那场突袭，您怎么看？”

===Original===

“Documents were what the man was after, obviously, sir.”

“It seems clear enough that he expected to get hold of something compromising amongst her correspondence. If you ask me, Inspector, Mr. Justice doesn’t seem to stick at much in his self-appointed task.”

===Chinese===

“那家伙图谋的显然是文件，先生。”

“看来相当清楚，他指望从她的通信里抓到什么见不得人的东西。您要问我的话，探长，这位‘正义’先生在他自封的使命里，似乎无所忌惮。”

===Original===

“I was pretty sure it was some of his work, sir. The Deepcar girl and Silverdale had a common interest in getting Mrs. Silverdale out of the way; there’s no doubt about that. And some people are perfect fools in what they put down on paper. It’s quite on the cards that Mr. Justice thought he might find something useful amongst Silverdale’s letters to Avice Deepcar.”

“He evidently found something which he thought worth taking away, at any rate,” Sir Clinton pointed out. “I had a notion that once you arrested Silverdale, things would begin to move faster. If Mr. Justice has got hold of any evidence, it’ll be in our hands before long, I’m prepared to bet.”

===Chinese===

“我本来就相当有把握，这是他的手笔之一，先生。迪普卡那姑娘同西尔弗代尔利害与共，都盼着西尔弗代尔太太让路，这一点毋庸置疑。况且有些人往纸上落笔的东西，蠢得可以。‘正义’先生以为能在西尔弗代尔写给艾维斯·迪普卡的信里翻出些有用的东西，这大有可能。”

“无论如何，他分明翻到了些他认为值得带走的东西，”克林顿爵士指出。“我原有这么个预感：您一逮捕西尔弗代尔，事情就会开始加速。倘若‘正义’先生抓到了什么证据，我敢打赌，不久它就会落到咱们手里。”

===Original===

“He’s saving us some trouble, if there *is* anything in writing,” the Inspector said, with a grin. “We would hardly have raided the Deepcar house on such a long chance as that; and he’s done the job for us.”

“A most useful and altruistic person, evidently,” Sir Clinton commented ironically. “Now what about the rest of the affair, Inspector? If you accept Miss Deepcar’s evidence, then the bottom’s out of your case against Silverdale. He couldn’t be with her and at the bungalow simultaneously.”

===Chinese===

“倘若当真*有*什么白纸黑字的东西，他倒是替咱们省了些事，”探长咧嘴一笑说。“如此渺茫的一线指望，咱们原不会为它出动去抄迪普卡的家；他把这活计替咱们干完了。”

“显然是位极有用处、又极有舍己精神的人物，”克林顿爵士讥诮地评论。“那么，案子的其余部分怎么看，探长？您若采信迪普卡小姐的证词，您控西尔弗代尔的案子就塌了底。他不可能既同她待在一处，又同时身在平房。”

===Original===

“Why should we accept her evidence at all?” Flamborough demanded crossly. “She had as much interest in getting Mrs. Silverdale out of the way as Silverdale himself had. Their interests are absolutely at one in the affair. It’s more than an even chance that she was his accomplice in the business—standing ready with this tale of hers to prove an alibi for him. I don’t reckon her statement was worth that!”

He snapped his fingers contemptuously.

“There’s something else, sir,” he continued. “This Mrs. Marple wasn’t at the house that night. What evidence is there that Silverdale and the Deepcar girl ever went home at all after they’d dined down town? There’s no corroboration of that story. Why not assume that the Deepcar girl was an actual accomplice on the spot? She and Silverdale may have driven out to the bungalow after dinner, and she may have stood at the window during the whole affair. There’s nothing against that, if you discount her story. My reading of the Deepcar girl is that she may be surface-shy, so to speak, but she’s got good strong fibre in her character underneath. Look how she faced up to you not ten minutes ago. Not much shyness about that.”

===Chinese===

“咱们凭什么要采信她的证词？”弗拉姆伯勒愠怒地反问。“在除掉西尔弗代尔太太这件事上，她的利害同西尔弗代尔本人一样大。这桩案子里，他们的利益完全一致。十有八九，她就是他这桩买卖里的同谋——预先备好她这套说辞，随时准备替他作不在场的证明。依我看，她那份陈述就值这么个价！”

他轻蔑地打了个响指。

“还有一层，先生，”他继续说。“这位马普尔太太那晚并不在宅子里。有什么证据表明，西尔弗代尔和迪普卡那姑娘在城里用过晚餐之后，当真回过家？那套说辞没有半点旁证。为什么不可以假定，迪普卡那姑娘就是亲临现场的同谋？她和西尔弗代尔很可能饭后驱车去了平房，整桩罪案期间，她或许就一直站在那扇窗前。若把她的说辞打上折扣，这一设想毫无抵触之处。我读迪普卡这姑娘是这么个看法：她这个人，不妨这样说，皮面上或许腼腆，底子里的性子却有一副坚韧的筋骨。瞧瞧不到十分钟之前，她是如何当面应付您的。那可没多少腼腆可言。”

===Original===

“I think I’d have been a bit stirred up myself, Inspector, if you came along in my absence and pawed over all my private possessions. One isn’t necessarily a scoundrel if one turns peevish over a thing of that sort.”

The Inspector let the point pass.

“Have you any notion who this Mr. Justice can be, sir?”

===Chinese===

“我想，探长，倘若您趁我不在登门，把我私人的物件件件扒拉个遍，我自己怕也会有点按捺不住。一个人为这种事发一通脾气，未必就是恶棍。”

探长把这一点放了过去。

“这位‘正义’先生可能是谁，先生，您心里可有什么眉目？”

===Original===

“I’ve a pretty fair notion, but it’s only a notion. Who stands to profit by the affair?”

Some recollection seemed to cross the Inspector’s mind.

“Spratton, of course, sir. And now I come to think of it, if you shaved off your moustache, he’s very like you in face and build. If Spratton’s going to collect his insurance on young Hassendean, then murder’s got to be proved.”

===Chinese===

“我有相当不错的眉目，不过终究只是个想法。这桩案子，谁会从中得利？”

某段记忆似乎掠过探长的心头。

“当然是斯普拉顿，先生。而且现在我想起来了，您若把唇髭剃掉，他的容貌和身量同您像得很。斯普拉顿若要领取小哈森丁那份保险金，那就非得把谋杀坐实不可。”

===Original===

“Well,” said Sir Clinton lightly. “I trust Mr. Spratton will get what he deserves in the matter.”

===Chinese===

“嗯，”克林顿爵士轻描淡写地说。“我相信，斯普拉顿先生在这件事上必能得到他应得的下场。”

## XVI Written Evidence / 十六 书面证据

===Original===

Inspector Flamborough had to wait a couple of days before his unknown ally, Justice, made any further move. It so happened that Sir Clinton was not at headquarters when the post brought the expected communication; and the Inspector had plenty of time to consider the fresh evidence, unbiased by his superior’s comments. As soon as the Chief Constable reappeared, Flamborough went to him to display the latest document in the case.

“This came by the midday post, sir,” he explained, laying some papers on the table. “It’s Mr. Justice again. The results of his raid on the Deepcar house, it seems.”

===Chinese===

弗拉姆伯勒探长不得不等了两天，他那位身份不明的盟友“正义”才有下一步动作。邮班送来那封预期中的函件时，恰逢克林顿爵士不在总部；探长于是有充裕的时间琢磨这份新证据，不受上司评论的先入之扰。郡警察局长一重新露面，弗拉姆伯勒便去找他，出示此案最新的一份文件。

“这是中午邮班送来的，先生，”他一边把几份文件摆到桌上，一边说明。“又是那位‘正义’。看来是他突袭迪普卡家宅的成果。”

===Original===

Sir Clinton picked up the packet and opened out the papers. Some photographic prints attracted his attention, but he laid them aside and turned first to a plain sheet of paper on which the now familiar letters from telegraph forms had been gummed. With some deliberation he read the message.

> I enclose photographs of part of the correspondence which has recently taken place between Dr. Silverdale and Miss Deepcar.
> Justice.

===Chinese===

克林顿爵士拿起纸包，摊开里面的文件。几张照片印片引起了他的注意，但他先把它们放到一边，去看一页素白的纸——上面用胶水贴着如今已为人熟知的、剪自电报纸的字母。他不慌不忙地把那条信息读了一遍。

> 兹附上西尔弗代尔医生与迪普卡小姐近来往来信件之一部分的照片。
> “正义”

===Original===

Sir Clinton gazed at the sheet for a moment or two, as though considering some matter unconnected with the message. At last he turned to the Inspector.

“I suppose you’ve tried this thing for fingerprints? No good, eh? I can still smell a faint whiff of rubber from it—off his gloves, I suppose.”

Flamborough shook his head in agreement with Sir Clinton’s surmise.

“Nothing on it whatever, sir,” he confirmed.

===Chinese===

克林顿爵士对着那页纸凝视了片刻，仿佛在思量与这条信息无关的某件事。末了，他才转向探长。

“我想你在这东西上验过指纹了吧？没用，嗯？我还能从上面闻到一丝淡淡的橡胶味——想必是他手套上蹭下来的。”

弗拉姆伯勒摇了摇头，表示赞同克林顿爵士的猜测。

“上面压根儿什么都没有，先生，”他证实道。

===Original===

The Chief Constable laid down the sheet of paper and took up one of the photographs. It was of ordinary half-plate size and showed a slightly reduced copy of one page of a letter.

> that things cannot go on any 

 longer in this way.
> The plan we talked over last 

 seems the best. When I have given 

 Hassendean hints about the use of 

 hyoscine, he will probably see for 

 himself how to get what he wants. 

 After that, it merely means watching 

 them, and I am sure that we shall soon 

 have her out of our way. It will be 

 very easy to make it seem intentional 

 on their part; and no one is likely 

 to look further than that.

===Chinese===

郡警察局长放下那页纸，拿起其中一张照片。照片是普通的半版尺寸，显示的是一封信中一页略微缩小的复制件。

> 事情不能再这样 

 继续下去了。
> 我们上次谈过的那个计划 

 似乎最好。等我给哈森丁 

 一些关于使用 

 东莨菪碱的暗示，他多半会自己 

 看出怎样得到他想要的。 

 之后，只需盯着他们， 

 而且我确信我们很快就会 

 让她不再碍事。要 

 让这事看来是他们 

 蓄意为之易如反掌；而且谁也不大 

 会再深究下去。

===Original===

Flamborough watched the Chief Constable’s face as he read the message, and as soon as he saw that Sir Clinton had completed his perusal of it, the Inspector put in his word.

“I’ve checked the writing, sir. It’s Silverdale’s beyond any doubt.”

The Chief Constable nodded rather absentmindedly and took up another of the prints. This showed a largely-magnified reproduction of the first two lines of the document; and for a minute or so Sir Clinton subjected the print to a minute scrutiny with a magnifying glass.

===Chinese===

弗拉姆伯勒注视着郡警察局长读那条信息时的脸色，一见克林顿爵士已读毕，探长便插了话。

“笔迹我核对过了，先生。千真万确是西尔弗代尔的。”

郡警察局长有些心不在焉地点了点头，又拿起另一张印片。这张显示的是文件开头两行放大了许多倍的复制品；克林顿爵士用放大镜对着印片细察了一分钟上下。

===Original===

“It’s an original, right enough,” Flamborough ventured to comment at last. “Mr. Justice has been very thorough, and he’s given us quite enough to prove that it isn’t a forgery. You can see there’s no sign of erasing or scraping of any sort on the paper of the original; and the magnification’s big enough to show up anything of that sort.”

“That’s true,” Sir Clinton admitted. “And so far as one can see, the lines of the writing are normal. There are none of those halts-in-the-wrong-place that a forger makes if he traces a manuscript. The magnification’s quite big enough to show up anything of that sort. I guess you’re right, Inspector, it’s a photograph of part of a real document in Silverdale’s own handwriting.”

“The rest of the things make that clear enough,” Flamborough said, indicating several other prints which showed microphotographic reproductions of a number of other details of the document. “There’s no doubt whatever that these are all genuine bits of Silverdale’s handwriting. There’s been no faking of the paper or anything like that.”

===Chinese===

“这可是原件，错不了，”弗拉姆伯勒终于斗胆评论道。“那位‘正义’办事非常周密，给我们的东西足以证明这不是伪造。您看得出，原件的纸面上丝毫没有涂擦刮改之类的痕迹；而且放大倍数够大，那类痕迹若有，必会显形。”

“这话不假，”克林顿爵士承认。“而且就所见而言，笔迹的线条都正常。没有临摹手稿的伪造者笔下那种落错位置的停顿。这放大倍数完全足以显出任何此类破绽。我想你说得对，探长，这是一份真正出自西尔弗代尔手笔的文件局部的照片。”

“其余这些东西把这一点说明得够清楚了，”弗拉姆伯勒说，一面指着另外几张印片，上面是文件其他若干细节的显微摄影复制品。“这些都是西尔弗代尔真迹的片段，毫无疑问。纸张没做过手脚，也没有任何类似的猫腻。”

===Original===

Sir Clinton continued his study of the photographs, evidently with keen interest; but at last he put all the prints on his desk and turned to the Inspector.

“Well, what do you make of it?” he demanded.

===Chinese===

克林顿爵士继续研究那些照片，显然兴致颇浓；但末了他把所有印片搁到桌上，转向探长。

“那么，你从中看出什么名堂了？”他问。

===Original===

“It seems clear enough to me,” Flamborough answered. “Look at the contents of that page as a whole. It’s as plain as one could wish. Silverdale and the Deepcar girl have had enough of waiting. Things can’t go on any longer in this way. They’ve been discussing various ways of getting rid of Mrs. Silverdale. ‘The plan we talked over last seems the best.’ That’s the final decision, evidently. Then you get a notion of what the plan was. Silverdale was going to prime Hassendean with information about hyoscine, and practically egg him on to drug Mrs. Silverdale so as to get her into his power. Then when the trap was ready, Silverdale and the Deepcar girl were to be on the alert to take advantage of the situation. And the last sentence makes it clear enough that they meant to go the length of murder and cover it up by making it look like a suicide-pact between young Hassendean and Mrs. Silverdale. That’s how I read it, sir.”

===Chinese===

“在我看来够清楚的了，”弗拉姆伯勒答道。“把那一页的内容当作整体看。再明白不过了。西尔弗代尔和迪普卡那姑娘已经等得不耐烦了。事情不能再这样继续下去。他们一直在商量除掉西尔弗代尔太太的种种办法。‘我们上次谈过的那个计划似乎最好。’显然，这就是最后的定案。接下来你就能约略知道那计划是什么。西尔弗代尔打算把东莨菪碱的有关知识预先灌输给哈森丁，实则就是怂恿他给西尔弗代尔太太下药，好把她置于自己掌控之中。待陷阱布妥，西尔弗代尔和迪普卡那姑娘便警醒待命，坐收其成。最后一句也说得够明白：他们打算走到谋杀那一步，再把它伪装成年轻的哈森丁与西尔弗代尔太太之间的殉情之约，掩盖过去。我就是这么解读的，先生。”

===Original===

Sir Clinton did not immediately endorse this opinion. Instead, he picked up the full copy of the manuscript page and studied it afresh as though searching for something in particular. At last he appeared to be satisfied; and he slid the photograph across the desk to the Inspector.

“I don’t wish to bias you, Inspector, so I won’t describe what I see myself. But will you examine the word ‘probably’ in that text and tell me if anything whatever about it strikes you as peculiar—anything whatever, remember.”

===Chinese===

克林顿爵士没有立刻附和这个看法。他反而拿起那页手稿的完整照片，重新研究起来，仿佛在搜寻什么特别的东西。末了他似乎满意了，便把照片从桌面推给探长。

“我不想先入为主地左右你，探长，所以我不描述我自己看出了什么。但请你检视正文里的‘probably’一词，告诉我它有没有任何一点让你觉得异样——记住，任何一点都算。”

===Original===

Flamborough studied the place indicated, first with his naked eye and then with the magnifying glass.

“There’s no sign of any tampering with the paper that I can see, sir. The surface is intact and the ink lines run absolutely freely, without the halts and shakes one would expect in a forgery. The only thing I do notice is that the word looks just a trifle cramped.”

“That’s what I wanted. Note that it’s in the middle of a line, Inspector. Now look at the word ‘shall’ in the fifth line from the bottom of the page.”

===Chinese===

弗拉姆伯勒研究所指之处，先用肉眼，再用放大镜。

“依我看，纸上没有任何做手脚的痕迹，先生。纸面完好无损，墨水线条流畅自如，没有伪造品中料想会有的停顿与颤抖。我唯一注意到的，是那个词看上去就是有点儿挤。”

“我要的就是这个。注意它在一行的中间，探长。现在再看页底倒数第五行里的‘shall’一词。”

===Original===

“One might say it was a trifle cramped too,” Flamborough admitted.

“And the ‘it’ in the third line from the foot?”

“It looks like the same thing.”

===Chinese===

“也可以说它有点儿挤，”弗拉姆伯勒承认。

“还有倒数第三行的‘it’呢？”

“看起来是一回事。”

===Original===

Flamborough relapsed into silence and studied the photograph word by word while Sir Clinton waited patiently.

“The word ‘the’ in the phrase ‘about the use of hyoscine’ seems cramped too; and the ‘to’ at the start of the last line suffers in the same way. It’s so slight in all these cases that one wouldn’t notice it normally. I didn’t see it till you pointed it out. But if you’re going to suggest that there’s been any erasing and writing in fresh words to fit the blank space, I’ll have to disagree with you, sir. I simply don’t believe there’s been anything of the sort.”

===Chinese===

弗拉姆伯勒重又沉默下来，逐字研究那张照片，克林顿爵士则耐心等候。

“‘about the use of hyoscine’这个短语里的‘the’似乎也挤；末行开头的‘to’也有同样的毛病。这些情形都轻微得很，平常根本不会留意，不等你指出我是看不见的。可您要是想暗示有人擦去了什么、再补写新词去凑空白，那我只能不同意了，先生。我压根儿不相信有过这种事。”

===Original===

“I shan’t differ from you over that,” Sir Clinton assured him blandly. “Now let’s think of something else for a change. Did it never occur to you, Inspector, how much the English language depends on the relative positions of words? If I say: ‘It struck you,’ that means something quite different from: ‘You struck it.’ And yet each sentence contains exactly the same words.”

“That’s plain enough,” Flamborough admitted, “though I never thought of it in that way. And,” he added in a dubious tone, “I don’t see what it’s got to do with the case, either.”

===Chinese===

“在这一点上我不与你争，”克林顿爵士温言宽慰他。“现在换换脑子，想点别的吧。你可曾想过，探长，英语在多大程度上依赖词语的相对位置？如果我说：‘It struck you’，它与‘You struck it’的意思大不相同。可两个句子里包含的词分毫不差。”

“这够明白的，”弗拉姆伯勒承认，“尽管我从来没那样想过。而且，”他以犹疑的口吻补了一句，“我也不明白这跟案子有什么相干。”

===Original===

“That’s a pity,” Sir Clinton observed with a sympathy which hardly sounded genuine. “Suppose we think it over together. Where does one usually cramp words a trifle when one is writing?”

“At the end of a line,” Flamborough suggested. “But these crampings seem to be all in the middle of the lines of that letter.”

“That’s what seems to me interesting about them,” Sir Clinton explained drily. “And somehow it seems to associate itself in my mind with the fact that Mr. Justice hasn’t supplied us with the original document, but has gone to all the trouble of taking photographs of it.”

===Chinese===

“那就可惜了，”克林顿爵士说道，语气里的同情听起来很难说真诚。“不如我们一起想想。写字的时候，人通常在什么地方会把词写得挤一点儿？”

“在一行的末尾，”弗拉姆伯勒提议道。“可这些挤缩之处，在那封信里似乎全落在一行的中间。”

“这正是我觉得它们有趣的地方，”克林顿爵士冷冷地解释道。“不知怎么，这在我脑子里总跟一件事联在一起：‘正义’没有把原件提供给我们，却不惮其烦地给它拍了照片。”

===Original===

“I wondered at that, myself,” the Inspector confessed. “It seems a bit futile, true enough.”

“Try a fresh line, Inspector. We learned on fairly good authority that Mr. Justice took away a number of letters from Miss Deepcar’s house. And yet he only sends us a single page out of the lot. If the rest were important, why doesn’t he send them. If they aren’t important, why did he take them away?”

“He may be holding them up for use later on, sir.”

===Chinese===

“我自己也对这一点感到纳闷，”探长坦言。“确实显得有点儿多此一举。”

“换条新思路吧，探长。我们得到相当可靠的消息，‘正义’从迪普卡小姐家拿走了若干封信。可他寄给我们的却只有其中单独一页。要是其余的重要，他为什么不寄来。要是不重要，他又为什么拿走？”

“他也许是留着以后再用，先生。”

===Original===

Sir Clinton shook his head.

“My reading of the business is different. I think this is Mr. Justice’s last reserve. He’s throwing his last forces into the battle now.”

“There seems to be something behind all this,” Flamborough admitted, passing his hand over his hair as though to stimulate his brain by the action, “but I can’t just fit it all together as you seem to have done, sir. You can say what you like, but that handwriting’s genuine; the paper’s not been tampered with; and I can’t see anything wrong with it.”

===Chinese===

克林顿爵士摇了摇头。

“我对这件事的读法不同。我认为这是‘正义’最后的预备队。他现在正把最后的兵力投入战斗。”

“这一切背后似乎另有文章，”弗拉姆伯勒承认，一只手抹过头发，仿佛想借这个动作刺激脑子，“可我没法像您那样把一切都串拢来，先生。随您怎么说，那笔迹是真的；纸没被动过手脚；我看不出它有什么毛病。”

===Original===

Sir Clinton took pity on the inspector’s obvious anxiety.

“Look at the phrasing of the whole document, Inspector.
