If you cared to do so, you could split it up into a set of phrases something after this style: ‘that things cannot go on any longer in this way. … The plan we talked over last seems the best. … When I have given … Hassendean … hints … about the … use of … hyoscine … he will probably see for himself how … to get what he wants. … After that, it merely means … watching them … and I am sure that … we shall soon have … her … out of our way. … It will be very easy … to make it seem … intentional … on their part … and no one is likely … to look further than that.’ Now, Inspector, if you met any one of these phrases by itself, would you infer from it inevitably that a murder was being planned? ‘Things cannot go on any longer in this way.’ If you consider how Mrs. Silverdale was behaving with young Hassendean, it’s not astonishing to find a phrase like that in a letter from Silverdale to the girl he was in love with. ‘The plan we talked over last seems the best.’ It might have been a day’s outing together that he was talking about for all one can tell. ‘He will probably see for himself how my wife is playing with him.’ And so forth.”

===Chinese===

克林顿爵士见探长焦灼之色明显，动了怜悯之心。

“看整份文件的措辞，探长。只要你愿意，可以把它拆成一组短语，大致是这么个样式：‘事情不能再这样继续下去了。……我们上次谈过的那个计划似乎最好。……等我给……哈森丁……些暗示……关于……使用……东莨菪碱……他多半会自己看出……怎样得到他想要的。……之后，那只是意味着……盯着他们……而且我确信……我们很快就会……让她……不再碍事。……那将非常容易……让这事显得……出于蓄意……在他们那方面……而且谁也不大……会再深究下去。’好了，探长，倘若你单独遇上这些短语中的任何一条，你会由它必然推断出有人正在策划一桩谋杀吗？‘事情不能再这样继续下去了。’想想西尔弗代尔太太与年轻的哈森丁之间的做派，在西尔弗代尔写给他恋慕的姑娘的信里见到这样一句话，并不出奇。‘我们上次谈过的那个计划似乎最好。’没准他说的只是一起出去游玩一天的事，谁说得准呢。‘他多半会自己看出我太太是怎样玩弄他的。’如此等等。”

===Original===

“Yes, that’s all very well,” Flamborough put in, “but what about the word ‘hyoscine?’ That’s unusual in love-letters.”

“Miss Deepcar was working on hyoscine under Silverdale’s directions, remember. It’s quite possible that he might have mentioned it incidentally.”

“Now I think I see what you mean, sir. You think that this document that Mr. Justice has sent us is a patchwork—bits cut out of a lot of different letters and stuck together and then photographed?”

===Chinese===

“是，这些都言之成理，”弗拉姆伯勒插话道，“可是‘东莨菪碱’这个词怎么说？情书里出现它可不多见。”

“别忘了，迪普卡小姐当时正在西尔弗代尔指导下做东莨菪碱的研究。他顺笔提到它，全然可能。”

“现在我想我明白您的意思了，先生。您是认为，‘正义’寄给我们的这份文件是拼凑货——从许多封不同的信里剪下只言片语，粘贴到一起，然后拍成照片？”

===Original===

“I’m suggesting it as a possibility, Inspector. See how it fits the facts. Here are a set of phrases, each one innocuous in itself, but with a cumulative effect of suggestion when you string them together as in this document. If the thing is a patchwork, then a number of real letters must have been used in order to get fragments which would suit. So Mr. Justice took a fair selection of epistles with him when he raided Miss Deepcar’s house. Further, in snipping out a sentence here and there from these letters, he sometimes had to include a phrase running on from one line to another in the original letter; but when he came to paste his fragments together, the original hiatus at the end of a line got transferred to the middle of a line in the final arrangement made to fit the page of the faked letter. That’s what struck me to begin with. For example, suppose that in the original letter you had the phrase: ‘he will probably see for himself how’; and the original line ended with ‘probably.’ That word might be a bit cramped at the end of the line. But in reconstructing the thing, ‘probably’ got into the middle of the line, and so you get this apparently meaningless cramping of the word when there was space enough for it to be written uncramped under normal conditions. Just the same with the other cases you spotted for yourself. They represent the ends of lines in the original letters, although they all occur in the middle of lines in the fake production.”

===Chinese===

“我是把它作为一种可能性提出，探长。看看它多么切合事实。这里有一组短语，每条单独看都无伤大雅，可像这份文件这样串联起来，便产生层层叠加的暗示效果。倘若这东西是拼凑的，那就必定用掉了若干封真信，才凑得出合用的碎片。所以‘正义’突袭迪普卡小姐家宅时，随身带走了一批经过挑选的书信。再者，从这些信里东剪一句西剪一句时，他有时不得不连带把原信中从一行末尾跨到下一行的短语剪进来；可等他把碎片粘贴到一起时，原本位于行末的断口就转移到了行中，被排进伪造信那一页的版面。这就是起初叫我起疑的地方。举个例子，假定原信里有这样一个短语：‘he will probably see for himself how’；而原信那一行恰以‘probably’收尾。这个词在行末可能写得有点儿挤。但在重构这东西时，‘probably’落到了一行的中间，于是你就见到这个表面看来毫无来由的挤缩——本来在正常条件下，空间尽够它舒舒展展地写。你自己察觉出的那几处也是同理。它们都代表原信里的行末，虽然在伪造品里全都出现在一行的中间。”

===Original===

“That sounds just as plausible as you like, sir. But you’ve got the knack of making things sound plausible. You’re not pulling my leg, are you?” the Inspector demanded suspiciously. “Besides, what about there being no sign of the paper having been tampered with?”

“Look at what he’s given us,” Sir Clinton suggested. “The only case where he’s given a large-scale reproduction of a whole phrase is at the top of the letter: ‘Things cannot go on any longer in this way.’ That’s been complete in the original, and he gives you a large-scale copy of it showing that the texture of the paper is intact. Of course it is, since he cut the whole bit out of the letter en bloc. When it comes to the microphotographs, of course he only shows you small bits of the words and so there’s no sign of the cutting that was needed at the end of each fragment. And in the photograph of the full text, there’s no attempt to show you fine details. He simply pasted the fragments in their proper order on to a real sheet of notepaper, filled up the joins with Chinese White to hide the solutions of continuity, and used a process plate which wouldn’t show the slight differences in the shades of the whites where the Chinese White overlay the white of the notepaper. If you have a drawing to make for black-and-white reproduction in a book, you can mess about with Chinese White as much as you like, and it won’t show up in the final result at all.”

===Chinese===

“这话要多可信有多可信，先生。可您就有本事把话说得天花乱坠。您不是在耍我吧？”探长狐疑地追问。“再说，纸上不见做手脚的痕迹，这又怎么讲？”

“看看他给了我们什么，”克林顿爵士提示道。“唯一一处给出整条短语大比例复制品的地方，是信的顶端：‘Things cannot go on any longer in this way.’（事情不能再这样继续下去了。）这一句在原件里是完整的，他给你一份大比例拷贝，显示纸的质地完好无损。当然完好，因为这一整块是他原封不动从信上剪下来的。至于显微照片，他当然只给你看词语的小小局部，于是每个碎片末端所需的裁剪痕迹一概不见。而全文照片呢，根本无意让你看清细节。他只是把碎片按应有的顺序贴到一张真正的信纸上，用中国白颜料填平接缝、遮掉断口，再改用制版干版拍摄——这种干版显不出中国白覆盖信纸白底处两种白色深浅的细微差别。你要是为书里的黑白复制件画图，尽可以随便拿中国白涂涂改改，成稿上一点儿也显不出来。”

===Original===

Flamborough, with a gesture, admitted the plausibility of Sir Clinton’s hypothesis.

“And you think that explains why he didn’t send us the original document, sir?”

“Since I’m sure he hadn’t an original to send, it’s hard to see how he could have sent it, Inspector.”

Flamborough did not contest this reading of the case. Instead, he passed to a fresh aspect of the subject.

===Chinese===

弗拉姆伯勒做了个手势，认可克林顿爵士假说的可信性。

“那么您认为，这就解释了他为什么不把原件寄给我们，先生？”

“既然我确信他根本没有什么原件可寄，那就很难想象他怎么寄得出原件来，探长。”

弗拉姆伯勒没有争辩这一解读。他转而谈起话题的另一面。

===Original===

“Mr. Justice is evidently ready to go any length to avenge somebody—and that somebody can hardly have been young Hassendean, judging from what we’ve heard about his character.”

Sir Clinton refused the gambit offered by the Inspector.

“Mr. Justice is a very able person,” he observed, “even though he does make a mistake now and again, as in this last move.”

===Chinese===

“那位‘正义’显然不惜走任何极端也要替什么人报仇——而就我们听说的他的为人来看，那个人很难是年轻的哈森丁。”

克林顿爵士不接探长抛出的这个试探。

“‘正义’是个非常干练的人，”他评说道，“尽管他也时不时失一手，比如这最后一步。”

===Original===

“You said you’d some idea who he was, sir?” Flamborough said with an interrogative note in his voice.

The Chief Constable showed no desire to be drawn. He glanced rather quizzically at his subordinate for a moment before speaking.

“I’ll give you the points which strike me in that connection, Inspector; and then you’ll be just as well placed as I am myself in the matter of Mr. Justice. First of all, if you compare the time of publication of the morning newspapers with the time, at which Mr. Justice’s telegram was collected from the pillar-box, I think it’s fairly evident that he didn’t depend on the journalists for his first information about the affair. Even the Ivy Lodge news wasn’t printed until after he had despatched his message.”

===Chinese===

“您说过对他的身份已有些眉目了，先生？”弗拉姆伯勒说道，话音里带着探询。

郡警察局长毫无被套出话来的意思。开口之前，他先似笑非笑地朝下属瞥了片刻。

“我把在这方面引起我注意的几点说给你，探长；这样，在‘正义’其人这件事上，你所处的位置就跟我一样有利了。首先，如果你把晨报的出版时间与‘正义’那封电报从邮筒里取出的时间两相对照，我想相当明显：关于此案的第一手消息，他并不是从记者那里得来的。连常春藤小屋的新闻，也是在他发出那条消息之后才见报的。”

===Original===

“That’s true, sir,” Flamborough admitted.

His manner showed that he expected a good deal more than this tittle of information.

“Therefore he must have had some direct information about the bungalow business. Either he was on the spot when the affair occurred, or else he was told about it almost immediately by someone who was on the spot.”

“Admitted,” the inspector confirmed.

===Chinese===

“这不假，先生，”弗拉姆伯勒承认。

他的神态表明，他期待的可远不止这一星半点的信息。

“因此，关于平房那桩事，他必定掌握了某种直接消息。要么事发时他就在现场，要么几乎在第一时间就有个在现场的人告诉了他。”

“承认，”探长确认道。

===Original===

“Then he obviously—or is it ‘she obviously,’ Inspector?—saw the importance of hyoscine as a clue as soon as any word about it got into the newspapers. Immediately, in comes the code advertisement, giving us—rather unnecessarily I think—the tip to inquire at the Croft-Thornton Institute.”

Flamborough’s face showed that he felt Sir Clinton was merely recapitulating very obvious pieces of evidence.

“Then there was the writing on the advertisements which he sent to the papers—Mrs. Silverdale’s writing rather neatly forged, if you remember.”

===Chinese===

“其次，他显然——或者该说‘她显然’，探长？——早在东莨菪碱一词见报的那一刻，就看出了它作为一条线索的重要性。紧接着，密码广告就来了，还给了我们一条去克罗夫特-索顿研究所查问的提示——依我看，这条提示给得未免多余。”

弗拉姆伯勒脸上的神情表明，他觉得克林顿爵士不过在复述一些明摆着的证据。

“还有他寄到各报的那些广告上的字迹——如果你记得的话，那是对西尔弗代尔太太笔迹相当利落的仿造。”

===Original===

“Yes,” said the Inspector, showing by his tone that at this point he was rather at sea.

“Then there was the fact that he managed to choose his time most conveniently for his raid on Miss Deepcar’s house.”

“You mean he made his visit when only the maid was at home, sir?”

“Precisely. I rather admire his forethought all through the business. But there’s more in it than that, if you think it over, Inspector?”

===Chinese===

“是，”探长应道，语气显示出他到这一步已经颇为茫然。

“再有，他突袭迪普卡小姐家宅的时间，也挑得再顺手不过。”

“您是说他上门时家里只有女仆在，先生？”

“正是。整件事里他处处的深谋远虑，我相当佩服。不过再往深处想想，里头还有名堂，探长？”

===Original===

“Well, sir, if your reading’s correct, he wanted some of Silverdale’s letters to serve as a basis for these photographs.”

“Something even more obvious than that, Inspector. Now, with all that evidence in front of you, can’t you build up some sort of picture of Mr. Justice? You ought to be able to come fairly near it, I think.”

“Somebody fairly in the swim with the Silverdale crowd, at any rate. I can see that. And someone who knew the Croft-Thornton by hearsay, at any rate. Is that what you mean, sir?”

===Chinese===

“这个嘛，先生，如果您的解读正确，他是需要一些西尔弗代尔的信，作这些照片的底本。”

“还有比这更明显的东西，探长。好了，这些证据都摆在你眼前，你就拼不出一幅‘正义’其人的画像吗？我想你应该能画得八九不离十。”

“至少是跟西尔弗代尔那伙人过从甚密、混得很开的人。这个我看得出。而且至少是个对克罗夫特-索顿研究所有所耳闻的人。您指的是这个吗，先生？”

===Original===

Sir Clinton betrayed nothing in his expression, though the Inspector scrutinised his face carefully; but he added something which Flamborough had not expected.

“Final points. The date on the fragment of an envelope that I found in the drawer in Mrs. Silverdale’s room was 1925. The date inside that signet-ring on her finger was 5–11–25. And there was the initial *B* engraved alongside the date.”

Inspector Flamborough quite obviously failed to see the relevancy of these details. His face showed it in the most apparent way.

===Chinese===

虽然探长仔细端详他的脸，克林顿爵士的神色没露半点口风；但他补了一句弗拉姆伯勒始料未及的话。

“最后几点。我在西尔弗代尔太太房间抽屉里发现的那片信封残片，上面的日期是 1925。她手指上那枚图章戒指内侧刻的日期是 5–11–25。日期旁边还刻着一个首字母*B*。”

弗拉姆伯勒探长显然全然看不出这些细节有什么干系。他的脸把这一点表现得再明白不过。

===Original===

“I don’t see what you’re getting at there, sir,” he said rather shamefacedly. “These things never struck me; and even now I don’t see what they’ve got to do with Mr. Justice.”

If he expected to gain anything by this frank confession, he was disappointed. Sir Clinton had evidently no desire to save his subordinate the trouble of thinking, and his next remark left Flamborough even deeper in bewilderment.

“Ever read anything by Dean Swift, Inspector?”

===Chinese===

“我不明白您那话指的是什么，先生，”他颇有些赧然地说。“这些事我从没留意过；即便是现在，我也看不出它们跟‘正义’有什么相干。”

倘若他指望凭这番坦白换回点什么，那他失望了。克林顿爵士显然无意替下属省去思考之劳，而他的下一句话让弗拉姆伯勒愈发如坠云雾。

“读过斯威夫特教长的什么著作吗，探长？”

===Original===

“I read *Gulliver’s Travels* when I was a kid, sir,” Flamborough admitted, with the air of deprecating any investigation into his literary tastes.

“You might read his *Journal to Stella* some time. But I guess you’d find it dull. It’s a reprint of his letters to Esther Johnson. He called her ‘Stella,’ and it’s full of queer abbreviations and phrases like ‘Night, dear M.D. Love Pdfr.’ It teems with that sort of stuff. Curious to see the human side of a man like Swift, isn’t it?”

“In love with her, you mean, sir?”

===Chinese===

“小时候读过《格列佛游记》，先生，”弗拉姆伯勒承认，那副神气，是不希望有人追究他的文学趣味。

“你有空不妨读读他的《致斯特拉日记》。不过我看你会觉得它乏味。那是他写给埃丝特·约翰逊的信件辑印本。他称她‘斯特拉’，信里满是古怪的缩略语和短语，比如‘Night, dear M.D. Love Pdfr.’。通篇都是这类玩意儿。能窥见斯威夫特这样一个人富有人情味的一面，挺有意思的，是不是？”

“您是说，他爱上了她，先生？”

===Original===

“Well, it sounds like it,” Sir Clinton replied cautiously. “However, we needn’t worry over Swift. Let’s see if we can’t do something with this case, for a change.”

He glanced at his watch.

“Half-past five. We may be able to get hold of her.”

He picked up the telephone from his desk and asked for a number while Flamborough waited with interest to hear the result.

===Chinese===

“嗯，听上去像是那么回事，”克林顿爵士谨慎地答道。“不过，咱们犯不着为斯威夫特操心。换个方向，看看能不能在这个案子上做点什么。”

他瞥了一眼手表。

“五点半。我们也许还来得及找到她。”

他从桌上拿起电话，报了一个号码，弗拉姆伯勒饶有兴致地等着听结果。

===Original===

“Is that the Croft-Thornton Institute?” Sir Clinton demanded at length. “Sir Clinton Driffield speaking. Can you ask Miss Hailsham to come to the telephone?”

There was a pause before he spoke once more.

“Miss Hailsham? I’m sorry to trouble you, but can you tell me if there’s a microphotographic camera in the Institute? I’d like to know.”

Flamborough, all ears, waited for the next bit of the one-sided conversation which was reaching him.

===Chinese===

“是克罗夫特-索顿研究所吗？”克林顿爵士终于开口。“我是克林顿·德里菲尔德爵士。可否请海尔沙姆小姐来接电话？”

停了片刻，他才再度开口。

“是海尔沙姆小姐？抱歉打扰，能否告诉我，研究所里有没有显微摄影机？我想知道。”

弗拉姆伯勒竖起耳朵，等着这通单边谈话传到他这边的下文。

===Original===

“You have two of them? Then I suppose I might be able to get permission to use one of them, perhaps, if we need it. … Thanks, indeed. By the way, I suppose you’re just leaving the Institute now. … I thought so. Very lucky I didn’t miss you by a minute or two. I mustn’t detain you. Thanks again. Goodbye.”

He put down the telephone and turned to Flamborough.

“You might ask Miss Morcott to come here, Inspector.”

===Chinese===

“有两台？那么，倘若我们用得着，我想我或许可以获准借用其中一台。……多谢，真的。顺便问一句，您现在正要离开研究所吧。……我就料到。真走运，没差一两分钟错过您。不敢再耽搁您。再次感谢。再见。”

他放下电话，转向弗拉姆伯勒。

“麻烦你去请莫科特小姐到这儿来一趟，探长。”

===Original===

Flamborough, completely puzzled by this move, opened the door of the adjoining room and summoned Sir Clinton’s typist.

“I want you to telephone for me, Miss Morcott,” the Chief Constable explained. “Ring up Dr. Trevor Markfield at his house. When you get through, say to his housekeeper: ‘Miss Hailsham speaking. Please tell Dr. Markfield that I wish to see him tonight and that I shall come round to his house at nine o’clock.’ Don’t say any more than that, and get disconnected before there’s any chance of explanations.”

Miss Morcott carried out Sir Clinton’s orders carefully and then went back to her typing. As soon as the door closed behind her, the Inspector’s suppressed curiosity got the better of him.

===Chinese===

弗拉姆伯勒对这一手全然摸不着头脑，打开隔壁房间的门，唤来了克林顿爵士的打字员。

“我要请你替我打个电话，莫科特小姐，”郡警察局长解释道。“往特雷弗·马克菲尔德医生家里打。接通后，对他的管家说：‘我是海尔沙姆小姐。请转告马克菲尔德医生，我想今晚见他，九点钟我到他府上去。’不要多说一个字，趁对方还来不及问什么就挂断。”

莫科特小姐一丝不苟地执行了克林顿爵士的指示，随后回去继续打字。她前脚出门、门刚在身后合上，探长憋了半晌的好奇心便再也按捺不住。

===Original===

“I don’t quite understand all that, sir. I suppose you asked about the photomicrographic affair just to see if these prints could have been made at the Croft-Thornton?”

“I hadn’t much doubt on that point. Photomicrographic apparatus isn’t common among amateur photographers, but it’s common enough in scientific institutes. No, I was really killing two birds with one stone: finding out about the micro-camera and making sure that Miss Hailsham was leaving the place for the night and wouldn’t have a chance to speak to Markfield before she went.”

“And what about her calling on Markfield tonight, sir?”

===Chinese===

“这一整套我不大明白，先生。我猜您问显微摄影设备那一茬，只是想看看这些印片有没有可能是在克罗夫特-索顿制作的？”

“这一点我原本就没有多少疑问。显微摄影设备在业余摄影者中间并不常见，可在科研机构里就寻常得很了。不，我其实是地地道道的一石二鸟：既摸清了显微摄影机的情况，又确认了海尔沙姆小姐当晚就要离开那里，走之前没机会跟马克菲尔德说话。”

“那么她今晚拜访马克菲尔德这一出呢，先生？”

===Original===

“She’ll have to do it by proxy, I’m afraid. We’ll represent her, however inefficiently, Inspector. The point is that I wanted to be sure that Markfield would be at home when we called; and I wished to avoid making an appointment in my own name lest it should put him too much on his guard. The time’s come when we’ll have to persuade Dr. Markfield to be a bit franker than he’s been, hitherto. I think I see my way to getting out of him most of what he knows; and if I can succeed in that, then we ought to have all the evidence we need.”

He paused, as though not very sure about something.

“He’s been bluffing us all along the line up to the present, Inspector. It’s a game two can play at; and you’ll be good enough to turn a deaf ear occasionally if I’m tempted out of the straight path. And whatever happens, don’t look over-surprised at anything I may say. If you can contrive to look thoroughly stupid, it won’t do any harm.”

===Chinese===

“恐怕她只好由人代劳了。我们就代她走这一遭——哪怕代得不怎么样，探长。要点在于，我必须确定我们登门时马克菲尔德在家；而我不想以我自己的名义预约，免得他戒备过深。时候到了，我们得劝马克菲尔德医生比迄今为止坦白得再多一点儿。我想我有法子让他说出他所知道的大部分；倘若这一点能成，我们需要的证据就该齐备了。”

他顿了顿，仿佛对什么事不太有把握。

“到眼下为止，他一路都在跟我们虚张声势，探长。这种把戏两个人都能玩；倘若我一时受了诱惑、越出正道，请你务必偶尔装聋作哑。还有，无论发生什么，对我可能说出的任何话都别显得太过惊讶。你若能设法装出一副彻底的蠢相，那只有好处没有坏处。”

## XVII Mr. Justice / 十七 “正义”

===Original===

Just before entering the road in which Markfield lived, Sir Clinton drew up his car; and as he did so, a constable in plain clothes stepped forward.

“Dr. Markfield’s in his house, sir,” he announced. “He came home just before dinnertime.”

Sir Clinton nodded, let in his clutch, and drove round the corner to Markfield’s gate. As he stopped his engine, he glanced at the house-front.

===Chinese===

快驶进马克菲尔德住的那条街时，克林顿爵士把车停了下来；他刚停稳，一名便衣警员便迎上前来。

“马克菲尔德医生在家，先生，”他报告道。“他晚饭前刚回来。”

克林顿爵士点点头，松开离合器，驾车转过街角，开到马克菲尔德家门口。引擎一熄，他打量了一下房子的正面。

===Original===

“Note that his garage is built into the house, Inspector,” he pointed out. “That seems of interest, if there’s a door from the house direct into the garage, I think.”

They walked up the short approach and rang the bell. In a few moments the door was opened by Markfield’s housekeeper. Rather to her surprise, Sir Clinton inquired about the health of her relation whom she had been nursing.

“Oh, she’s all right again, sir, thank you. I got back yesterday.”

===Chinese===

“注意他的车库是建在房子里头的，探长，”他指出。“要是房子里有一道门直通车库，我看这事儿就有意思了。”

两人走上短短的门径，按了门铃。不一会儿，开门的是马克菲尔德的管家。令她颇感意外的是，克林顿爵士竟问起她此前照护的那位亲戚的身体状况。

“哦，她好利索了，先生，多谢您惦记。我昨天刚回来。”

===Original===

She paused a moment as though in doubt, then added:

“I’m not sure if Dr. Markfield is free this evening, sir. He’s expecting a visitor.”

“We shan’t detain him if his visitor arrives,” Sir Clinton assured her, his manner leaving no doubt in her mind as to the advisability of his own admission.

===Chinese===

她迟疑地顿了顿，似乎拿不定主意，随后又补了一句：

“我说不准马克菲尔德医生今晚方不方便，先生。他在等一位客人。”

“他的客人要是来了，我们也绝不耽搁他，”克林顿爵士让她安心，而他那副做派让她毫不怀疑：放这两位进去才是明智之举。

===Original===

The housekeeper ushered them into Markfield’s sitting-room, where they found him by the fire, deep in a book. At the sound of Sir Clinton’s name he looked up with a glance which betrayed his annoyance at being disturbed.

“I’m rather at a loss to understand this visit,” he said stiffly, as they came into the room.

Sir Clinton refused to notice the obviously grudging tone of his reception.

===Chinese===

管家领他们进了马克菲尔德的起居室，只见他坐在炉火旁埋头读书。一听报出克林顿爵士的名头，他抬起眼来，那一瞥流露出被人打扰的不悦。

“我实在闹不清这一趟来访是什么意思，”他们进屋时，他生硬地说。

克林顿爵士对这种明显勉强的接待口气置若罔闻。

===Original===

“We merely wish to have a few minutes’ talk, Dr. Markfield,” he explained pleasantly. “Some information has come into my hands which needs confirmation, and I think you’ll be able to help us.”

Markfield glanced at the clock.

“I’m in the middle of an experiment,” he said gruffly. “I’ve got to run it through, now that it’s started. If you’re going to be long. I’d better bring the things in here and then I can oversee it while I’m talking to you.”

===Chinese===

“我们只想谈上几分钟，马克菲尔德医生，”他和颜悦色地解释道。“我手里得到一些消息，需要加以证实，我想你能帮上忙。”

马克菲尔德瞥了一眼时钟。

“我正做到实验一半，”他粗声粗气地说。“既然开了头，就得把它做下去。要是你们要待上好一阵子。我最好把东西搬到这儿来，这样一边跟你们说话，一边也好照看着。”

===Original===

Without waiting for permission, he left the room and came back in a couple of minutes with a tray on which stood some apparatus. Flamborough noticed a conical flask containing some limpid liquid, and a stoppered bottle. Markfield clamped a dropping funnel, also containing a clear liquid, so that its spout entered the conical flask; and by turning the tap of the funnel slightly, he allowed a little of the contents to flow down into the flask.

“I hope the smell doesn’t trouble you,” he said, in a tone of sour apology. “It’s the triethylamine I’m mixing with the tetranitromethane in the flask. Rather a fishy stink it has.”

He arranged the apparatus on the table so that he could reach the tap conveniently without rising from his chair; then, after admitting a little more of the liquid from the funnel into the flask, he seated himself once more and gave Sir Clinton his attention.

===Chinese===

他不等许可便出了屋，两分钟后端着一个托盘回来，上面摆着几件仪器。弗拉姆伯勒注意到一只盛着清澄液体的锥形瓶，还有一只带塞的瓶子。马克菲尔德把同样装着透明液体的滴液漏斗用夹子固定住，让漏斗的管嘴伸进锥形瓶里；随后他把漏斗的旋塞稍稍拧开一点，让里面的液体少许流入瓶中。

“但愿这气味没熏着二位，”他说道，语气里带着一股酸溜溜的歉意。“我正往瓶里的四硝基甲烷中兑三乙胺。那股鱼腥臭相当够味儿。”

他把仪器在桌上安顿好，坐在椅子上不必起身就够得着旋塞；随后再往瓶里放进一点漏斗中的液体，便重新落座，把注意力转向克林顿爵士。

===Original===

“What is it you want to know?” he demanded abruptly.

Sir Clinton refused to be hurried. Putting his hand into his breast-pocket, he drew out some sheets of typewriting which he placed on the table before him, as though for future reference. Then he turned to his host.

“Some time ago, a man Peter Whalley came to us and made a statement, Dr. Markfield.”

===Chinese===

“你们到底想打听什么？”他没头没脑地问道。

克林顿爵士偏不受催促。他把手伸进胸前的内袋，掏出几页打字稿，摆在自己面前的桌上，仿佛备日后查考之用。然后他才转向主人。

“前不久，一个名叫彼得·沃利的人来找过我们，作了一份陈述，马克菲尔德医生。”

===Original===

Markfield’s face betrayed some surprise.

“Whalley?” he asked. “Do you mean the man who was murdered on the Lizardbridge Road?”

“He was murdered, certainly,” Sir Clinton confirmed. “But as I said, he made a statement to us. I’m not very clear about some points, and I think you might be able to fill in one or two of the gaps.”

===Chinese===

马克菲尔德的脸上掠过一丝惊讶。

“沃利？”他问。“你说的是在蜥蜴桥路上被害的那个人？”

“他确实是被谋害的，”克林顿爵士予以确认。“不过正如我所说，他给我们作过一份陈述。有几处我还不太了然，我想你或许能把一两处空白补上。”

===Original===

Markfield’s face showed a quick flash of suspicion.

“I’m not very sure what you mean,” he said, doubtfully, “If you’re trying to trap me into saying things that might go against Silverdale, I may as well tell you I’ve no desire to give evidence against him. I’m sure he’s innocent; and I don’t wish to say anything to give you a handle against him. That’s frank enough, isn’t it?”

“If it relieves your mind, I may as well say I agree with you on that point, Dr. Markfield. So there’s no reason why you shouldn’t give us your help.”

===Chinese===

马克菲尔德脸上飞快地闪过一层疑色。

“我不太明白你的意思，”他将信将疑地说，“要是你们想套我的话，好叫这些话对西尔弗代尔不利，那我不妨直说：我没打算出面指证他。我确信他是清白的；我也不愿说出任何话来给你们递他的把柄。这够坦白了吧？”

“要是这能让你安心，我不妨说一句：在这一点上我同意你的看法，马克菲尔德医生。所以你没有理由不帮我们这个忙。”

===Original===

Markfield seemed slightly taken aback by this, but he did his best to hide his feelings.

“Go on, then,” he said. “What is it you want?”

Sir Clinton half-opened the paper on the table, then took away his hand as though he needed no notes at the moment.

===Chinese===

这话似乎让马克菲尔德微微一怔，但他竭力掩饰住自己的情绪。

“那好，说吧，”他说。“你们想要什么？”

克林顿爵士把桌上的纸页摊开一半，随即又把手挪开，仿佛眼下并不需要借助什么记录。

===Original===

“It appears that on the night of the affair at the bungalow, when Mrs. Silverdale met her death, Peter Whalley was walking along the Lizardbridge Road, coming towards town,” Sir Clinton began. “It was a foggy night, you remember. He’d just passed the bungalow gate when he noticed, ahead of him, the headlights of a car standing by the roadside; and he appears to have heard voices.”

The Inspector listened to this with all his ears. Where had Sir Clinton fished up this fresh stock of information, evidently of crucial importance? Then a recollection of the Chief Constable’s warning flashed through his mind and he schooled his features into a mask of impassivity. A glance at Markfield showed that the chemist, though outwardly uninterested, was missing no detail of the story.

“It seems,” Sir Clinton went on, “that the late Mr. Whalley came up to the car and found a man and a girl in the front seat. The girl seemed to be in an abnormal state; and Mr. Whalley, from his limited experience, inferred that she was intoxicated. The man, Whalley thought, had stopped the car to straighten her in the seat and make her look less conspicuous; but as soon as Whalley appeared out of the night, the man started the car again and drove slowly past him towards the bungalow.”

===Chinese===

“看来在平房出事的那天夜里——就是西尔弗代尔太太殒命的那晚——彼得·沃利正沿着蜥蜴桥路朝城里走，”克林顿爵士开了腔。“你总记得，那晚有雾。他刚走过平房的大门，就注意到前头路边停着一辆汽车，车前灯亮着；而且他似乎还听见了人声。”

探长竖起耳朵听着这一切。克林顿爵士这批显然至关重要的新情报，是从哪里钓出来的？随即，郡警察局长那句告诫在他脑中一闪而过，他立刻把脸上的神情绷成一付不动声色的面具。他瞟了一眼马克菲尔德，只见这位化学家虽然表面上漠不关心，故事的每个细节却一个不落。

“情况似乎是，”克林顿爵士继续道，“已故的沃利先生走近那辆车，发现前座上坐着一男一女。那姑娘看上去状态反常；沃利先生凭他那点有限的见识，推断她是喝醉了。沃利心想，那男人把车停了下来，是为了把她在座位上扶正，好让她显得不那么惹眼；可沃利刚从夜色里冒出来，那男人便重新发动汽车，缓缓从他身边驶过，朝平房开去。”

===Original===

Sir Clinton mechanically smoothed out his papers, glanced at them, and then continued:

“The police can’t always choose their instruments, Dr. Markfield. We have to take witnesses where we can get them. Frankly, then, the late Mr. Whalley was not an admirable character—far from it. He’d come upon a man and a girl alone in a car, and the girl was apparently not in a fit state to look after herself. An affair of this sort would bring two ideas into Mr. Whalley’s mind. Clothing them in vulgar language, they’d be: ‘Here’s a bit o’ fun, my word!’ and ‘What is there in it for me?’ He had a foible for trading on the weaknesses of his fellow-creatures, you understand?”

Markfield nodded grimly, but made no audible comment.

===Chinese===

克林顿爵士机械地抹平纸页，扫了一眼，接着往下讲：

“警方挑选工具并不能随心所欲，马克菲尔德医生。证人嘛，能抓到什么样的就算什么样的。那么坦白说吧，已故的沃利先生可不是什么可敬的人物——差得远。他撞见孤男寡女单独待在车里，而那姑娘显然已经无力照管自己。这种事儿落在沃利先生脑子里，只会生出两个念头。用市井粗话给它们披上外衣，那就是：‘好家伙，这可有热闹瞧了！’和‘这里头有什么油水？’他有个癖好，专爱拿同胞的弱点做买卖，你明白吗？”

马克菲尔德冷冷地点了点头，没有出声评论。

===Original===

“The late Mr. Whalley, then, stared after the car; and, to his joy, no doubt, he saw it turn in at the gate of the bungalow. He guessed the place was empty, since there hadn’t been a light showing in it when he passed it a minute or two before. Not much need to analyse Mr. Whalley’s ideas in detail, is there? He made up his mind that a situation of this sort promised him some fun after his own heart, quite apart from any little financial pickings he might make out of it later on, if he were lucky. So he made his best pace after the car.”

Sir Clinton turned over a page of the notes before him and, glancing at the document, knitted his brows slightly.

“The late Mr. Whalley wasn’t a perfect witness of course, and I’m inclined to think that at this point I can supply a missing detail in the story. A second car came on the scene round about this period—a car driving in towards town—and it must have met the car with the man and the girl in it just about this time. But that’s not in Mr. Whalley’s statement. It’s only a surmise of my own, and not really essential.”

===Chinese===

“于是，已故的沃利先生目送着那辆车；而且，无疑令他心花怒放的是，他看见车拐进了平房的大门。他猜那房子是空的，因为一两分钟前他路过时，里面没有透出灯光。沃利先生的心思，不必细加剖析了吧？他打定了主意：这般局面，保管有正对他胃口的热闹可看，更不用说运气好的话，日后还能从中捞上一票小小的外快。于是他撒开腿，尽力朝那辆车追去。”

克林顿爵士翻过面前记录的一页，目光掠过那份文件，眉头微微一皱。

“已故的沃利先生当然算不得十全十美的证人，而我倾向于认为，讲到这一处，我可以给故事补上一个缺失的细节。大约就在这个当口，现场出现了第二辆车——一辆正朝城里开来的车——它必定恰在此时与那辆载着男人和姑娘的车交会而过。不过这可不在沃利先生的陈述里。这只是我个人的臆测，并非真的紧要。”

===Original===

Inspector Flamborough had been growing more and more puzzled as this narrative unfolded. He could not imagine how the Chief Constable had accumulated all this information. Suddenly the explanation crossed his mind.

“Lord! He’s bluffing! He’s trying to persuade Markfield that we know all about it already. These are just inferences of his; and he’s put the double bluff on Markfield by pretending that Whalley’s statement wasn’t quite full and that he’s filling the gap with a guess of his own. What a nerve!” he commented to himself.

===Chinese===

这段叙述步步展开，弗拉姆伯勒探长越听越纳闷。他想象不出郡警察局长是从哪里积攒下这一切情报的。忽然，一个解释掠过他的脑海。

“天哪！他在唬人！他想叫马克菲尔德相信，我们已经全盘尽知了。这些不过是他自己的推断；他还对马克菲尔德来了个连环空城计，假装沃利的陈述不甚完整，而那处空白正由他自己的猜测来填补。好大的胆子！”他心里暗自评点。

===Original===

“By the time the late Mr. Whalley reached the bungalow gate,” Sir Clinton pursued, “the man had got the girl out of the car and both of them had gone into the house. Mr. Whalley, it seems, went gingerly up the approach, and, as he did so, a light went on in one of the front rooms of the bungalow. The curtains were drawn. The late Mr. Whalley, with an eye to future profit, took the precaution of noting the number of the motor, which was standing at the front door.”

Flamborough glanced at Markfield to see what effect Sir Clinton was producing. To his surprise, the chemist seemed in no way perturbed. With a gesture as though asking permission, he leaned over and ran a little of the liquid from the funnel into the flask, shook the mixture gently for a moment or two, and then turned back to Sir Clinton. The Inspector, watching keenly, could see no tremor in his hand as he carried out the operations.

“The late Mr. Whalley,” Sir Clinton continued, when Markfield had finished his work. “The late Mr. Whalley did not care about hanging round the front of the bungalow. If he stood in front of the lighted window, anyone passing on the road would be able to see him outlined against the glare; and that might have led to difficulties. So he passed round to the second window of the same room, which looked out on the side of the bungalow and was therefore not so conspicuous from the road. Just as he turned the corner of the building, he heard a second car stop at the gate.”

===Chinese===

“等已故的沃利先生赶到平房门口时，”克林顿爵士接着说，“那男人已经把姑娘弄下了车，两人一同进了屋。看来沃利先生蹑手蹑脚地摸上门径，正走着，平房一间前屋的灯亮了。窗帘拉上了。已故的沃利先生着眼于日后的进项，未雨绸缪，把停在正门前那辆汽车的牌号记了下来。”

弗拉姆伯勒瞟向马克菲尔德，想看看克林顿爵士这番话起了什么效果。出乎他意料，这位化学家似乎毫无悸动。他做了个像是请示的手势，俯身把漏斗里的液体放进锥形瓶少许，将混合液轻轻晃了一两下，然后转回来对着克林顿爵士。探长凝神细看，那双手在做这一连串动作时竟不见一丝颤动。

“已故的沃利先生，”马克菲尔德做完手头的活计后，克林顿爵士继续道。“已故的沃利先生可不愿在平房前头打转。要是他站在亮着灯的窗前，路上过往的任何人都能看见他映在灯光里的身影；那可就要惹出麻烦了。于是他绕到同一房间的第二扇窗前——那扇窗朝着平房的侧面，从路上看不那么显眼。就在他拐过屋角的时候，他听见第二辆车在大门口停了下来。”

===Original===

Sir Clinton paused here, as though undecided about the next part of his narrative. He glanced at Markfield, apparently to see whether he was paying attention; then he proceeded.

“The late Mr. Whalley tiptoed along to this side-window of the lighted room, and, much to his delight, I’ve no doubt, he found that the curtains had been carelessly drawn, so that a chink was left between them through which he could peep into the room. He stepped on to the flowerbed, bent down, and peered through the aperture. I hope I make myself clear, Dr. Markfield?”

“Quite,” said Markfield curtly.

===Chinese===

克林顿爵士在这里停了停，仿佛对叙述的下一段还拿不定主意。他看了马克菲尔德一眼，显然是想看看对方是否在听；然后才继续。

“已故的沃利先生踮着脚尖蹭到这间亮灯屋子的侧窗跟前，而且——我敢说准叫他喜出望外——他发现窗帘拉得马虎，中间留了一道缝隙，正好可以窥探屋内。他踏上花坛，弯下腰，凑着那道缝隙往里瞧。我希望我把话说得够明白了，马克菲尔德医生？”

“够明白了，”马克菲尔德简短地答道。

===Original===

Sir Clinton nodded in acknowledgment, glanced once more at his papers as though to refresh his memory, and continued:

“What he saw was this. The girl was lying in an armchair near the fireplace. The late Mr. Whalley, again misled by his limited experience, thought she’d fallen asleep—the effects of alcohol, he supposed, I believe. The young man who was with her—we may save the trouble by calling him Hassendean, I think—seemed rather agitated, but not quite in the way that the late Mr. Whalley had anticipated. Hassendean spoke to the girl and got no reply, evidently. He shook her gently, and so on; but he got no response. I think we may cut out the details. The net result was that to Mr. Whalley’s inexperienced eye, the girl looked very far gone. Hassendean seemed to be thunderstruck by the situation, which puzzled the late Mr. Whalley considerably at the time.”

Markfield, apparently unimpressed, leaned across and ran some more of the liquid out of his funnel. Flamborough guessed the movement might be intended to conceal his features from easy observation.

===Chinese===

克林顿爵士点头致意，又看了一眼纸页，仿佛在唤起记忆，随即继续：

“他看到的是这么一幅光景。那姑娘躺在壁炉边的一张扶手椅里。已故的沃利先生又一次被他那点有限的见识误导了，只当她睡着了——我相信，他当时八成以为是酒精的作用。陪在她身边的那个年轻人——我看咱们不妨省点事，就管他叫哈森丁吧——显得相当慌乱，但又不完全像已故的沃利先生预料的那副样子。哈森丁跟姑娘说话，显然没有得到回答。他轻轻摇她，如此等等；可她毫无反应。细节咱们可以略去不谈。最终的结果是：在沃利先生那双缺乏经验的眼里，姑娘已是一副沉疴难醒的模样。哈森丁似乎被眼前的局面惊得目瞪口呆，这在当时让已故的沃利先生颇为纳闷。”

马克菲尔德似乎不为所动，俯身又从漏斗里放出去一些液体。弗拉姆伯勒猜想，这个动作也许是为了不让旁人轻易看清他的脸色。

===Original===

“The next stage in the proceedings took the late Mr. Whalley by surprise, it seems,” Sir Clinton went on. “Leaving the girl where she was, young Hassendean left the room for a minute or two. When he came back, he had a pistol in his hand. This was not at all what the late Mr. Whalley had been expecting. Least of all did he expect to see young Hassendean go up to the girl, and shoot her in the head at close quarters. I’m sure you’ll appreciate the feelings of the late Mr. Whalley at this stage, Dr. Markfield.”

“Surprising,” Markfield commented abruptly.

Sir Clinton nodded in agreement.

===Chinese===

“接下来的进展，看来让已故的沃利先生大吃一惊，”克林顿爵士继续道。“小哈森丁把姑娘撂在原处，离开房间一两分钟。等他回来时，手里多了一支手枪。这完全不是已故的沃利先生料想得到的。而最最料不到的，是他亲眼看见小哈森丁走到姑娘跟前，抵近她的头部开了一枪。我相信你定能体会已故的沃利先生此时此刻的心情，马克菲尔德医生。”

“是够惊人的，”马克菲尔德唐突地评了一句。

克林顿爵士点头表示同意。

===Original===

“What must have been even more surprising was the sequel. The glass of the front window broke with a blow, and from behind the curtains a man appeared, who fell upon Hassendean. There was a struggle, a couple of shots from Hassendean’s pistol, and then Hassendean fell on the ground—dead, as Whalley supposed at the time.”

Flamborough stared hard at Markfield, but at that moment the chemist again turned in his chair, ran the remainder of the liquid from the funnel into his flask, and then refilled the funnel from the bottle on the tray. This done, he turned once more with an impassive face to Sir Clinton.

“By this time, the late Mr. Whalley seems to have seen all that he wanted. Just as he was turning away from the window, he noticed the newcomer take some small object from his waistcoat pocket and drop it on the floor. Then Mr. Whalley felt it was time to make himself scarce. He stepped back on to the path, made his way round the bungalow, hurried down the approach to the gate. There he came across a car—evidently the one in which the assailant had arrived. The late Mr. Whalley, even at this stage, was not quite free from his second idea: ‘What is there in it for me?’ He took the number of the car, and then he made himself scarce.”

===Chinese===

“而更加匪夷所思的，恐怕还是后文。前窗的玻璃被一击打碎，窗帘后面闯出一条汉子，朝哈森丁扑了过去。一场扭打，哈森丁的手枪响了两声，接着哈森丁便栽倒在地——在当时沃利看来，人是死了。”

弗拉姆伯勒死死盯着马克菲尔德，可就在这时，化学家又从椅子上侧过身去，把漏斗里剩下的液体尽数放进锥形瓶，再从托盘上的瓶子里把漏斗重新灌满。做完这些，他又转回来，面无表情地对着克林顿爵士。

“到这时，已故的沃利先生想看的想必都看全了。就在他转身离开窗口的当口，他注意到那条新来的汉子从马甲口袋里摸出一件小物件，丢在了地板上。沃利先生觉得，此时不走更待何时。他退回小径，绕过平房，急匆匆顺着门径溜到大门口。他在那儿撞见一辆车——显然是袭击者乘来的那辆。已故的沃利先生即使到了这步田地，也没能全然丢开他的第二个念头：‘这里头有什么油水？’他记下了那辆车的号码，然后便溜之大吉。”

===Original===

Sir Clinton stopped for a moment or two and gazed across at Markfield with an inscrutable face.

“By the way, Dr. Markfield,” he added in a casual tone, “what was the pet name that Mrs. Silverdale used to call you when you were alone together—the one beginning with *B*?”

This time, it was evident to the Inspector, Sir Clinton had got home under Markfield’s guard. The chemist glanced up with more than a shade of apprehension on his face. He seemed to be making a mental estimate of the situation before he replied.

===Chinese===

克林顿爵士停顿了片刻，用一张高深莫测的脸凝视着马克菲尔德。

“对了，马克菲尔德医生，”他用闲谈般的口气补了一句，“西尔弗代尔太太跟你单独相处的时候，惯常唤你的那声爱称——以 *B* 开头的那声——是什么？”

探长看得分明：这一回，克林顿爵士这一剑终于刺破了马克菲尔德的守势。化学家抬起眼来，脸上挂着不止一丝的惶惑。他似乎先在心里掂量了一番局势，才开口作答。

===Original===

“H’m! You know that, do you?” he said finally. “Then there’s no use denying it, I suppose. She used to call me ‘Bear’ usually. She said I had the manners of one, at times; and perhaps there was something in that.”

Sir Clinton showed no sign that he attached much importance to Markfield’s explanation.

“You became intimate with her some time in 1925, I think, just after the Silverdales came here?”

===Chinese===

“哼！这个你们也知道，是么？”他终于开了口。“那么再抵赖也没用了，我想。她从前通常唤我‘熊’。她说我有时候的做派跟熊一个样；这话嘛，或许也不算冤枉。”

克林顿爵士毫无表示，看不出他把马克菲尔德这番解释当作什么要紧事。

“我想，你和她厮熟起来是在 1925 年间的某个时候，就在西尔弗代尔夫妇搬来本地之后不久吧？”

===Original===

Markfield nodded his assent.

“And very shortly after that, you and she thought it best to conceal your liaison by seeing as little of each other as possible in public, so as not to draw attention to your relations?”

“That’s true.”

===Chinese===

马克菲尔德点头认了。

“而此后不久，你们俩便觉得最好把这层私情遮掩起来，在公开场合尽量少碰面，免得引人留意你们的关系？”

“是这么回事。”

===Original===

“And finally she got hold of young Hassendean to serve as a blind? Advertised herself with him openly, whilst you stayed in the background?”

“You seem to know a good deal about it,” Markfield admitted coldly.

“I think I know all that matters,” the Chief Constable commented. “You’ve lost the game, Dr. Markfield.”

===Chinese===

“最后，她抓来小哈森丁当幌子？公然同他出双入对、招摇过市，而你则退居幕后？”

“这些事你倒知道得不少，”马克菲尔德冷冷地承认。

“要紧的事，我想我全知道了，”郡警察局长评了一句。“这局你输了，马克菲尔德医生。”

===Original===

Markfield seemed to consider the situation rapidly before he spoke again.

“You can’t make it worse than manslaughter,” he said at last. “It’s no more than that, on the evidence you’ve given me just now. I saw him shoot Yvonne, and then, in the struggle afterwards, his pistol went off twice by accident and hit him. You couldn’t call that a case of murder. I shall plead that it was done in self-defence; and you haven’t Whalley to put into the box against me.”

Sir Clinton took no pains to conceal a sardonic smile.

===Chinese===

再度开口之前，马克菲尔德似乎把局面飞快地盘算了一遍。

“你们没法把罪名定得比非预谋杀人更重，”他终于说。“就凭你们刚才摆给我的这些证据，至多也就是这么个性质。我亲眼看见他朝伊冯娜开枪，随后在扭打之中，他的手枪走火两响，打中了他本人。这怎么也算不上谋杀。我会以正当防卫作辩；再说，你们也没有沃利可以提上证人席来对质了。”

克林顿爵士索性毫不掩饰地露出一丝讥诮的笑。

===Original===

“It won’t do, Dr. Markfield,” he pointed out. “You might get off on that plea if it were only the bungalow business that you were charged with. But there’s the murder of the maid at Heatherfield as well. You can’t twist that into a self-defence affair. No jury would look at it for a moment.”

“You seem to know a good deal about it,” Markfield repeated thoughtfully.

“I suppose what you really wanted at Heatherfield was a packet of your love-letters to Mrs. Silverdale?” Sir Clinton asked.

===Chinese===

“这说不过去，马克菲尔德医生，”他指出。“要是指控你的只有平房那一桩，凭这套说辞你或许还能脱身。可还有希瑟菲尔德女仆被杀那桩呢。那个你没法拐成什么正当防卫。陪审团连看都不会多看一眼。”

“这些事你倒知道得不少，”马克菲尔德若有所思地又说了一遍。

“我想，你在希瑟菲尔德真正想要的，是那一包你写给西尔弗代尔太太的情书吧？”克林顿爵士问。

===Original===

Markfield confirmed this with a nod.

“That’s all you have against me, I suppose?” he demanded after a pause.

Sir Clinton shook his head.

===Chinese===

马克菲尔德点头证实了这一点。

“你们手上对付我的，就这些了吧？”他停了片刻问道。

克林顿爵士摇了摇头。

===Original===

“No,” he said, “there’s the affair of the late Mr. Whalley as well.”

Markfield’s face betrayed neither surprise nor chagrin at this fresh charge.

“That’s all, then?” he questioned again, with apparent unconcern.

===Chinese===

“不，”他说，“还有已故的沃利先生那桩事呢。”

听到这新添的罪状，马克菲尔德脸上既不见惊讶，也不见懊恼。

“那么，就这些了？”他又问了一遍，一副满不在乎的样子。

===Original===

“All that’s of importance,” Sir Clinton admitted. “Of course, in the guise of our friend Mr. Justice, you did your best to throw suspicion on Silverdale. That’s a minor point, so far as you’re concerned now. It’s curious how you murderers can’t leave well alone. If you hadn’t played the fool there, you’d have given us ever so much more trouble.”

Markfield made no answer at the moment. He seemed to be reviewing the whole situation in his mind, thinking hard before he broke the silence.

“Good thing, a scientific training,” he said at length, rather unexpectedly. “It teaches one to realise the bearing of plain facts. My game seems to be up. You’ve been too smart for me.”

===Chinese===

“要紧的就这些了，”克林顿爵士承认道。“当然，你还曾化名我们那位朋友‘正义’，千方百计想把嫌疑往西尔弗代尔身上引。就你眼下的处境而言，那只是小事一桩。真奇怪，你们这班杀人犯偏不肯就此罢手。你要不是在那上头逞能胡闹，本可以给我们添多得多的麻烦。”

马克菲尔德一时没有作声。他似乎在心里把整个局面重新过了一遍，苦苦思量之后才打破沉默。

“科学训练真是件好东西，”他终于开了口，语气颇为出人意料。“它教会人认清干巴巴的事实意味着什么。看来我这局是输定了。你们比我高明。”

===Original===

He paused, and a grim smile crossed his face, as though he found something humorous in the situation.

“You seem to have enough stuff there to pitch a tale to a jury,” he continued, “and I daresay you’ve more in reserve. I’m not inclined to be dragged squalling to the gallows—too undignified for my taste. I’ll tell you the facts.”

Flamborough, eager that things should be done in proper form, interposed the usual official cautionary statement.

===Chinese===

他顿了顿，一丝冷峻的笑意掠过脸庞，仿佛从眼下的处境里咂摸出几分滑稽来。

“你手头那堆材料，看来足够给陪审团编套说辞了，”他接着说，“而且我敢说你还留着后手。我可不想又哭又闹地被拖上绞架——按我的口味，那太不成体统。我把事实告诉你们。”

弗拉姆伯勒讲究凡事须按规矩来，照例插入了官方的那句警告声明。

===Original===

“That’s all right,” Markfield answered carelessly. “You’ll find paper over yonder on my desk, beside the typewriter. You can take down what I say, and I’ll sign it afterwards if you think that necessary when I’ve finished.”

The Inspector crossed the room, picked up a number of sheets of typewriting paper, and returned to the table. He pulled out his fountain-pen and prepared to take notes.

“Mind if I light my pipe?” Markfield inquired.

===Chinese===

“无所谓，”马克菲尔德漫不经心地答道。“那边我桌上就有纸，打字机旁边。你们可以把我说的话记下来，等我说完了，你们觉得有必要的话，我再签字。”

探长穿过房间，取来几页打字纸，回到桌旁。他抽出自来水笔，准备记录。

“介意我点个烟斗吗？”马克菲尔德问道。

===Original===

As the chemist put his hand to his pocket, Flamborough half-rose from his seat; but he sank back again into his chair when a tobacco-pouch appeared instead of the pistol which he had been afraid might be produced. Markfield threw him a glance which showed he had fathomed the meaning of the Inspector’s start.

“Don’t get nervous,” he said contemptuously. “There’ll be no shooting. This isn’t a film, you know.”

He reached up to the mantelpiece for his pipe, charged it deliberately, lighted it, and then turned to Sir Clinton.

===Chinese===

化学家刚把手伸向口袋，弗拉姆伯勒便从座位上半直起身来；可等掏出来的是一只烟丝袋，而不是他方才暗怕会出现的那支手枪，他又坐了回去。马克菲尔德朝他瞥了一眼，那一眼表明，他已看穿了探长方才一惊的缘由。

“别紧张，”他不屑地说。“不会开枪的。这可不是拍电影，知道吧。”

他伸手从壁炉架上取下烟斗，不紧不慢地装上烟丝，点着，然后转向克林顿爵士。

===Original===

“You’ve got a warrant for my arrest, I suppose?” he asked in a tone which sounded almost indifferent.

Sir Clinton’s affirmative reply did not seem to disturb him. He settled himself comfortably in his chair and appeared interested chiefly in getting his pipe to burn well.

“I’ll speak slowly,” he said at last, turning to the Inspector. “If I go too fast, just let me know.”

Flamborough nodded and sat, pen in hand, waiting for the opening of the narrative.

===Chinese===

“你们带了逮捕证了吧，我猜？”他问道，口气近乎无动于衷。

克林顿爵士的肯定回答似乎并未惊动他。他在椅子里舒舒服服坐定，看起来主要操心的，倒是怎样让烟斗烧得通透。

“我会说慢些，”他终于开口，转向探长。“要是我讲得太快，尽管提醒我。”

弗拉姆伯勒点点头，手握钢笔坐定，等着这段供词开场。

## XVIII The Connecting Thread / 十八 联系的线索

===Original===

“I don’t see how you did it,” Markfield began, “but you got to the root of things when you traced a connection between me and Yvonne Silverdale. I’d never expected that. And considering how we’d kept our affairs quiet for years, I thought I’d be safe at the end of it all.

===Chinese===

“我不明白你们是怎么查出来的，”马克菲尔德开口道，“不过当你追出我和伊冯娜·西尔弗代尔之间的关联时，你确实挖到了事情的根子上。这一点我万万没有料到。想想我们把私情瞒得严严实实这么多年，我原以为到头来总能安然无恙。

===Original===

“It was in 1925, as you said, that the thing began—just after Silverdale came to the Croft-Thornton. There was a sort of amateur dramatic show afoot then, and both Yvonne and I joined it. That brought us together first. The rest didn’t take long. I suppose it was a case of the attraction of opposites. One can’t explain that sort of thing on any rational basis. It just happened.”

He hesitated for a moment, as though casting his mind back to these earlier times; then he continued:

===Chinese===

“正如你所说，事情是在1925年开的头——就在西尔弗代尔进克罗夫特-索顿研究所之后不久。当时正筹演一出业余话剧，我和伊冯娜都参加了。我们俩就是这么先搭上的。往下的事没费什么工夫。我想这大概就是相反相吸的道理。这种事没法用任何理性讲清楚。它就这么发生了。”

他迟疑了片刻，仿佛在心里回溯那段早年的时光；随后接着说道：

===Original===

“Once it *had* happened, I did the thinking for the pair of us. Clearly enough, the thing was to avoid suspicion. That meant that people mustn’t couple our names even casually. And the way to prevent that was to see as little of each other as possible in public. I dropped out of things, cut dances, left the theatrical affair, and posed as being engrossed in work. She advertised herself as dance-mad. It suited her well enough. Result: we hardly ever were seen in the same room. No one thought of linking our names in the remotest way. I gave her no presents. …”

===Chinese===

“事情既然*已经*发生，我们两个人的盘算就都由我来打。显而易见，要紧的是别招人疑心。这就是说，人们哪怕随口也不能把我们的名字扯到一块儿。要做到这一点，办法就是尽量少在公开场合照面。我退出了各种活动，舞会不去了，话剧也辞了，摆出一副埋头工作的姿态。她则把自己张扬成个舞迷。这也正合她的心意。结果是：我们几乎从不在同一个房间里一同露面。谁做梦也不会把我们的名字联想到一起。我没送过她什么礼物。……”

===Original===

“Think again,” Sir Clinton interrupted. “You gave her at least one present.”

Markfield reflected for some moments; then his face showed more than a trace of discomfiture.

“You mean a signet-ring? Good Lord! I forgot all about it, that night at the bungalow! So that’s where you got your story about the initial *B* from? I never thought of that.”

===Chinese===

“再好好想想，”克林顿爵士打断他。“你至少送过她一件礼物。”

马克菲尔德寻思了片刻；随即他脸上露出的可就不止一丝窘态了。

“你是说那枚图章戒指？天哪！平房那晚的事，我早把它忘得一干二净了！你们那套字母*B*的说辞就是打那儿来的？我压根儿没想到这一层。”

===Original===

Sir Clinton made no comment, and after a few seconds Markfield continued.

“In the early days, we wrote letters to each other—just a few. Later on, I urged her to burn them, for safety’s sake. But she treasured them, apparently; and she wouldn’t do it. She said they were quite safe in a locked drawer in her bedroom. Silverdale never entered her room, you know. It seemed safe enough. It was these damned letters that landed me in the end.

===Chinese===

克林顿爵士不置一词，过了几秒钟，马克菲尔德继续说了下去。

“早先，我们互相通过几封信——就那么几封。后来我一再劝她，为安全起见把信烧掉。可她显然把那些信当宝贝，说什么也不肯。她说信锁在她卧室带锁的抽屉里，万无一失。你是知道的，西尔弗代尔从不进她的房间。瞧上去够稳妥了。到头来把我坑了的，正是这批该死的信。

===Original===

“Yvonne and I hadn’t any reason to worry about Silverdale. He’d lost all interest in her and gone off after Avice Deepcar. Oh, that was all quite respectable and aboveboard. She’s a decent girl—nothing against her. We’d have been quite glad to see him marry her, except that it wouldn’t have suited our book. My screw was good enough for a single man. It wouldn’t have kept two of us—not on the basis we needed, anyhow. And a divorce case might have got me chucked out of the Croft-Thornton. Where would we have been then? So you see that alley was barred.

===Chinese===

“伊冯娜和我犯不着为西尔弗代尔操心。他对她早已兴味索然，转而追艾维斯·迪普卡去了。噢，那档子事完全规规矩矩、光明正大。她是个正派姑娘——挑不出一点毛病。我们本来乐见他娶她，只可惜不合我们的算盘。我的薪水养活一个单身汉尽够了，却养不起我们两个——反正撑不起我们需要的那个局面。再说，一桩离婚官司很可能把我从克罗夫特-索顿研究所扫地出门。到那时我们还有什么出路？所以你瞧，这条路也堵死了。

===Original===

“By and by, young Hassendean turned up. When I found he was getting keen on Yvonne, I encouraged her to keep him on her string. She had no use for the boy except as a dancing-partner; but we used him as a blind to cover the real state of affairs. So long as people could talk about him and her, they weren’t likely to think of her and me. So she led him on until the brat thought he was indispensable. I suppose he fell in love with her, in a way. We never imagined he might be dangerous.

“That was the state of things up to ten days before the affair at the bungalow. There seemed to be no reason why it shouldn’t have lasted for years. But just then Yvonne got news of this money that had been left her—about £12,000. That put a new light on the affair. It gave her an income of her own. We could afford to let Silverdale divorce her; then I could have chucked the Croft-Thornton, married her, and set up in private practice somewhere. Her money would have kept us going until I had scraped a business together; and no one cares a damn about the matrimonial affairs of a chemical expert in private practice.

===Chinese===

“后来，小哈森丁出现了。我发现他对伊冯娜渐生痴念，便怂恿她把他吊在自己线上。这小子于她无非是个舞伴；可我们拿他当幌子，遮掩事情的真局面。只要人们有他和她的闲话可嚼，就不大会想到她跟我。于是她一路吊着他，吊得这小崽子自以为缺他不可。我看他也算真的爱上她了，多少算是吧。我们万万没想到他会成祸根。

“局面就这样一直维持到平房案发前十天。本来照这么过下去，再撑个几年也未见得不行。可偏偏就在那时，伊冯娜得到消息，说有人给她留了一笔钱——大约£12,000。这一下整件事另当别论了。她有了属于自己的进项。我们尽可以让西尔弗代尔跟她离婚；然后我可以甩了克罗夫特-索顿研究所的差事，娶她，到别处挂牌开业。她的钱够我们撑到我一步步张罗起自己的营生；再说，一个私人开业的化学专家，婚娶上的事没谁会放在心上。

===Original===

“We talked it over, and we practically made up our minds to take that course. It seemed a bit too good to be true. Anyhow it would have got us out of all the hole-and-corner business. After three years of that, we were getting a bit sick of it. Another week or two, and Westerhaven would have had all the scandal it needed, if it was inclined that way. We’d have got each other. And Silverdale could have married his girl with all the sympathy of the town. Ideal, eh?”

He puffed savagely at his pipe for a moment or two before speaking again.

===Chinese===

“我们商量过之后，实际上已经拿定主意走这条路了。这好得有点让人不敢相信是真的。不管怎么说，这一来我们就彻底摆脱了这种鬼鬼祟祟的勾当。这种日子过了三年，我们都有点腻了。再过一两个星期，韦斯特黑文想要丑闻的话也就尽够它受用了——倘若它好这一口的话。我们得到了彼此。西尔弗代尔也能带着全镇的同情娶他的姑娘。十全十美，是不是？”

他恶狠狠地抽了两口烟斗，才重新开口。

===Original===

“Then that young skunk Hassendean. … He must needs get above himself and ruin the whole scheme, damn him! I can only guess what happened. He got to know about the properties of hyoscine. There was plenty of it at the Croft-Thornton. He must have stolen some of it and used it to drug Yvonne that night. However, that’s going a bit fast. I’ll tell you what happened, as it seemed to me.”

Markfield paused and glanced inquiringly at the Inspector.

“It’s all right,” Flamborough reassured him. “If you don’t speak quicker than that, I can take it down easily.”

Markfield leaned over and gave the contents of his flask a gentle shake before continuing his narrative.

===Chinese===

“接着就是小哈森丁那条臭鱼。……他偏要自不量力，把整个计划搅了个稀烂，这该死的！出了什么事，我只能靠猜。他打听到了东莨菪碱的药性。克罗夫特-索顿研究所里有的是这东西。他准是偷了一些，那天夜里用来麻翻伊冯娜。不过，这么说未免扯得太快。我还是照我看来的情形，把发生的事讲给你们听。”

马克菲尔德顿了顿，探询地望了探长一眼。

“没关系，”弗拉姆伯勒宽慰他。“只要你说得不比这更快，我轻轻松松就能记下来。”

马克菲尔德俯过身，轻轻晃了晃烧瓶里的东西，继续陈述。

===Original===

“That night, I’d been out late at the Research Station on a piece of work. I mean I’d gone there after dinner for a few minutes. When I finished, I came in by the Lizardbridge Road in my car. It was a bit foggy, and I was driving slowly. Just after I’d passed the bungalow, I met an open car. We were both crawling, owing to the fog; and I had a good look at the people in the other car. One was young Hassendean. The other was Yvonne; and even as I passed them, I could see there was something queer about the business. Besides, what would she be doing with that young whelp away out of town? I knew her far too well to think she was up to any hanky-panky with him.

===Chinese===

“那天夜里，我为件活计在农业研究站待到很晚。我是说，晚饭后我到那儿去了几分钟。干完活，我开车取道蜥蜴桥路回城。雾不小，我开得很慢。刚驶过平房不远，我迎面遇上了一辆敞篷车。两边都因为雾爬得跟蜗牛似的，我把对面车里的人瞧了个真切。一个是小哈森丁，另一个是伊冯娜；两车交会的一瞬，我就看出这事透着蹊跷。再说，她深更半夜跟那条小狗崽子跑到城外老远的地方做什么？我对她知之太深，绝不信她会跟他闹什么鬼名堂。

===Original===

“It looked queer. So as soon as I was past them, I turned my car, meaning to follow them and stand by. Unfortunately in the fog, I almost ditched my car in turning; and it gave me some trouble to swing round—one wheel got into the trench at the edge of the road. It was a minute or so before I got clear again. Then I went off after them.

“I saw the car at the door of the bungalow, and some lights on in the place which hadn’t been there when I’d passed it on my way down. So I stopped my car at the gate and walked up to the bungalow door. It was locked.

===Chinese===

“事情透着古怪。所以一错开车，我就掉了头，打算跟上去看个究竟。倒霉的是，雾里掉头，我差点把车栽进沟里；费了好一番手脚才把车身正过来——一只轮子陷进了路边的排水沟。折腾了一分来钟才重新脱身。随后我便追了上去。

“我看见那辆车停在平房门口，屋里亮着几处灯——我先前经过时还没有。于是我把车停在院门口，走到平房门前。门锁着。

===Original===

“I didn’t care about hammering on the door. That would only have put Hassendean on the alert and left me still on the wrong side of the door. So I walked round to the lighted window and managed to get a glimpse of the room through the curtains. Yvonne was lying back in an armchair, facing me. I thought she’d fainted or something like that. The whole affair puzzled me a bit, you see. That young skunk Hassendean was wandering about the room, evidently in a devil of a state of nerves about something or other.

“Just as I was making up my mind to break the window, he bolted out of the room; and I thought he meant to clear off from the house, leaving Yvonne there—ill, perhaps. That made me pretty mad; and I kept my eye on the front door to see that he didn’t get away without my catching him. That prevented me from breaking the window and climbing into the room.

===Chinese===

“我不想抡拳头砸门。那只会叫哈森丁警觉起来，而我还照样被关在门外。于是我绕到亮灯的那扇窗前，隔着帘子把屋里的情形窥了个大概。伊冯娜仰躺在扶手椅里，正对着我。我以为她是晕了过去还是怎么的。你也明白，整个事情让我有点摸不着头脑。小哈森丁那条臭鱼在屋里转来转去，显然为什么事紧张得要死。

“我正打定主意砸窗，他却突然冲出房间；我以为他打算撇下伊冯娜——说不定还病着——自顾逃离这房子。这可把我气坏了；我死死盯住前门，务求他逃不出我的手心。也正因这样，我顾不上砸窗爬进屋去。

===Original===

“Then, a bit to my surprise, the young swine came back again with something in his hand—I couldn’t see what it was then. He walked over to where Yvonne was, in the chair, lifted his arm, and shot her in the head. Deliberately. Nothing like an accident, remember. And there, before my eyes, I saw the whole of our dreams collapsing, just when we thought they were going to come true. Pretty stiff, wasn’t it?”

He bent forward and made a pretence of knocking the ashes from his pipe. When he looked up again, his face was set once more.

===Chinese===

“随后，颇叫我意外，那小畜生又回来了，手里攥着个什么东西——当时看不清是什么。他走到伊冯娜坐的椅子跟前，抬起手臂，朝她的头开了一枪。蓄意的。记住，绝没有半点走火的样子。就在我眼前，我们全部的美梦轰然坍塌——偏偏赶在我们以为它就要成真的当口。够狠的吧？”

他俯下身，装模作样地磕烟斗里的灰。再抬起头来时，他的脸色又绷紧了。

===Original===

“I’m no psychologist to spin you a yarn about how I felt just then,” he continued. “In fact, I doubt if I felt anything except that I wanted to down that young hound. Anyhow, I broke the glass, got my hand inside, undid the catch, and was through the curtain before he knew what was happening. I don’t know what he thought when he saw me. His face was almost worth it—sheer amazement and terror. He was just bringing up his pistol when I dropped on him and got his wrist. Then there was a bit of a struggle; but he hadn’t a chance against me. I shot him twice in the body and when he dropped, with blood coming from his mouth, I knew I’d got him in the lung, and I didn’t bother further about him. He seemed done for. I hoped he was.”

Markfield’s voice in the last few sentences had expressed the bitterness of his emotions; but when he continued, he made a successful effort to keep his tone level.

===Chinese===

“我不是心理学家，没法给你们编一套当时心情如何的故事，”他接着说。“说实话，除了想放倒那条年轻的猎狗，我怀疑自己是否还有别的感觉。总之，我砸碎玻璃，把手探进去，拨开门闩，趁他还没弄清怎么回事就掀帘子冲了进去。他见到我时心里想什么，我不知道。他那张脸差点让我觉得值了——纯粹是错愕加惊恐。他刚要举枪，我已扑上去攥住他的手腕。接着扭打了一阵；可他哪里是我的对手。我朝他身上开了两枪，他倒下去时嘴里往外冒血，我知道打中了肺，也就没再费心理会他。看样子他是完了。我还巴不得他完了呢。”

最后这几句里，马克菲尔德的声音道出了他心头的怨毒；但他往下说时，总算把语调稳住了。

===Original===

“One thinks quick enough in a tight corner. First thing I did was to look at Yvonne.”

He shrugged his shoulders to express what he seemed unable to put into words.

“That dream was done for. The only thing to do was to clear myself. I had another look at Hassendean. He seemed to have had his gruel. I’d a notion of shooting him again, just to make sure, but it didn’t seem worth while. Besides, there had been row enough already. A fourth shot might draw some passerby. So I left him. I picked up the pistol and cleaned my fingermarks off it before putting it on the floor again. Then I did the same for the window-hasp. These were the only two things I’d touched, so I wasn’t leaving traces.

===Chinese===

“人到绝境，脑子转得飞快。我做的头一件事是查看伊冯娜。”

他耸了耸肩，来表达他似乎说不出口的东西。

“那个梦算是彻底完了。当务之急是给自己脱身。我又看了看哈森丁。他像是已经遭了报应。我本想再补他一枪，图个保险，但想想不值得。再说，动静已经闹得够大了。第四声枪响说不定会招来过路人。于是我撇下了他。我拾起手枪，擦掉我留下的指印，才重新放回地板上。然后又把窗搭扣照样擦了一遍。我只碰过这两样东西，所以没留下什么痕迹。

===Original===

“Then I remembered something. Silverdale was always leaving his cigarette holder lying about the lab. He’d put it down on a bench or a desk and wander off, leaving the cigarette smouldering. That happened continually. That very afternoon, he’d left the thing in my room and I’d pocketed it, meaning to give it back to him when I saw him again. There it was, in my vest-pocket.

“In this world, it’s a case of every man for himself. My business was to get out of the hole I was in. If Silverdale got into a hole himself, it was his affair to get out of it. Besides, he’d probably have an alibi, whereas I hadn’t. In any case, the more tangled the business was, the better chance you fellows had of getting off my scent. If the whole story came out, I didn’t see how I was to persuade a jury it had been pure self-defence when I knew myself that it wasn’t that really. Besides, there were these infernal love-letters waiting at Yvonne’s house, all ready for the police and pointing straight to me as a factor in the affair. I’d have had awkward questions to answer about the contents of them.

===Chinese===

“这时我想起一件事。西尔弗代尔总爱把他的烟嘴撂得实验室里到处都是。他把它往实验台或书桌上一搁就晃到别处去了，烟卷还兀自燃着。这种事三天两头有。就在那天下午，他把这玩意儿落在了我屋里，我顺手揣进了兜里，打算回头见面再还他。此刻它就躺在我马甲口袋里。

“在这个世界上，人不为己、天诛地灭。我的事，是从自己陷的坑里爬出来。西尔弗代尔要是自己也掉了坑，爬出来便是他自己的事。何况他多半拿得出不在场证明，我却没有。不管怎么说，这案子越是缠成一团乱麻，你们这班人从我这条线索上脱轨的机会就越大。倘若事情全盘抖搂出来，我自己心里明白那压根不是正当防卫，我想不出拿什么去说服陪审团相信那是纯然自卫。何况伊冯娜家里还压着那些天杀的情书，就等着警察去取，一封封直指我是案中一环。信里写了些什么，我少不得要答一堆尴尬的问题。

===Original===

“The net result was that I cleaned Silverdale’s cigarette-holder with my handkerchief to take off any fingerprints; and I dropped it on the floor to amuse you people. It had that fly in the amber—absolutely unique and easily identifiable.

“Then I switched off the lights, got out of the window again, closed it behind me in case it should attract a passerby. I used my handkerchief to grip the hasp when I closed it, so as not to leave any fingerprints there. In fact, as I walked down to my car, I felt I’d done remarkably well on the spur of the moment.

===Chinese===

“到头来便是这么个结果：我用手帕把西尔弗代尔的烟嘴擦干净，抹去所有指纹；再把它丢在地板上，给你们各位添个消遣。它正是琥珀里的那只苍蝇——举世无双，一认便知。

“然后我关了灯，重新翻出窗户，随手带上了窗，免得招引过路人。关窗时我垫着手帕捏搭扣，免得在那上头留下指纹。说实在的，走回汽车那一路，我觉得自己临场应付得漂亮极了。

===Original===

“As I drove in toward Westerhaven, I conned things over; and it struck me I’d be none the worse of seeing someone as soon as I could. My housekeeper was away nursing a sick relation, so no one could swear whether I’d been at home in the evening or not. If I could drop in on someone, there was always the chance of creating some sort of alibi. The bother was, I knew I wasn’t quite normal. That was only natural. But if I called on someone who saw me every day, they might spot that I was a bit on edge and that might lead to anything, you know. Then it flashed into my mind that Ringwood had come here lately. I hadn’t seen him for years. He wouldn’t see anything funny in my manner, even if I was a bit abnormal.

===Chinese===

“驱车回韦斯特黑文的路上，我把事情翻来覆去掂量了一遍；忽然觉得，尽快去见个人对我只有好处。我的管家外出照护生病的亲戚去了，没人能担保我那天晚上究竟在不在家。要是能顺路拜访什么人，总还有机会制造出某种不在场证明。麻烦在于，我知道自己神色不大对劲。这也难怪。可要是我去拜访天天见面的人，人家兴许就看出我有点心神不定，那就什么事都兜得出来。这时我猛然想起，林伍德新近来到了本地。我多年没见过他了。哪怕我的举止有点反常，他也瞧不出什么古怪。

===Original===

“I drove to his house, and there I had a bit of luck—a perfect gift from the gods. From a telephone message he got while I was in the room with him, I learned that Silverdale was out that night, one of his maids was in bed, and the maid wanted Ringwood to call at once. One’s mind works quickly, as I told you, and I saw in five seconds what a chance I’d got. I offered to pilot Ringwood over to Heatherfield. That meant I’d a perfectly sound excuse if I was seen in the neighbourhood of the house.

“I dropped him at the end of Lauderdale Avenue, as I expect he told you. During the run, I’d had time to think over things. There was only one solution that I could see. I had to get hold of these letters, cost what it might. I calculated that Ringwood’s visit wouldn’t be a long one; and as soon as he’d gone, I meant to drop into Heatherfield, silence the maid, and get the packet of letters.

===Chinese===

“我把车开到他府上，在那儿交上了几分好运——简直是天上掉下来的礼物。我在他屋里的当口，他接了个电话；从那通电话里我得知：西尔弗代尔当晚外出，他的一个女仆病倒在床，那女仆请林伍德立刻过去。我说过，人的脑子转得飞快，不出五秒钟我就看出了这是多大的一个机会。我主动提出给林伍德领路去希瑟菲尔德。这样一来，万一我被人撞见在那宅子附近出没，也有个万分妥帖的借口。

“我把他在劳德代尔大街街口放下——我想他已经告诉过你们了。开车那一程，够我把事情盘算周全。依我看，解答只有一个：不管付出什么代价，也得把那几封信弄到手。我估摸林伍德那一趟长不了；他一走，我就打算摸进希瑟菲尔德，叫那女仆永远闭嘴，取走那包信。

===Original===

“I must have run a bigger risk than I intended; for evidently I got into Heatherfield between Ringwood’s visit and yours. Can you wonder I was a bit pleased with my luck, when it all came out? I made the tourniquet while I was waiting about. Then I went up to Silverdale’s house, rang the bell, and asked for Silverdale. Of course he wasn’t there; but the maid knew me and let me in to write a note for him. Once she’d seen my face and recognised me, it was all up with her. One’s own skin comes first. I might have risked it if it hadn’t been that the drawer was locked and I had to burst it open. That meant leaving traces. And, since she knew me, that meant losing the game. So …”

He made a gesture as if using the tourniquet.

===Chinese===

“如今回想，我准是冒了比本意更大的风险；因为显而易见，我进希瑟菲尔德是在林伍德出诊之后、你们到场之前。如今一切败露，你们还怪我那时为什么有点自鸣得意吗？守候的工夫，我做好了那条止血带。随后我走到西尔弗代尔家，按了门铃，求见西尔弗代尔。他自然不在家；可那女仆认得我，放我进去给他留张便条。她既然看清了我的脸、认出了我，她就只有死路一条了。人总是先顾自己的皮肉。要不是那抽屉上了锁、我不得不把它砸开，我原本或许还肯赌一赌运气。砸抽屉就要留痕迹。而她既然认得我，留痕迹便意味着满盘皆输。于是……”

他做了个手势，仿佛正在使用那条止血带。

===Original===

“I went home after that and destroyed these letters. Then I sat down to do the hardest bit of thinking I’ve done in my life. Time meant a good deal to me just then, for I had to have everything cut and dried before any questions were asked.

“Then the notion of a double game came into my mind. Why not follow up the cigarette-holder move and do my best to throw discredit on Silverdale. It was up to him to clear himself. That gave me the notion of anonymous letters. And obviously if I wanted any attention paid to them, I’d have to make a good start. That suggested giving the police the earliest information about the bungalow affair. If they got that from ‘Justice’ then they’d pay real attention to anything else he liked to send them. So I hit on the telegram idea as being the safest and the quickest. And, as a sequel to that, the obvious thing was to make a show in public of being on Silverdale’s side, so that you wouldn’t suspect me of having any possible connection with the anonymous letters.”

===Chinese===

“那之后我回了家，把那几封信毁了。然后我坐下来，做了这辈子最苦的一场思索。时间在那一刻对我是天大的事，因为我必须在有人发问之前，把一切安排得滴水不漏。

“接着，两面手法的主意浮上了心头。何不顺着烟嘴那一着往下走，竭力把脏水泼向西尔弗代尔。洗刷自己，是他该操心的事。这就让我想到了匿名信。而且很显然，要想让警方把它们当回事，我就得先来个响亮的开场。这就想到把平房案的消息第一时间捅给警方。倘若这消息出自“正义”之手，那么他往后乐意寄去什么，警方都会正眼看待。于是我相中了发电报这个主意——最稳妥，也最快当。作为后手，顺理成章的一步是公开摆出站在西尔弗代尔一边的姿态，好叫你们想不到我跟那些匿名信有任何瓜葛。”

===Original===

“You overdid it just a trifle,” Sir Clinton commented in a dry tone.

Markfield made a noncommittal gesture, but did not argue the point.

“Then,” he continued, “just as I thought I’d fixed everything neatly, this creature Whalley descended on me. He’d taken the number of my car at the gate and faked up a yarn about an accident, so that he could get me identified for him. He called on me and started blackmail. I paid him, of course, to keep him quiet. But naturally I couldn’t let him stand in my way after all I’d gone through safely. He wasn’t a very valuable life at the best, I gather.

===Chinese===

“就是演得稍稍过了点头，”克林顿爵士用干巴巴的口吻评论道。

马克菲尔德做了个不置可否的手势，但没有就这一点争辩。

“后来，”他继续说，“就在我自以为一切都料理得干干净净的时候，沃利这路货色从天而降，找上了我。他在院门口记下了我的车牌号，又编了一套车祸的鬼话，好让人替他把我的身份验明白。他登门拜访，开口便是敲诈。我当然付了钱，堵他的嘴。可我历尽千难万险平安闯到这一步，自然容不得他半路横在道上。再说我听说，这种人的命本来就值不了几个钱。

===Original===

“Anyhow, I got him up here one night—my housekeeper was still away—and throttled him without too much trouble. Then I took the body down into the garage, put it into my car, and drove out the Lizardbridge Road a bit before tipping him into the ditch. I left the tourniquet beside his body. It was a specially-contrived one, meant to throw some more suspicion on Silverdale. I forgot to say that I borrowed Silverdale’s lab. coat to wear during the operation, in case of there being any blood. And I tore off a button and left it in Whalley’s hand. Then I put the torn jacket back on Silverdale’s peg, ready for the police.

“Naturally I was quite pleased to hear that Silverdale had been arrested. That was his lookout, after all. And he seemed to be in trouble over an alibi, which was better news still. The next thing was to clinch the business, if possible.

===Chinese===

“总之，一天夜里，我把他弄到了这儿楼上——管家那时还没回来——没费多大事就掐死了他。然后我把尸体搬下楼放进车库，装上我的车，沿蜥蜴桥路开出城外一段，把他掀进了沟里。我把止血带留在他尸体旁边。那是条特意做的，为的是再给西尔弗代尔添上几分嫌疑。忘了说一句，动手时我借了西尔弗代尔的工作服穿在身上，以防溅出血来。我又撕下一颗纽扣，留在沃利手里。然后我把那件撕破的工作服挂回西尔弗代尔的挂钩上，专候警察来看。

“听到西尔弗代尔被捕，我自然暗自欢喜。说到底，那是他自己该当心的事。而且他似乎还在不在场证明上碰了钉子，这更是好消息。下一步，如果办得到，就要把这案子钉死。

===Original===

“I’ve told you that once upon a time I played some parts in an amateur dramatic show. I was really not bad. And it struck me, after I’d seen you once or twice, Sir Clinton, that I could make myself up into a very fair copy of you. We’re about the same height to start with. I wouldn’t have risked it with anyone who knew both of us; but I’d learned that Avice Deepcar was out of town, and I thought I could manage to take in her maid easily enough.

“So I raided her place, posing as Sir Clinton Driffield—I’d had some notion of the sort in my mind for a while and had cards printed in London all ready: one of these print-’em-while-you-wait places which left no traces behind in the way of an address or an account. In my raid, I got a valuable document.”

===Chinese===

“我告诉过你们，从前我在一出业余话剧里客串过几个角色。说实在的，演得还不赖。见过您一两回之后，克林顿爵士，我忽然想到，我可以把自己化装成您的一个很像样的翻版。首先，咱俩个头相仿。要是对方是个咱们俩都熟的人，我绝不敢冒这个险；但我打听到艾维斯·迪普卡出了城，我想骗过她的女仆还不是易如反掌。

“于是我冒充克林顿·德里菲尔德爵士搜了她的住所——这念头在我心里转悠有些日子了，名片也早早就在伦敦印好备着：就是那种立等可取的印刷铺子，地址账目一概不留，半点痕迹不落。这一趟，我弄到了一份要紧的文件。”

===Original===

“It was a clever enough fake, Dr. Markfield,” Sir Clinton said reflectively. “But you left one or two things in it that we took hold of easily enough. By the way, I suppose you simply traced Mrs. Silverdale’s writing from some old letters when you put the faked address on the code advertisements you sent to the newspapers?”

Markfield nodded.

“You don’t seem to have missed much,” he admitted.

===Chinese===

“伪造得够高明的，马克菲尔德医生，”克林顿爵士若有所思地说。“不过你在里面留了一两处东西，让我们轻而易举就抓住了把柄。顺便问一句，你往报馆寄的那些密码广告上伪造地址的时候，西尔弗代尔太太的笔迹，想必是从几封旧信上描下来的吧？”

马克菲尔德点了点头。

“你们好像什么都没漏掉，”他认了。

===Original===

He rose slowly to his feet and put down his pipe.

“I think that’s the whole story,” he said indifferently. “If you’ve got it all down now, Inspector, I’ll sign it and initial it for you. Then I suppose it’ll be a case of ringing up the Black Maria or something like that.”

He glanced at Sir Clinton.

“You wouldn’t care to tell me how you worried the thing out, I suppose?”

“No,” said the Chief Constable bluntly. “I don’t feel inclined to.”

===Chinese===

他缓缓起身，放下烟斗。

“我想事情就全在这儿了，”他淡漠地说。“探长，你要是都记下了，我就给你签名，再逐页签上姓名缩写。然后嘛，想来就该打个电话，叫囚车来接我这一类的事了。”

他瞥了克林顿爵士一眼。

“我猜，您大概不肯告诉我，您是怎么把这桩事一步步磨明白的吧？”

“不，”郡警察局长直截了当地说。“我没这份兴致。”

===Original===

Markfield made a gesture as though regretting this decision. He drew his fountain pen from his pocket, unscrewed the cap deliberately, and moved round the table towards the sheets of paper which the Inspector had spread out for signature. A thought seemed to occur to him as he did so, and he bent forward to the apparatus on the tray. His manner was so unconcerned and the gesture so natural that neither Sir Clinton nor the Inspector thought of interfering before it was too late. Markfield put his hand on the tap of the funnel, and as he did so, his face lighted up with malicious glee.

===Chinese===

马克菲尔德做了个手势，似乎对这一决定引以为憾。他从口袋里掏出自来水笔，不慌不忙拧开笔帽，绕过桌子，朝探长摊开待签的那几页纸走去。走着走着，他似乎想起了什么，俯身凑向托盘上的那套装置。他的神态那么若无其事，动作那么自然，克林顿爵士也好，探长也好，谁都没来得及想到拦阻，一切已成定局。马克菲尔德把手搭上漏斗的旋塞，就在那一瞬，他的脸上亮起一抹恶毒的狂喜。

===Original===

“Now!” he exclaimed.

He turned the tap, and on the instant the whole house shook under a terrific detonation.

===Chinese===

“就是现在！”他喊道。

他拧开旋塞，刹那间，一声惊天动地的爆炸震得整座房子都摇晃起来。

## XIX Excerpts from Sir Clinton’s Notebook / 十九 克林顿爵士笔记本摘录

===Original===

Written after the murder at Heatherfield

===Chinese===

记于希瑟菲尔德谋杀案之后

===Original===

… The following things seem suggestive. (1) The breakup of the Silverdale ménage, with Silverdale turning to Avice Deepcar whilst Mrs. Silverdale lets Hassendean frequent her openly. (2) Hassendean’s interference with the usual routine of coffee serving after dinner at Heatherfield. (3) The “dazed” appearance of Mrs. Silverdale when she left the house after coffee. (4) The fact that the two shots which wounded Hassendean at close quarters were not fired in Ivy Lodge. (This exonerates Dr. Ringwood, who might otherwise have come under suspicion). (5) The disappearance of Mrs. Silverdale, who was last seen in Hassendean’s company. (6) The words: “Caught me … Thought it was all right. … Never guessed,” which Hassendean uttered before he died. (7) The murder of the maid at Heatherfield, which was clearly done by someone she knew well or she would not have admitted him at that time of night. (8) The ransacking of one particular drawer in Mrs. Silverdale’s bedroom, suggesting that the murderer had full knowledge of her private affairs. (9) The envelope fragment with the date-stamp 1925, which might indicate that the drawer had held letters compromising to the murderer. (10) The old dance programmes on which asterisks stood for the name of some partner, who must have been intimate with her at that period.

===Chinese===

……以下各点似具提示意义。(1) 西尔弗代尔家庭解体，西尔弗代尔转而亲近艾维斯·迪普卡，西尔弗代尔太太则听任哈森丁公开出入左右。(2) 哈森丁对希瑟菲尔德晚宴后照例上咖啡的流程横加干预。(3) 西尔弗代尔太太喝完咖啡离宅时那副“迷迷糊糊”的模样。(4) 近距离击伤哈森丁的那两枪并非在常春藤小屋中所发。（这一条还了林伍德医生清白，否则他本可能招来嫌疑。）(5) 西尔弗代尔太太失踪，最后一次有人见到她时，她正与哈森丁同行。(6) 哈森丁临死前吐出的那几个字：“抓住我了……还以为没事……万没想到”。(7) 希瑟菲尔德女仆被害，凶手显然是她熟识的人，否则深夜那个钟点她绝不会放人进门。(8) 西尔弗代尔太太卧房中那一只抽屉被翻检一空，可见凶手对她的私事了如指掌。(9) 那片带1925年邮戳的信封残片，或可表明抽屉里原存放着足以连累凶手的信件。(10) 那些旧舞序卡上以星号代替某位舞伴的名字，此人在那段时期必定与她过从甚密。

===Original===

The affair can hardly have been the usual social-triangle tragedy: Silverdale surprising his wife with Hassendean. This hypothesis fails to account for (a) the dazed appearance of Mrs. Silverdale, which suggests drugging; (b) the murder and burglary at Heatherfield, Silverdale’s own house in which he could come and go freely without resorting to such extremes; and (c) The expression “Caught *me* …” in Hassendean’s last words, since “Caught *us* …” would have been the natural phrase in the case of the triangle-drama.

===Chinese===

这桩事很难是那种寻常的三角情杀悲剧：西尔弗代尔当场撞破妻子与哈森丁的私情。这一假设解释不了：(a) 西尔弗代尔太太那副恍惚迷离的模样，这提示她被下了药；(b) 希瑟菲尔德的凶杀与窃案——那是西尔弗代尔自家的宅子，他进出随意，何须使出这般极端的手段；以及 (c) 哈森丁遗言里“抓住的是*我*……”这一说法——若真是三角戏码，顺口的话本该是“抓住的是*我们*……”。

===Original===

Curious that Dr. Markfield should pilot Ringwood right across the town and then drop him at the end of the avenue instead of going a hundred yards or so further, to the very gate of the house. Worth keeping in mind that Dr. Markfield knew Mrs. Silverdale well at one time, though he cooled off later (Ringwood’s evidence). Compare the old dance-programmes?

===Chinese===

蹊跷的是，马克菲尔德医生竟领着林伍德横穿全城，却只把他送到那条大街的尽头便撇下，而不肯再往前走百来码，径直送到那宅子门口。还有一点值得记取：马克菲尔德医生一度与西尔弗代尔太太交好，尽管后来冷淡了下去（林伍德的证词）。可与那些旧舞序卡对一对？

===Original===

Written after the discovery of the bungalow tragedy

===Chinese===

记于平房惨案被发现之后

===Original===

This is clearly the second half of the Hassendean business. Obviously Hassendean prepared the bungalow beforehand for the reception of Mrs. Silverdale. Either she consented to go there willingly; or else, as seems more likely, he drugged her after dinner and took her there without her consent. In any case, it was premeditated on his part. Evidently he overshot the dose of the drug and killed her. His subsequent shooting the body suggests that he meant to leave an obvious cause of death, which might divert attention from the poison altogether and cause it to be overlooked in a P.M. examination. In that case, it’s likely that he meant to take the body elsewhere in his car and leave it—meaning to suggest that she committed suicide. Of course the shooting may have been done accidentally or by a third party who did not know she was already dead. But this seems unlikely on the face of things.

===Chinese===

这显然是哈森丁那桩事的下半场。显而易见，哈森丁事先把平房收拾停当，专为接纳西尔弗代尔太太。要么是她自愿同意前去；要么——这似乎更可能——是他在晚饭后给她下了药，未经她同意便把她弄到了那里。无论如何，这在他是有预谋的。显然他用药过了量，把她给害死了。他随后对尸体开枪，表明他想留下一个显豁的死因，好把注意力从毒药上整个引开，令其在尸检（P.M.，post-mortem）中被漏检。若果真如此，他多半还打算用汽车把尸体运往别处抛下——摆出她是自杀的模样。当然，那两枪也可能是失手所致，或是某个不知她已死的第三者所开。但从表面情形看，这不太可能。

===Original===

Four people at least were at the bungalow that night: Mrs. Silverdale, Hassendean, and the two watchers at the windows. One of the watchers must be this fellow “Justice,” who had the first news of the affair. One of them was probably the murderer of Hassendean, since he entered the room. The second watcher may have seen the murder committed, though this is not certain.

===Chinese===

当夜至少有四个人到过平房：西尔弗代尔太太、哈森丁，还有窗外的两个窥望者。其中一个窥望者必是“正义”这家伙无疑，他最先得到了这桩事的消息。其中一人多半就是枪杀哈森丁的凶手，因为他进过那间屋子。第二个窥望者可能目击了凶案经过，不过这一点尚难断言。

===Original===

Apart from the general state of the bungalow, the only clues of interest are the cigarette-holder and the signet-ring on Mrs. Silverdale’s finger.

===Chinese===

撇开平房的总体状况不谈，值得留意的线索只有烟嘴和西尔弗代尔太太指上那枚图章戒指。

===Original===

Silverdale denies that he gave her the ring; and as the date 1925 in it belongs to the period of dissociation in the Silverdale ménage, it seems probable that he is speaking the truth. The initial *B* engraved in the ring evidently indicates the donor, and it may stand for either a real initial or the initial of a pet name. Possibly the donor was the person indicated by an asterisk on the dance-programmes and (or) the person who burgled Heatherfield to get hold of letters which perhaps compromised him.

===Chinese===

西尔弗代尔否认戒指出自他所赠；而戒指上刻的1925年正值西尔弗代尔夫妇离心的时期，如此看来，他说的多半是实话。戒指上镌的字母*B*显然指明赠与之人，它既可能是真实的首字母，也可能是某个昵称的首字母。赠戒之人或许就是舞序卡上以星号代指的那一位，并且（或者）就是为攫取那些或许会连累自己的信件而潜入希瑟菲尔德行窃的人。

===Original===

The cigarette-holder found at the bungalow is undoubtedly Silverdale’s, but that does not necessarily prove that Silverdale was ever there. Someone else, who had a chance of laying hands on his cigarette-holder, may have left it to mislead us. All that it tells is that someone associated with Silverdale was at the bungalow. Both Hassendean and Mrs. Silverdale fit this description.

===Chinese===

平房里发现的那只烟嘴无疑是西尔弗代尔的，但这未必能证明西尔弗代尔到过那里。旁的什么人若有机会弄到他的烟嘴，也可能把它留在那里误导我们。它说明的只是：与西尔弗代尔有牵连的某人到过平房。哈森丁和西尔弗代尔太太都合乎这一描述。

===Original===

As to Silverdale, it’s evident that he wanted to get rid of his wife and marry Miss Deepcar. But that does not prove he was prepared to go the length of murder to gain his ends. He has no alibi for the period of the bungalow affair; but few of us could produce an alibi for a given time on the spur of the moment.

===Chinese===

至于西尔弗代尔，他显然想摆脱妻子，娶迪普卡小姐。但这并不能证明，他为达到目的便不惜走到谋杀那一步。平房案的时段他拿不出不在场证明；可要是让人一时半刻说出某个特定时刻自己在哪儿，我们中间也没几个人办得到。

===Original===

Miss Hailsham had a grudge against Hassendean, but there is no evidence connecting her with the bungalow affair.

===Chinese===

海尔沙姆小姐对哈森丁怀有宿怨，但没有任何证据把她与平房案牵到一起。

===Original===

The maid at Heatherfield seems a mere pawn in the game. Silverdale might have used her to drug the coffee; but Hassendean’s unusual interference with the normal serving of the coffee (coupled with his preparations beforehand at the bungalow) point to him as the administrator of the drug.

===Chinese===

希瑟菲尔德的那个女仆，看来不过是这盘棋局里一枚小小的卒子。西尔弗代尔或许曾借她之手往咖啡里下药；但哈森丁对照例上咖啡的反常干预（连同他事先在平房的种种布置），都指向他才是下药之人。

===Original===

As to the drug, Hassendean must have had easy access to it. It’s a mydriatic drug, since the eye-pupils were expanded. Miss Deepcar mentioned hyoscine when she came into the room at the Croft-Thornton Institute, so that evidently they have it on the premises there. Hyoscine narcosis has one special peculiarity: it obliterates from the patient’s memory all recollection of what may have happened while the drug was acting. At least that’s what they say about the “Twilight Sleep” treatment. This would be the very drug Hassendean would require for his purpose. Mrs. Silverdale would wake up from the narcosis with only the very faintest recollection of what had happened.

===Chinese===

至于那味药，哈森丁必定轻易就能到手。那是一种扩瞳剂，因为死者的瞳孔是散大的。迪普卡小姐在克罗夫特-索顿研究所走进那间屋子时提到了东莨菪碱，可见所里显然备有此药。东莨菪碱麻醉有一桩特别的性质：药力作用期间无论发生什么，都会从用药者的记忆中抹尽。至少“朦胧睡眠”（Twilight Sleep）疗法是这么说的。这恰恰是哈森丁达成目的所需的那味药。西尔弗代尔太太从麻醉中醒来时，对发生过的事只会剩下极淡极淡的一点印象。

===Original===

A preliminary hypothesis seems possible. Hassendean resolved to drug Mrs. Silverdale with hyoscine and take her to the bungalow while under the influence of the narcotic. He prepared the place beforehand and got her there successfully. But he overshot the dose he gave her, and she died in his hands at the bungalow. He then shot her in the head, meaning to take her away in his car and leave the body somewhere, arranged as though it were a case of suicide. He might hope that in these circumstances the drug might not be spotted and thus he would be completely clear. But someone else saw the shooting and, being keenly interested in Mrs. Silverdale, shot Hassendean in revenge. On the face of things, this third party must be either “Justice” or the second watcher. Then, if this third party had been intimate with Mrs. Silverdale, there might be letters in her possession which would bring out their relations; and these letters it might be essential to secure. Hence the murder of the maid and the burglary at Heatherfield. Very sketchy, of course, but it seems suggestive.

===Chinese===

一个初步的假设似乎可以成立。哈森丁决意用东莨菪碱麻醉西尔弗代尔太太，趁药力发作把她带到平房。他事先布置好了那地方，也顺利把她弄到了那里。但他给她用的剂量过了头，她死在了他手里，就死在平房。于是他朝她的头部开枪，打算用汽车把她运走，把尸体抛在某处，摆成自杀的样子。他或许指望，事到这般田地，毒药不会被查出，自己便可彻底脱干系。但另一个人目睹了这场枪击，此人又对西尔弗代尔太太关切入骨，便开枪打死了哈森丁报仇。从表面看，这第三方不是“正义”就是第二个窥望者。再者，倘若这第三方与西尔弗代尔太太有过私情，她手里就可能存着能抖出两人关系的信件；而这些信件或许非到手不可。于是便有了女仆被害和希瑟菲尔德的窃案。当然还很粗疏，但似有启发。

===Original===

If it be the truth or near it, then the murderer must have known when to strike at Heatherfield, for usually there were two maids on the premises, which would be too big a job for a single assailant. But, from Ringwood’s evidence, *Markfield learned the state of affairs at Heatherfield that night from the phone call which came through when he was at Ringwood’s house*. And at once he offered to pilot Ringwood through the fog—which gave him a perfectly sound excuse for being in the neighbourhood of Heatherfield if anyone happened to recognise him. Further, he deliberately avoided taking Ringwood up to the Heatherfield gate, but dropped him at the end of Lauderdale Avenue. This would avoid any chance of his being directly connected with Heatherfield that night; and after he left Ringwood, he could easily drive round to the back of Heatherfield and watch his chance to enter the house.

===Chinese===

倘若这是真相或接近真相，那么凶手必定知道何时对希瑟菲尔德下手为宜，因为宅里平日有两个女仆，单人行凶未免摊子太大。可是据林伍德的证词，*马克菲尔德那晚身在林伍德家中，从接进来的那通电话里得知了希瑟菲尔德的情形*。他随即主动提出领林伍德穿雾而行——这就给了他一个无懈可击的借口，万一有人碰巧认出他，也说得清自己为何在希瑟菲尔德左近。不仅如此，他故意不把林伍德送到希瑟菲尔德门口，只把他撂在劳德代尔大街的尽头。这样便可避免他与当晚的希瑟菲尔德沾上任何直接瓜葛；而撇下林伍德之后，他大可绕到希瑟菲尔德宅后，伺机进屋。

===Original===

Written after reading Hassendean’s Journal

===Chinese===

记于读罢哈森丁日记之后

===Original===

Three things emerge from Hassendean’s M.S. (1) He seems to have excited Miss Hailsham to the extent of a loss of control when he jilted her; but that does not in itself prove anything. (2) Mrs. Silverdale obviously led him on and continually disappointed him. This fits in with the hypothesis I made. (3) His remark: “Only I shall know of my triumph,” agrees very neatly with the memory-blotting property of hyoscine. As a whole, then, the hypothesis, seems justified.

===Chinese===

哈森丁的手稿浮现出三点。(1) 他甩掉海尔沙姆小姐时，似乎把她激惹到了失态的地步；但这本身证明不了什么。(2) 西尔弗代尔太太显然一直在撩拨他，又不断叫他失望。这与我先前所作的假设正相吻合。(3) 他那句“唯有我将知道我的凯旋”，与东莨菪碱抹除记忆的特性扣合得天衣无缝。总而言之，这个假设看来是站得住的。

===Original===

As to Markfield, I notice he makes a parade of intense reluctance if he is asked to give evidence involving Silverdale; but when he is actually induced to talk, he says things which tell heavily against his colleague. As he’s by no means a fool, this seems worth attention.

===Chinese===

至于马克菲尔德，我注意到，凡要他提供牵涉西尔弗代尔的证词，他总要装出万分为难之态；可一旦真被引得开了口，他说出的话却对他的同事大为不利。此人绝非蠢材，这一点似乎值得留意。

===Original===
